# Mudhal Tiruvandhadi

## 1. Vaiyam tagaliya

*By using te terms vaiyam, vārkadal and kadirōn, āḻvār is referring to te elements eart, vater and fire trough analojñ, tus implying all five elements. ṭhrough tese five, he tinks of te entire constellation, narroving to eart, its hardness, te tenderness of vater, te heat of fire, and tinks of te entire leelā vibhūti (material realm). By using te term sudarāḻiyān (divine cakra – disc of emperumān) he refers to nityavibhūti  (spiritual realm) and trough te term adikkē he refers to te divine feet of emperumān, tus implying tat emperumān has a divine form and controls bot te vibhūtis (realms). He also implies te śēśitvam (lordśip) of emperumān as vell as his ovn śēśatvam (servitude), carrying out service to emperumān trough his divine feet. śince emperumān is happy vit his service, āḻvār too feels happy for having gladdened emperumān. He says tat in order to remove te lovliness of not having carried out suc kainkaryam (service to emperumān) so far, he is adorning emperumān’s divine feet \[vit his verses\]. ānoter interpretation is tat since he had not got te knovledge so far to do tis, he vants emperumān to remove te hurdle of samsāram vhic is te cause of his continued ignorance.*

**Пословный перевод:**

vaiyam tagaḷiyā – earth as holding vessel for oil; vār kadalē neyyāga – sea, surrounding the earth, as ghee (clarified butter); veyya kadirōn viḷakkāga – sun, with his hot rays, as lamp \[wick\]; seyya sudarāḻiyān adikkē – at the divine feet of emperumān, who has reddish coloured chakra (disc) weapon; idar āḻi nīngugavē enṟu – let the ocean of sorrow (in this world) disappear; sol mālai – garland made of words; sūttinēn – I adorned him with

**Перевод:**

**vaiyam tagaḷiyā – śrīviṣṇu purāṇam 1-14-28 says “kātinyavān yō pibharti jagatētadaśēśata: I śabdādi samśrayō vyāpī tasmai bhūmyātmanē nama: īī (salutations to the all pervading supreme entity who is in the form of earth with the five qualities of śabda etc (śabda, rūpa, rasa, ganda and sparśam, the five senses relating to sound, vision, taste, smell and touch respectively). Earth is a hard material and holds the world in it. Thus earth is considered as capable of holding \[like a lamp\] the substances which are mentioned as simile to ghee (clarified butter) and wick which are mentioned later in the pāsuram (hymn). Since the world has a form, like a lamp, it appears that it should have been created by an entity; it is being compared to the container (of lamp) to affirm the existence of supreme entity.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

**vār kadalē neyyāga** – ocean surrounds the earth fully and is in the form of a liquid. It obeys the order of supreme entity not to cross its border (shore) and to remain within its boundary. Due to this it is compared to ghee which will enable a lamp (which is to be lit) to keep glowing for a long time. vār kadal – surrounding ocean (surrounding earth). It can also be construed as a completely filled up ocean. It does not overflow because of excessive flow into it by rivers. It does not shrink if water from rivers does not flow into it.  It does not destroy the earth even though it is at a higher level than earth. There must be an entity to take care of all these. Just as ghee enables the lamp to glow for a long time, the oceanic waters are pulled by sun’s rays and make the rays to glow. For these reasons, ocean is compared to a lamp that reveals [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/). There is no significance for the long syllable in the word *kadalē.* This can be applied to the other words vaiyam, kadiron  also for uniformity (*vaiyamē tagaḷiyā, kadirōnē viḷakkāga*)

**veyya kadirōn viḷakkāga** – sun is compared to the lamp (wick) since it has hot rays emanating from it and it is a radiant material. As taittiriya upaniśat says “*bhīśōdēti sūrya:*” (sun rises because of his fear of emperumān). He is seen for 30 *nāḻigai* (one nāḻigai is 24 minutes) and is not seen for the other 30 *nāḻigai*. He reveals emperumān like a wick in a lamp.

*vaiyam tagaḷiyā* – Just as a log of wood floats in water, the earth is also floating. Should there not be a reason for it? *vār kadalē neyyāga* – instead of inundating the earth, the ocean is limiting itself within its boundary. Should there not be a reason for it? *veyya kadirōn viḷakkāga* – without reducing or increasing from its 30 nāḻigai, sun rises and sets every day. Should there not be a reason for it? And, should not such an entity, which is the reason for all these, be omniscient and omnipotent?

Once it is known that everything is his and he is the master of all, the hymn says what should be done further.

**seyya sudarāḻiyān adikkē** – [nammāḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/nammazhwar/), in periya tiruvandādi 26 says “*iruḷanna māmēni em iṟaiyār*” (my Lord’s divine form has the colour of darkness). For such a dark form, the divine chakra (disc) adds a contrasting red colour. These words negate the opinions of other philosophies which say that *brahmam* (supreme entity) does not exist, or that it has weapons other than chakra or that it does not have any auspicious qualities etc. These words confirm that [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) has all the wealth, has divine weapons such as chakra, has a divine, beautiful form etc.  The reddish colour of chakra contrasts well with the black colour of his divine form. Since [āḻvārs](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/azhwars/) were bestowed with unblemiśed knowledge, the first realisation that they get is that they are servitors and they should serve the divine feet of [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/). [pirātti](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sri-mahalakshmi/) (emperumān’s consort, mahālakśmi) holds emperumān’s hands. [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/poigai-azhwar/) is not pirātti. Hence, he holds on to emperumān’s divine feet. Under the divine feet of such an emperumān…..

**idarāḻi nīnguga enṟu** – my ocean of sorrow, since it has not performed any kainkaryam, should leave me.

**sol mālai sūttinēn** – I adorned his divine feet with a garland of words, which will never lose its fragrance and will never fade, having been strung on his auspicious qualities and deeds. This is just like the decoration that anasūya (wife of riśi atri) made on sītā pirātti \[garland not fading\]. Just as the tiruttuḻāy (tuḷasi) garland establishes the supremacy of [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/), this garland of words of the āḻvār also establishes his supremacy. āḻvār is able to know emperumān fully and is speaking of his qualities when even vēdam had to return after failing in its attempt to measure the greatness of emperumān (taittiriya upaniśat 9.1 “*yatō vācō nivarttantē apprāpya manasā saha”*)

**idarāḻi nīngugavē enṟu sūttinēn** – āḻvār indicates that he wants to rid of his thirst of not having strung a garland of verses for [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) till now.  He also implies that he does not exist on days when he does not praise emperumān. For [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/poigai-azhwar/), every object such as earth, ocean etc reminds of emperumān. Once a person asked [māṟanēri nambi](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/maraneri-nambi/) “how do I remember emperumān?” māṟanēri nambi asked him in return “tell me, how do I forget emperumān?”

I strung a garland of hymns for the samsāris, who do not know anything regarding emperumān, and who are losing their *svarūpam* (basic nature), who, by learning this text which explains the truths about [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/), would have their ocean of sins, a container for all their sorrows, dry up.

There is not a single meaning which has not been conveyed by this pāsuram \[i.e., all the important meanings are present in this pāsuram itself\].

We shall next take up the 2<sup>nd</sup> pāsuram.

adiyēn kriṣṇa ramanuja dāsan

## 2. Enru kadal kadaindadu

*enrū kadal kadaindadu evvulagum nīrēṟṟadu onṟum adanai uṇarēn nān – anṟu adu adaittu udaittuk kaṇ padutta āḻi idu nī padaittu idandu uṇḍu umiḻnda pār*

**Пословный перевод:**

kadal kadaindadu enṟu – when was the ocean (of milk) churned?; nīrēṟṟadu evvulagam – for which world did you accept water; adanai onṟum uṇarēn nān – I do not know anything at all about that; adu anṟu adaittu udaittu – dam was built on that ocean (by śrī rāma) and was broken; kaṇ padutta āḻi – ocean on which \[you are\] reclining; idu nī padaittu – this world was recovered (after accepting water); idandu, uṇḍu – \[as varāha avatāram\] dug up and eaten \[during deluge\]; umiḻnda pār – \[after deluge\] the world that was spat out

**Перевод:**

**enṟu kadal kadaindadu – when was the ocean churned? The implied answer is that it was churned today. Have the waves and the agitation of the ocean stopped after he churned it? Do they not remain as they were? emperumān has blessed āḻvār such that he could see all that happened, is happening and will happen, all at the same time right before him! Thus, the great task that he had undertaken \[of churning the milky ocean\] appears to āḻvār as if it has been carried out today.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*āḻvār says “what have I done? Do I need to prove through conjecture that emperumān is there because there are effects and hence there should be a cause? Through his atimānuśa deeds (deeds that are beyond the capabilities of human beings), we can easily know his presence” Let us look at the pāsuram and its meanings, first.*

**evvulagam nīrēṟṟadu** – which world did he measure after accepting water? (Here too, the implication is that it is this world). Is there a world other than ours that he measured after accepting water? Is it not to protect this world that he went seeking alms, concealing his identity totally \[as vāmana\]? [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/poigai-azhwar/) says that his foot print that he set out to measure the world has not disappeared. Is this world suffering because of mahābali? (No) As said in [tiruvāimoḻi 4-4-1](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2017/02/thiruvaimozhi-4-4-1-mannai-irundhu/) “*vāmanan maṇ*” (the world measured by vāmana) it appears that this world is only his.

**onṟum adanai uṇarēn nān** – āḻvār says that from the day that [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) measured this world, we should have realised that and should have been talking this only. Instead of that, we are constantly engaged in and wasting our time in eating, dressing up and similar activities. Another interpretation is that instead of knowing such wondrous deeds of emperumān, we are trying to establish emperumān through conjecture. Another interpretation is that just as [nammāḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/nammazhwar/) swooned and did not regain his consciousness for 6 months after reciting the verse “*ettiṟam*” in [tiruvāimoḻi 1-3-1](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2015/06/thiruvaimozhi-1-3-1-paththudai-adiyavar/), [poygai āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/poigai-azhwar/) too goes into a trance after seeing his wondrous deeds. Reference to churning of ocean and accepting water \[to recover the world\] are pointers to all his deeds.

**anṟu** – did [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) not churn the ocean today? To [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/poigai-azhwar/), it appears that he has been churning the ocean from that day and is still at it. Though time keeps moving, to āḻvār it appears that what was done yugams (ages) back is being done right now, due to his deep knowledge. Since tiruppāṟkadal is a little distance away from where he is, āḻvār is calling it as *adu* (that).

**adaittu udaittuk kaṇ padutta āḻi** – he built a bridge across the ocean with stone. He broke it with his bow (after killing rāvaṇan). He reclined in that ocean. u*daittu* also means destroy.

**idu nī padaittu idandu uṇḍu umiḻnda pār** – since [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/poigai-azhwar/) is in samsāram, he calls the world which is near him as *idu* (this). [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) created this world when it was not there; when it got drowned during *praḷayam* (deluge) he dug it out; before the onset of praḷayam, he kept it in inside his stomach to protect it; after praḷayam ends, he spat it out this earth so that it could see all the galaxies. This earth has suffered a similar destiny that cotton suffers from the time it comes out as a flower till it gets converted into a cloth (*parutti patta panniraṇḍu*m is an old saying in tamiḻ which means the twelve processes that cotton undergoes before it becomes useful. Earth suffers like this at his hands). While other dēvatās call themselves as “*īsvaran*” (one who controls), if they are really *īsvaran*, would they not have taken the steps that he had taken to retrieve this earth? This alone is enough to understand that they are not *īsvaran*. Also, if this earth were indeed not his \[emperumān’s\] would he have carried out all these actions? As [nammāḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/nammazhwar/) says in [tiruvāimoḻi  4-5-10](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2017/02/thiruvaimozhi-4-5-10-undum-umizhndhum/) “*kaṇḍavāṟṟāl tanadē ulagena ninṟān*” (he establishes that the world is his due to all his deeds).

We shall see the 3<sup>rd</sup> pāsuram in this tiruvandādi, next.

## 3. Par alavum oradi

*pāraḷavum ōradi vaittu ōradiyum pārudutta nīraḷavum sella nimirndadē – sūruruvin pēyaḷavu kaṇḍa perumān aṟigilēn nī aḷavu kaṇḍa neṟi*

**Пословный перевод:**

pāraḷavum ōradi vaittu – keeping one divine foot till the end of this world \[to measure it\]; ōr adiyum pār udutta – the other divine foot surrounding the entire universe; nīr aḷavum sella nimirndadu – it rose to reach the waters covering the universe; sūr uruvin pēy – a demon who came in the garb of a celestial person; aḷavu kaṇḍa perumān – Oh benefactor, who determined the boundary (of life); nī aḷavu kaṇḍa neṟi aṟigilēn – I am not able to know the extent of the deeds carried out by you

**Перевод:**

**pāraḷavum ōradi vaittu – keeping a divine foot to reach the end of the world**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*āḻvār says “do we need all these deeds to prove that emperumān is superior to everyone else? Through this one deed (of measuring the worlds) which is beyond the capabilities of human race, is it not enough to remind everyone of his supreme nature?”*

**ōradiyum pārudutta nīr aḷavum sella nimirndadu** – if a person asked another, “tell me an equivalent of [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/)’s divine foot”, the other person will say “emperumān’s other foot”. His other divine foot rose all the way to the waters surrounding the universe as a cover (here *pār* is taken to mean universe). This is similar to [tirumangai āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thirumangai-azhwar/)’s periya tirumoḻi 10-6-3 “*pavva nīr udai ādaiyāgac cuṟṟi*” (water circling the earth like its dress). If *pār* were taken to be earth, the other divine foot rose till the waters surrounding earth.

**sūr uruvil … aṟigilēn** – [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/poigai-azhwar/) says “since one divine foot spread all over the world and there was no space to keep the other divine foot, I am not able to understand how you measured this world”.  [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) asks him back “why could you not understand?”

**sūr uruvil pēyaḷavu kaṇḍa perumān** – isn’t this (the trouble that pūtanā had to undergo) what people who set out to measure you, have to suffer? [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/poigai-azhwar/) asks [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) “are you in a state where someone could measure you?”

**sūr uruvil** – as per the tamiḻ lexicon “*sūrum aṇangum deyvappeṇṇē”* – sūr refers to a divine person. Here, this term actually refers to the demon pūtanā, but since she took the form of yaśōdā, she is referred to as divine person. In perumāḷ tirumoiḻi 7-5, [kulaśēkara āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/kulasekarazhwar/) refers to yaśōdā as “*deyva nangai yasōdai*” (the divine lady yaśōdā). One can also consider sūr as a demon with a beautiful form. This is because demons are considered as belonging to the class of dēvas as per the saying “*piśāsō dēva yōnaya:*” in amara kōsam 1-1-11.

**pēyaḷavu kaṇḍa** – considering \[measuring / estimating\] her as a demon. Measuring is also killing. She \[pūtanā\] came to kill [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/), who, knowing her intention, killed her instead. Oh emerpumān! When you measure someone, you don’t have any sorrow \[difficulty\]; but when someone comes to measure you, (s)he get killed, like pūtanā.

**perumān** – by killing her, you removed your enemy and gave yourself to the world as its Lord. [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/poigai-azhwar/) points, out through this word, to harivamśam 65 which says “*stanyam tat viśasammiśram rasyamāsīt jagadgurō*” (the poisoned milk of pūtanā’s bosom was like a sweet juice to kaṇṇan, the Lord of the world).

**aṟigilēn nī aḷavu kaṇḍa neṟi** – I do not know what you are thinking of. I do not know whether you are enabling me or not, to know you. Another interpretation – [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/poigai-azhwar/) cannot estimate each and every deed of [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/)’s. Just as you took the earth, which was snatched by mahābali, under your feet, you had taken me under your feet and removed by enemies just as you removed pūtanā. We can take it that [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/poigai-azhwar/) does not know this.

We shall  next take up the 4th pāsuram.

## 4. Neri vasal

*neṟivāsal tanē āy ninṟānai aindu poṟivāsal pōrkkadavam sātti – aṟivānām āla mara nīḻal aṟam nālvarkku anṟu uraitta ālam amar kaṇḍattu aran*

**Пословный перевод:**

anṟu aḷamara nīḻal – on that day \[once upon a time\], under the shade of the banyan tree; nālvarkku aṟam uraitta – one who gave a discourse to four riśis on dharma (righteousness); ālam amar kaṇḍattu aran – Siva, who has poison in his throat; aindu poṟi vāsal – among the five sensory organs; pōrkkadavam sātti – closing the door, which is difficult to close; neṟi vāsan tānēyāy ninṟānai – emperumān who is both the path and the goal; aṟivān Am – would he know?

**Перевод:**

**nerivāsal tānēyāy ninṟānai – neri refers to upāyam (the path to attain) and vāsal refers to prāpyam (that which is attained or in other words, the goal). Thus, through these words it is clearly stated that emperumān himself is both the path (to attain him) and the goal. The purpose of stating this is to understand that knowledge about something else will come only if the object to be attained is something (or somebody) else and not emperumān \[instead of knowing this, Siva tried to give discourse to others\]. Moreover, even if a person’s attempts at realising knowledge were to succeed, that would require emperumān’s mercy. Thus āḻvār says emphatically that it is only emperumān who is the path to attain him. This is the path suited to jīvātmā’s svarūpam (basic nature)**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*āḻvār says that despite emperumān bestowing unblemiśed knowledge on him, he was finding it difficult to know him, while Siva, by carrying out some penance holding on to his breath, is trying through his own efforts to know emperumān. How ignorant is this action! Why is āḻvār struggling to know emperumān fully? What is the hurdle? Even though hurdles which contract knowledge have been removed and there is no shortage of implements for gaining knowledge, it is impossible to measure those entities which cannot be measured. Thus, it is not possible to measure (know) emperumān with the knowledge granted by him. vēdas, which set out to measure him, returned, saying that he is “mahāntam” (he is great)\[taittirīya upaniśat\].  One can keep seeing the same features again and again, but not the full picture. Even if a person stands near the sea (at the shore), isn’t it true that he cannot see the whole ocean! Let us look at the pāsuram.*

**āy ninṟa** – unlike other upāyams (paths) [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) is readily available \[while the others are to be attempted by jīvātmā\].

**aindu poṟivāsal pōrkkadavam sātti** – is it possible to know such an emperumān by controlling one’s senses? \[not possible, is the opinion here\]

**aindu poṟi vāsal** – the five sensory organs viz. ears, mouth, eyes, nose and skin. Any one among these five is enough to prevent a person from carrying out attempts on his own as means \[to attain [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/)\]. Since these are capable of pulling a person towards them through some deceit or the other, [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/poigai-azhwar/) refers to these senses as “*poṟi*” (trap). The word “*pōrkkadavam*” refers to the fact that these sensory organs are waging a battle against the jīvātmā (*pōr* means war and *kadavam* means door). While the jīvatmā tries to control his senses, these senses slip out of his hands making it difficult for him to control them and they wander all over the place.

**sātti** – even if one were to close the door \[to the senses\] shut, it is almost impossible. If one tries to control one sense, the others will break free and pull him. When one attempts to plug water, which is flowing rapidly, it will create a hole elsewhere and run forcefully again. Senses are similar to this phenomenon. Hasn’t arjuna said in [śrī bhagavat gītā 6-34](http://githa.koyil.org/index.php/6-34/) “*cancalam hi mana:*” (mind is restless)! If one does not feed the senses, they will tear up the person.  As [śrī bhagavat gītā says in 2-59](http://githa.koyil.org/index.php/2-59/) “*param druśtvā nivartatē*” (only after seeing the superior entity ([emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/)) will desires exit). Thus, is there any way to control the senses other than seeing emperumān? The opinion here is that while it is possible to control the senses to an extent by carrying out certain deeds, it is virtually impossible to control them such that they do not go after *viśayam* (pleasures).

**arivānām** – [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/poigai-azhwar/) is saying sarcastically “Oh, doesn’t Siva, who has controlled his senses, not know [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/)!” (when Siva started doing his penance, he fell for pārvati and spent time with her; the opinion expressed here is: could that Siva control his senses?)

**ālamara nīḻal** – under the shade of the banyan tree where he \[siva\] was performing his penance.

**aram nālvarkku anṟu uraitta** – aṟam here refers to the path to attain [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/). Siva preached four riśis *agastyar, dakśar, pulastyar* and *mārkaṇḍeyar* about knowledge on emperumān.

**anṟu uraitta** – Siva preached others even before he had heard fully about emperumān. Just as a person, by name bālāgi, who did not know about *brahmam* (supreme entity) preached the most learned ajāta śatru about *brahmam*, Siva also preached. This is similar to a person trying to enjoy the goal even before carrying out the deeds to reach it. Siva had consumed poison due to his tāmasa guṇa (the quality of being ignorant). śrī bhagavat gītā 14-27 says “*satvāt sanjāyatē jñānam*” (one gets knowledge through satva guṇa (purely good quality)). But, here, Siva desired to gain knowledge through his tāmasa guṇa.

**ālam amar kaṇḍattu aran** – when [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) churned the milky ocean, “*hālahāla viśa*” (a poisonous substance) came out first. emperumān ordered Siva to consume it and Siva did the same, keeping it in his throat. His pride comes out of this act of taking the poison. Just because he had preached a few persons who had slightly inferior knowledge to his own, and because he was able to hold poison in his throat (with the mercy of emperumān) could he measure (know) emperumān? While bālāgi had not known *brahmam*, he pointed out whatever he saw to ajāta śatru and said “this is brahmam, hold on to this”. In the same way, while Siva had not fully realised brahmam, he preached others about brahmam. Is it possible to preach others merely because one had the power to consume poison and the power to destroy others? [sīrāmap piḷḷai](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/vedha-vyasa-bhattar/) (twin brother of [parāsara bhattar](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/parasara-bhattar/), both being the sons of [kūrattāḻvān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/kuraththazhwan/), prime disciple of [śrī rāmānuja](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/emperumanar/)) spoke ill of Siva because he was proud and called himself as Lord. parāsara bhattar told him “Siva, by nature, is a good person. Only because of his tamō guṇa (quality of being ignorant) he is proud. Hence, do not vilify him”.

We shall enjoy the 5<sup>th</sup> pāsuram next.

adiyēn kriṣṇa ramanuja dāsan

## 5. Aran naranan namam

*aran nāraṇam nāmam ān vidai puḷ ūrdi urai nūl maṟai uṟaiyum kōyil – varai nīr karumamaḻippaḷippuk kaiyadu vēl nēmi uruvam eri kār mēni onṟu*

**Пословный перевод:**

nāmam – name; aran – (for one) it is haran \[sivan\]; nāraṇan – (for one) it is nārāyaṇan; ūrdi – vehicle; An vidai – (for one) bull, which has no knowledge; puḷ – (for one) garuda, who has vēdam as his śarīram (body); urai – pramāṇam (authentic proof); nūl – (for one) āgamam (scripture) composed by men; maṟai – (for one) vēdam (sacred texts) not composed by men; uṟaiyum kōyil – dwelling place; varai – (for one) hard mountain; nīr – (for one) comfortable water; karumam – profession; aḻippu – (for one) destruction; aḷippu – (for one) protection; kaiyadu – weapon in one’s hand; vēl – (for one) trident; nēmi – (for one) cakkaram (chakra, divine disc); uruvam – form; eri – (for one) like fire; kār – (for one) like cloud; onṟu mēni – (of the two) one is a body to the other

**Перевод:**

**aran nāraṇan nāmam – as per samskritam etymology, “haratīti hara:” one who destroys is known as hara: (another name for Siva). Thus, one person has as his name what he carries out as his profession \[hara: is written as aran in tamiḻ\]. When the world, which is created, engages in wrongful deeds and tries to ruin itself, sivan carries out that task after becoming a form (body) for emperumān. Even though he carries out that task as a subordinate of emperumān,  he lives in samsāram (materialistic realm) which is to be destroyed. sivan’s position is like that of an official in a kingdom who has to pull down the decorative pandal after mahānavami \[as part of navarāttiri or dussehra festival\] on the orders of the King. The name of the other entity, is nārāyaṇan, the emperumān (as per etymology, he is the resting place for all indestructible entities (both sentient and insentient) and he has these entities as his dwelling place since he pervades them all). He has all the universes as his body and as his servitors and he is their Lord, ruling over them as their ātmā (in-dwelling soul of all souls). When someone gets hurt in his foot due to stepping on to a thorn, the pain is felt by the soul which dwells inside the body. In the same way, if people in this world suffer, emperumān treats those sufferings as his own. Since he does not think that he could exist without them, he is called as nārāyaṇan.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*Earlier āḻvār had mentioned through all resources that only emperumān is the supreme entity. He has to say that others are not emperumān. He says that now. āḻvār was asked, “you had mocked at sivan saying ‘aran aṟivānām’. But there are people who consider him to be īsvaran” He responds “through their characteristic features one can know who is īsvaran and who is not”*

**An vidai puḷ ūrdi** – if one were to look at the vehicles for sivan and nārāyaṇan, similar differences will surface. sivan, in line with his cruel characteristic \[of annihilation\] has the bull as his vehicle \[it is common parlance to call a person without knowledge as “*aṟivillāda* *mādu*” (*vidai* is *mādu*) While the word *vidai* also denotes lion, in order to distinguish it from bull, the prefix *An* has been added to *vidai* to denote bull. [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/), on the other hand, has as his vehicle garuda who is the embodiment of vēdam (sacred texts). This shows his greatness.

**urai nūl maṟai** – the authentic proofs that disclose these entities are also similar. *urai* is pramāṇam (authentic proof). This could be either *nūl* or *maṟai*. Here, *nūl* refers to the āgamam that sivan narrated of himself when he was under tāmasa guṇam (state of ignorance). For [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/), it is *maṟai* (sacred text) which does not have the fault of having been written by *a person* and does not yield to the three guṇas (tāmasa, rājasa and sātvika, which are ignorance, passion and purely good, respectively).

**uṟaiyum kōyil varai nīr** – their dwelling places are also of similar nature. For sivan, in line with his cruel nature \[of annihilation\] the dwelling place is the hardened \[*kailāsa*\] mountain while for [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/), in line with his cool and merciful nature, it is *tiruppāṟkadal* (the milky ocean).

**karumam aḻippu aḷippu** – their deeds are also different. One \[sivan\] destroys all the worlds while the other protects the worlds by gently stroking with his divine hands.

**kaiyadu vēl nēmi** – the weapons on their hands will also be different. For sivan, the weapon is the destructive trident while for [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/), it is the protective *tiruvāḻi* (divine disc). [tirumangai āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thirumangai-azhwar/) says in periya tirumadal “*konnavilum vēl*” (the trident kills) and [nammāḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/nammazhwar/) says in [tiruvāimoḻi 2-10-6](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2016/06/thiruvaimozhi-2-10-6-kiriyena-ninaimin/) “*aṟamuyal āḻi*” (the disc establishes righteousness). That emperumān holds weapons such as the disc is brought out by an event which happened in [parāsara bhattar](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/parasara-bhattar/)([kūrattāḻvān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/kuraththazhwan/)’s son)’s time. A person by name nilāttukkuṟip pagavar \[an advaiti\] asked of bhattar “is there any pramāṇam to show that emperumān is with four hands (along with his divine weapons) in paramapadam \[śrīvaikuṇṭam\] too?” to which bhattar quoted mandōdari in śrī rāmāyaṇam yudda kāṇḍam 114-115  “*tamasa: paramō dātā Sanka chakra gadādara:*” (emperumān is superior to tamas \[tamas here refers to the end of prakruti, the primordial nature\]; he supports everything; he holds Sanka and chakra in his hands). pagavar became angry because he did not want to accept this. bhattar said “when pramāṇam is like this, what is the point in getting angry?”.

**uruvam eri kār mēni onṟu –** *uruvam* is form. The forms are also quite varied for the two of them. One \[sivan\] is a burning form like fire while the other is like rain-bearing cloud. A person who is suffering from *tāpatrayam*  (*ādyātmikam, ādibhoudikam* and *ādidaivikam*) – illness caused by (a) one’s own wrong deeds, (b) by nature and c) by [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) – gets liberated from these but confronts such a burning form, he would think that his earlier state was better. The other is like the happiness that a person gets similar to \[an agricultural\] field expecting rain, which indeed receives that rain. taittiriya upaniśat nārāyaṇavalli 11 says “*neelatōyata madyaśtā vidyullēkēva bhāsvarā*” (like lightning amidst blue coloured clouds), thus describing emperumān’s divine form. After “*uruvam eri kār*”, [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/poigai-azhwar/) says *mēni onṟu.* One is sivan who is the *śarīram* (body) while the other is emperumān who is the *śarīri* (the indvelling soul). yajur āraṇyakam 3-11-21 says “*anta:praviśtaśśāsta janānām sarvātmā*” (emperumān pervades all *janas* (those who are born) and controls them). Thus is it clear that everyone is his body (form). Hence in the context of [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/poigai-azhwar/)’s pāsuram, if one is body, the other has to be ātmā.

We shall take up the 6<sup>th</sup> pāsuram next.

## 6. Onru marandariyen

*onṟu maṟandu aṟiyēn ōda nīr vaṇṇanai nān inṟu maṟappanō ēḻaigāḷ – anṟu karuvarangattuḷ kidandu kai toḻudēn kaṇḍēn tiruvarangamēyān tisai*

**Пословный перевод:**

anṟu – that day; karuvarangattuḷ kidandu – lying inside the womb; tiru arangam mēyān – emperumān who is reclining inside tiruvarangam (śrīrangam); tisai – all his svabhāvam (natural characteristics); kaṇḍēn – I saw; kai toḻudēn – did anjali (joined palms together in salutation); onṟum – even a little bit \[of his nature, qualities and wealth\]; maṟandaṟiyēn – I have not forgotten; ēḻaigāḷ – Oh those who have lost the wealth of bhagavat viśayam (knowledge of emperumān); nān – adiyēn \[I, the servitor\]; ōdanīr vaṇṇanai – emperumān who has the complexion of ocean; inṟu – today \[when I have been blessed by his knowledge\]; maṟappanō – how can I forget?

**Перевод:**

**onṟum maṟandaṟiyēn – āḻvār says that there is nothing of emperumān’s qualities that he does not know.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*āḻvār was asked “you make fun of sivan as being body to emperumān and hence he cannot preach others about emperumān. You are also emperumān’s śēśabhūtar (servitor) only. How then would you be able to know about emperumān and preach his qualities to others?”; he responds saying “whenever his satva guṇa (pure good quality) surfaces, sivan learns about emperumān; but when rajō and tamō guṇas surface, he thinks of himself and proclaims that he is īsvaran (one who controls sentient and insentient entities). That is not the case with me. Since emperumān showed himself to me, there is no question of forgetting him at any point of time”. Let us first see the pāsuram:*

**ōdanīr vaṇṇanai** – How can I forget him? Is his \[beautiful\] form such that it could be forgotten! īsn’t his form like that of an ocean which removes one’s sorrow instantaneously on seeing it!

**inṟu maṟappanō** – He came and displayed his beautiful form to me. Is there any possibility for me forgetting him?

**ēḻaigāḷ** – Oh people, who are poor because of lack of knowledge on [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/)! Do not think that I too have matters in this world to think of just as you people have and forget emperumān?

**anṟu karuvarangattuḷ kidandu kai toḻudēn kaṇḍēn** – when I was in the womb, he came and showed himself to me \[since [poygai āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/poigai-azhwar/) was born in a lotus flower, the lotus flower itself is considered as the womb\]. [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) showed himself there because “when he is in the womb, he will not reject me if I show myself to him now. Hence this is the correct time”. [nammāḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/nammazhwar/) says in tiruvāimoḻi 2-3-3 “*aṟiyāk kālattuḷḷē adimaukkaṇ anbu seyvittu*” (emperumān showered his mercy during the time that I was inside the womb, when I had no knowledge). mahābhāratam śānti parvam 358-73 says “*jāyamānam hi puruśam yam paśyēt madusūdana: sātvikas sa tu vijñēya: sa vai mōkśārta cintaka:*” (only that person who is blessed by madusūdanan \[emperumān who killed the demon madu\] at the time of his birth, has sātvika (purely good) qualities; he will desire the puruśārtam (goal) of mōkśam (śrīvaikuṇṭam)). Thus, having remembered emperumān at a time when I could not have thought of (viz. inside the womb), will I forget now when I can only think of him!

**kaṇḍu kai toḻudēn** – I was able to see. As a result of seeing him, I joined my hands in salutation to him.

**tiruvangam mēyān tisai** – he came on his own towards me and gave me knowledge. I did not forget his nature, his form, his qualities and his wealth. Is it the paratva state (the supreme state of [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) that he displays in paramapadam \[śrīvaikuṇṭam\]) of emperumān that [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/poigai-azhwar/) is talking about? No, it is in the [arcā state](http://ponnadi.blogspot.in/2012/10/archavathara-anubhavam-parathvadhi.html) (the form displayed by emperumān in various [divya dēśams](https://koyil.org/)) which is the epitome of his simplicity. It is his form in śrīrangam that I will never forget \[emperumān is reclining in śrīrangam because no one rejected him when he came here\].

We shall consider the 7<sup>th</sup> pāsuram next.

## 7. 7- Tisaiyum tisaiyuru

*tisaiyum tisaiyuṟu deyvamum deyvat tisaiyum karumangaḷ ellām – asaivil sīrk kaṇṇan nedumāl kadal kadainda kārōda vaṇṇan padaitta mayakku*

**Пословный перевод:**

tisaiyum – worlds with all their directions; tisai uṟu deyvamum – the deities in these directions; deyvattu isaiyum karumangaḷ – appropriate activities (like creation, destruction etc) for these deities; ellām – all these; kaṇṇan – the one who was born as kriṣṇa; nedumāl – the one who has extreme love \[towards his devotees\]; kadal kadainda – the one who churned the ocean \[with the request from the dēvas as the reason\]; kār ōda vaṇṇan – the one who has the complexion of cloud and ocean; padaitta – created; mayakku – entities to bewilder

**Перевод:**

**tisaiyum – world having all directions**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*āḻvār was asked “you are saying that only emperumān is fit to be attained. But wherever we see, there are people who are trying to attain and those who are trying to be attained”. āḻvār responds “unlike the situation where emperumān showed himself to me for me to see, the others \[who are not devoted to him\] are trying to attempt to see on their own; emperumān has created an illusion so that they are unable to attain him”. In śrī bhagavat gītā 9-23, kriṣṇa tells arjuna “tē api māmēva kauntēya yajanti avidi pūrvakam” (those who worship other dēvatās (deities) worship only me, but against the rules) and in śrī bhagavat gītā 7-14 he says “mama māyā duratyayā” (it is very difficult to go past this illusion). Let us look at the pāsuram.*

**tisai uṟu deyvamum** – all the deities who are in these directions

**deyvattu isaiyum karumangaḷ ellām** – all the activities of these deities. One deity is given the role of creating, one for destroying, one for destroying everything etc. All these activities have been ordained by [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) only.

**asaivil sīr** – the qualities of emperumān do not contract or expand based on time. They are always fully with him (it is implied that in the case of other deities, this is not so; their qualities will shrink or expand).

**kaṇṇan** – he incarnated himself to show these qualities, as a simple person. He made himself as equal to others through his *iccā* (wish).

**asaivil sīrk kaṇṇan** – even though he incarnated himself as being equal to others, his qualities do not have shortfall. While he created other deities and gave them name and fame, their qualities are not consistent and change.

**nedumāl** – *māl* means greatness. Even though he makes himself simple, he is so great that no one can measure \[know\] him. taittirīya upaniśat ānandavalli says “*yatō vācō nivartantē*” (words return, unable to measure [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/)’s qualities). Alternatively, *māl* also denotes infatuation. He is so infatuated with his devotees that he will never let them down.

**kadal kadainda** – even though he is great, he carries out impossible tasks to protect his devotees \[such as churning the ocean to grant immortality to dēvatās to enable them to fight demons\] . He needs people who pray to him “please give us this”. Just as other means \[such as karma, jñāna, bhakti etc\] need his mercy, the other deities too need his mercy. For his devotees, the other means and other deities are to be avoided completely.

**kārōda vaṇṇan** – even if he does not carry out any of these tasks and even if he brings an end to us, his beauty is such that we will never let go of him.

**padaitta mayakku** – he gives such special powers to other deities and makes us bewildered. He makes those who do not listen to him, to approach such deities by giving these deities powers to grant certain boons, to carry out certain difficult tasks etc. These are the illusory acts that he indulges in to bewilder those who do not believe in him. As [kriṣṇa](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/), he himself says in [śrī bhagavat gītā 7-14](http://githa.koyil.org/index.php/7-14/) *“mama māyā duratyayā”* (it is difficult to overcome my illusion),

We shall take up the 8<sup>th</sup> pāsuram next.

## 8. Mayanga valampuri

*mayanga valampuri vāy vaittu vānathu īyangum eri kadirōn tannai muyangu amaruḷ tērāḻiyāl maṟaittadu en nī tirumālē pōrāḻik kaiyāl porudu*

**Пословный перевод:**

tirumālē – consort of tiru \[mahālakśmi\]; nī – you (who are a satya sankalpan, one who fulfils what he says); muyangu amaruḷ – in the mahābhārata battlefield which is full (of armies); pōr āḻi kaiyāl – with the sudarśana chakra (disc) in your hand for engaging in battle; porudu – fighting (with bhīśma); mayanga – making people (both friendly and inimical) bewildered/perplexed; valampuri – śrī pāncajanyam (conch); vāy vaittu – keeping it in your divine mouth \[to blow\]; vānattu iyangum – the one who is journeying in the skies; eri kadiroṇ tannai – sun who is emitting his rays; tērāḻiyāl – with the wheel of the chariot \[here it refers to his sudarśana\]; maṟaittadu – hiding it; en – for what purpose

**Перевод:**

**mayanga valampuri vāy vaittu – emperumān projected arjuna merely as the motive for the battle. When the enemies were not put down by arjuna’s arrows, kriṣṇa would blow his pāncajanyam (divine conch). Anyone who hears it, whether they are friends or enemies, would become perplexed. Enemies would become fearful on hearing its pitch and get perplexed about what will happen to them while friends will worry as to what will happen to emperumān and become perplexed. śrī bhagavat gītā 1-19 says “sa ghōśa: dārtrāśtrāṇām hrudayāni vyadārayat” (the sound of kriṣṇa’s conch broke the heart of drutarāśtra’s sons into smithereens). śrīviṣṇu purānam 5-21-29 says “yasya nādēna daityānām balahānirajāyata, dēvānām vavrudē tēja: prasādaścaiva yōginām ” (by hearing the tone of which pāncajanya, demons suffered loss of strength, dēvas became more radiant and yōgis (riśis) became extremely happy…). Thus itihāsam and purānams speak about the strength of pāncajanyam’s tone.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*āḻvār was asked “does this mean that everyone will become bewildered and not know about emperumān?” āḻvār responds saying “the bewilderment is in general matters. In the case of his followers, even if he has to tell a lie, emperumān will do it to carry out their tasks and show his affection for his followers” Let us enjoy the pāsuram.*

**vānattu iyangum eri kadirōn tannai** – radiant sūrya (sun) who is journeying in his orbit in the skies. Sun is in a place where he cannot be hidden and he does not stop in one place but keeps moving about. emperumān hid such a splendorous sun whose rays burn down people \[who are at a great distance from him\] who cannot see him with their eyes directly.

**muyangu amarauḷ** – in the battlefield where people are very close to each other \[lot of people were in the battlefield\]. The meaning conveyed here is that he had many people as witnesses to his actions. [nammāḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/nammazhwar/) says in tiruvāimoḻi 7-5-9 “*dēsam aṟiya*” (for the whole world to know).

**tērāḻiyāl maṟaittadu en nī** – cakkaram (disc) is referred to as “*ratāngam*” (part of chariot) in samskrutam (Sanskrit). āḻvār uses the same term in tamiḻ as *tērāḻi* (wheel of chariot). Why did you hide the sun with your divine disc? While you are neutral to everyone, is it proper on your part to do this only for the sake of arjuna? vēdas refer to emperumān as “*satya sankalpa*” (one who is true to his words). When vēdas call you *satya sankalpa,* is it proper on your part to deceive people? \[the reference here is to the act of kriṣṇa in creating darkness by hiding the sun with his sudarśana (divine disc) so that jayadrata would come out of hiding and enable arjuna to kill him\]. Is your act synchronous with your own words in mahābhārata uttara kāndam 70-48 “*na mē mōgham vacō bhavēt*” (my words will never be false)? Will it be appropriate to your being the divine entity? ōr will it be appropriate to the human form that you took when you incarnated yourself?

**tirumālē** – the one who raises the questions \[āḻvār\], answers them himself by calling him as *tirumālē* (consort of mahālakśmi). Since mahālakśmi is biased towards her devotees, tirumāl (one who is in a state of stupor with mahālakśmi) also shows this quality towards his followers.

**pōr āḻik kaiyāl porudu** – you had taken part in the battle with your weapon. In the previous verse, it was mentioned that he had hidden sun with his divine disc. He would have carried out that action with his *sankalpam* (divine will), viz. he did not carry the disc in his hands to hide the sun. But that was not the case with bhīśma (great grandfather of both pāṇḍavas and kauravas, the main protagonists in the battle). kriṣṇa had taken a vow that he will not hold any weapon in the battlefield. bhīśma had challenged him saying that he will make kriṣṇa take to his weapons. āḻvār is now asking emperumān as to why he took his weapon (disc) while going after bhīśma in the battle? The subtle meaning conveyed here is that in order to fulfil the words of his *bhakta* (devotee; bhīśma in this context), he will go against his own word!

We shall consider the 9<sup>th</sup> pāsuram next.

adiyēn kriṣṇa ramanuja dāsan

## 9. Porukottu or enamay

*porukōttu ōr ēnamāyp pukku idandāykku anṟu un oru kōttin mēl kidandadanṟē viritōtta sēvadiyai nītti tisai nadunga viṇ tuḷanga māvadivin nī aḷanda maṇ*

**Пословный перевод:**

viri tōtta sēvadiyai – reddish coloured divine feet which resemble a lotus with blossomed petals; nītti – making them grow; tisai nadunga – making people in all directions to quiver; viṇ tuḷanga – making people in the upper worlds also to tremble out of fear; mā vadivin – with a huge form (as trivikraman); nī aḷanda – measured by you; maṇ – the earth; poru kōttu – with tusks which are meant for battle; ōr ēnamāy – a unique boar \[incomparable\]; pukku idandāykku – (during deluge) immersed in water and dug it out; anṟu – during that time; un – your; oru kōttin mēl – in a corner of one tusk; kidandadu anrē – was it not staying?

**Перевод:**

**poru kōttu ōr ēnamāy – as a boar with tusks meant for battling. He took the incarnation of boar which keeps digging at all the places with its tusks. The boar cannot keep quiet without digging; it is its nature. When not digging the earth, he would dig the saffron present in bhūmip pirātti’s bosom.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*Will he protect only his followers? What would happen to those who are not his followers? Will they have to die? When there is a danger, without making any distinction between his followers and disbelievers, he will protect every living being, even at the cost of his greatness. āḻvār says that when there was danger through mahābali (a ruler in the lineage of hiraṇyakaśyap and prahlāda) and deluge, he took the form of a mendicant to beg from mahābali and took the form of a boar to dig out the earth respectively, not minding about what such forms would do to his greatness. Let us look at the pāsuram.*

**ōr ēnam** – an incomparable, unique boar. Even if he has a body which could be destroyed, his beauty was such captivating that one would carry out *ālatty* (a custom carried out in the southern parts of  India to ward off evils).

**pukku idandāykku** – the way the boar went under water to search for earth which had got stuck to the walls of the universe. Just us pearl divers dive into the deep parts of the ocean in search of pearls, [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) also dove into deeper parts to search for earth.

**anṟu un oru kōttin mēl kidandadanṟē** – the earth that you dug out from the deep waters with so much of involvement was lying at one corner of your tusk like a bluish jewel! (the opinion here is that the earth was so small compared to his huge form that it could have passed through the gap between his teeth). It is clear from this that the involvement of the person who protects is much larger than the object that is to be protected. When a magnanimous person is asked to arrange food for one person, he would arrange for nine persons. In the same way, to dig out such a small earth, [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) took the huge form of a boar.

**viritōtta sēvadiyai nītti** – stretching his reddish coloured divine feet which were like the blossomed petals of a lotus. While there is reference to only the petals here, mentioning the petal is equivalent to mentioning the flower itself. Another interpretation is: “*viritōtta*” also refers to shining ear studs. Here it would be calling out to [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) who has shining ear studs \[and then referring to his reddish coloured divine feet\].

**tisai nadunga viṇ tuḷanga** – emperumān measured the worlds making people in various directions to quiver; making dēvas who are in upper worlds (celestial persons) to quiver. He measured the worlds only for redeeming them for the sake of dēvas. Why then should they be afraid? Here, their fear is out of affection for him, wondering “what will happen now?”

**māvadivil nī aḷanda maṇ** – the huge form that you had taken as trivikrama in order to measure the earth. Wasn’t that same earth like a dot on your tusk when you took varāha avatāram! The opinion conveyed in this pāsuram is that [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) will carry the earth on his head as well as on his foot in order to protect it (as varāha and as trivikrama respectively) when his focus in on protecting the earth.

We shall next enjoy the 10<sup>th</sup> pāsuram.

## 10. Mannum malaiyum

*maṇṇum malaiyum maṟi kadalum mārudamum viṇṇum viḻungiyadu meyyenbar eṇṇil alagu aḷavu kaṇḍa sīr āḻiyāykku anṟu iv vulagu aḷavu uṇḍō un vāy*

**Пословный перевод:**

maṇṇum – the earth; malaiyum – the mountains; maṟi kadalum – the oceans which throw up waves constantly; mārudamum – the air; viṇṇum – the sky \[all these\]; viḻungiyadu – ate up; mey enbar – (riśis, sages) will say that it is true; eṇṇil – if one were to delve into this; alagu aḷavu kaṇḍa sīr āḻiyāykku – one who has seen the boundary (limitlessness) of auspicious qualities and who has the divine chakra (disc); anṟu – during that time; un vāy – your divine mouth; ivvulagu aḷavum uṇḍō – was it bigger than the worlds

**Перевод:**

**maṇṇum – the earth, which supports all the entities in the world**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*In the earlier pāsuram, āḻvār mentioned that with a huge form, he carried out a small task (of begging King mahābali). In this pāsuram, he talks about carrying out a huge task (swallowing the entire world) with a small form \[he lay down as a child on the leaf of a banyan tree after swallowing all the worlds\]. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings first.*

**malaiyum** – the mountains which serve the earth

**maṟi kadalum** – the oceans which surround the earth and throw up the waves

**mārudamum** – air which is required for living beings to breathe and for supporting them

**viṇṇum** – the skies which provide space for the aforementioned entities

**viḻungiyadu meyyenbar** – vēdam and riśis,  who speak only truth, say that you had swallowed all the above mentioned entities like butter, to contain them in a small portion of your stomach.

Normally when one recounts the five elements, in tamiḻ, the usage is maṇṇum punalum (earth, water etc). Why did [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/poigai-azhwar/) say earth, mountains etc? When he swallowed all the entities, he did not swallow them in any order! That he swallowed these was true is claimed by vēdas and those who are highly knowledgeable in vēdas (riśis or sages).

**eṇṇil** – if we have to get a measure of such you \[you refers to [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/)\]

**alagu aḷavu kaṇḍa sīr āḻiyāykku** – alagu refers to the instrument used for measuring. It has several units of measurement such as lakhs, crores, śankam, mahāśankam, padmam, kumudam etc. The count of auspicious qualities that you have is so huge that these units cannot measure them. He has auspicious qualities and weapons such as the divine disc. It is said “*matikśayānnivartatē na gōvinda guṇakśayāt*” (I am unable to about the qualities of gōvinda, not because of any shortcoming of his qualities but due to shortcoming of my knowledge).

**anṟu ivvulagaḷavum uṇḍō un vāy** – on that day when you swallowed all the worlds to keep them safely in your divine stomach, was your divine mouth bigger than these worlds to enable you to swallow them? [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/poigai-azhwar/) hints here that with a small mouth, he was able to swallow such large dimensional worlds, thus exhibiting his wondrous activity.

We shall move on to the 11<sup>th</sup> pāsuram.

## 11. Vay avanai alladu

*vāy avanai alladu vāḻttādu kai ulagam tāyavanai alladu tām toḻā pēy mulai nanju ūṇāga uṇḍān uruvodu pēr allāl kāṇā kaṇ kēḷa sevi*

**Пословный перевод:**

vāy – (my) mouth; avanai alladu – other than emperumān; vāḻttādu – will not praise; kai tām – the hands themselves; ulagam tāyavanai alladu – other than trivikraman who measured all the worlds by growing; toḻā – will not worship (anyone else); pēy mulai nanju – the poison which was applied to pūtanā’s bosom; ūṇāga uṇḍān – one who drank the milk as his food (to sustain himself); uruvodu pērallāl – other than his divine form and his divine names; kaṇ kāṇā, sevi kēḷā – eyes will not see; ears will not listen to

**Перевод:**

**vāy avanai alladu vāḻttādu – my mouth will not praise anyone other than emperumān. Even if it is asked to praise others like the people of the world do, it would not do so.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*āḻvār says that even if he wishes to move away from emperumān, who is the protector of all, his senses will not leave emperumān and praise anyone else. Just as emperumān will not stop from protecting the worlds, āḻvār’s senses will also not stop from praising emperumān. Redeeming his senses from emperumān is as difficult as making someone to get involved with emperumān.*

k**ai ulagam tāyavanai alladu tām toḻā –** my hands will not worship anyone other than the entity who kept his divine feet on the heads of all the people in the world when he went about the task of measuring the worlds. He did this even when nobody asked him to keep his divine feet on their heads. Even when I prevent my hands from vorśipping, they will worship him automatically, after seeing the simplicity of [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/). His divine feet are such that they will make the hands of those who do not worship him, to worship him!

**pēy mulai nanju ūṇāga uṇḍān** – the term pēy refers to pūtanā, the demon friend of kamsa. She came as if she could not live without giving him her milk. He \[kriṣṇa\] also drank that milk as if he could not live if he had not consumed that milk.

**uruvodu pērallāl kāṇā kaṇ kēḷā sevi** – my eyes will not see any form other than [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/)’s; my ears will not listen to anyone’s name other than emperumān’s.

[āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/poigai-azhwar/) thus describes how all his sensory organs had got engaged with [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/). Is he not hriśīkēśan! (one who controls senses)

We shall take up the 12<sup>th</sup> pāsuram next.

## 12. Sevi vay kan

*sevi vāy kaṇ mūkku udal enṟu aimpulanum sendī puvi kāl nīr viṇ būtam aindum aviyāda jñānamum vēḷviyum nallaṟamum enbarē ēnamāy ninṟārkku iyalvu*

**Пословный перевод:**

sevi vāy kaṇ mūkku udal – ear, mouth, eye, nose and body \[skin\]; enṟu aimpulanum – the five sensory organs; sendī puvi kāl nīr viṇ – fire, earth, wind, water and sky \[ether\]; būtam aindum – the five elements; aviyāda jñānamum – jñāna (knowledge) which is the indestructible form of bhakti (devotion); vēḷviyum nallaṟamum – karmas (deeds such as agnihōtram, a ritual) and auspicious qualities; ēnamāy ninṟārkku iyalvu enbarē – are the path to attain varāhap perumāḷ, say people (how ignorant!)

**Перевод:**

**sevi vāy … – āḻvār lists the lowest level of entities for those who follow bhaktiyōgam (devotion as a means of attaining emperumān). prakruti (primordial matter) is at the lowest level to be known. jīvātmā is at a higher level, to be known. emperumān is at the highest level and is to be attained. āḻvār has first listed the five sensory organs viz. ear, mouth, eye, nose and skin. Mentioning these five implies the five organs of action (speech, hands, legs and the excretory organs) for carrying out tasks.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*In the earlier pāsuram, āḻvār said that only emperumān is the entity to be held on to. In this pāsuram, he says that bhakti (devotion) is the path through which he is to be attained. Let us look at the pāsuram and its meanings.*

**sendī puvi kāl nīr viṇ būtam aindum** – this refers to the five elements of fire, earth, wind, water and ether and by implication, their subtle elements of form, smell, feel, taste and sound respectively. Thus the five sensory organs, the five organs of action, the five elements and the five subtle elements, totalling twenty entities are mentioned. Taken together with prakruti (primordial matter), mahān (the derivative of prakruti), ahankāram (ego) and manas (mind), these twenty four would together make up the body. Hence we must take it that [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/poigai-azhwar/) is talking about the physical body here.

**aviyāda jñānamum** – the unquenchable knowledge. This term refers to bhakti (devotion), which like a stream of oil, flows uniformly without any distraction. In vēdārta sangraham it is said “*bhaktiśca jñāna viśēśa: eva*” (bhakti is a type of jñāna only). Hence jñāna here refers to bhakti.

**vēḷviyum** – deeds like carrying out yajña (rites) for development of bhakti and for overcoming sins, without focussing on the end result. brahma sūtram 4-1-16 says “*agnihōtrādi tu tat kāryāyaiva tat darśanāt*” (deeds such as agnihōtram (a type of ritual with agni, fire) are to be carried out for the development of bhakti, since it is seen this way in vēdam). brahma sūtram 41-12 says “*āprayaṇāt tatrāpi hi druśtam*” (one should observe such deeds till the end of one’s life. Such rules are seen at all times). bruhadāraṇyaka upaniśat 6-4-22 says “*tamētam vēdānuvacanēna brāhmaṇā vividiśanti yajñēna dānēna tapasā anāśakēna* ” (the *brahmajñānis* (those who know about [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/)) like to know about the supreme entity through practicing of vēda, *yajña*, *dāna* (charity), *tapas* (penance) without desire in the end result). From all these it is to be known that deeds, which are carried out for creation of bhakti and for its development, are the path to attain emperumān.

**nallaṟamum** – ability to distinguish between good and bad, as well as qualities such as compassion, charity etc are referred to by this word.

**enbarē** – these are famously mentioned in śāstrams such as vēdam (sacred texts).

**ēnamāy ninṟārkku iyalvu** – all the above mentioned are considered as paths to attain [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) who is simplicity personified and who helps those who are in danger. Even if these are the means, it is emperumān who gives the result \[*puruśārttam*\] as is brought about by the usage of the term “*ēnamāy ninṟār*” (one who stood as a boar). emperumān himself has said in śrī bhagavat gītā 12-6 and 12-7 “*yē tu sarvāṇi karmāṇi mayi sanyasya matparā: \| ananyēnaiva yōgēna mām upāsitē  \|\| tēśām aham samuddārtā mrutyu samsāra sāgarāt \| bhavāmi nacirāt pārta mayyāvēśit cētasām \|\|*” (arjuna! I lift from the ocean of samsāra (materialistic realm) which takes people towards destruction, those who submit all their karmas (deeds or activities) to me  and who consider me as the end result, without looking for any other result \[such as wealth, kaivalyam (soul enjoying itself) etc\] through the path of meditation, worship etc, with total dedication).

Thus, [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/poigai-azhwar/) divines through these that bhakti is the path to attain [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/). There is another interpretation too for this pāsuram. Instead of assuming that the pāsuram is specifying the path of *sādana bhakti* (carrying out devotion as a means of attaining emperumān), one can consider that it is referring to āḻvār’s sādya bhakti (carrying out devotion to spend one’s time on earth after surrendering to emperumān) which is a part of his experience in this world. ōr, it can be assumed that he is condemning *sādana bhakti* and is recommending us to carry out *prapatti* (surrendering to emperumān). In this case, for the word *enbarē* one should consider the meaning as mentioned in [pāsuram 4](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2017/06/mudhal-thiruvandhadhi-4/) “*aran aṟivānām*” viz. “people are interpreting like this. How ignorant are they!” In that case, the following meanings should be considered:

**sevi vāy ….** – [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/poigai-azhwar/) mentions about the five sensory organs which wade into matters other than [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/), the five elements which are the causative factors for the sense organs, (the physical) body which was created by these five elements, the knowledge which is focussed on other matters (than on emperumān) and says, when no one sought him at the time of danger, emperumān himself came as a huge boar to save the earth from deluge. Finally he condemns them saying, would these (the sensory organs, elements, body, knowledge etc) be the *sādanam* (means) for attaining emperumān? [nammāḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/nammazhwar/) too, in his tiruviruttam 99 says “*jñānappirānai allāl illai nān kaṇḍa nalladuvē*” (there is no means other than knowledge personified varāha \[boar\] among all the means). During deluge, no one prayed to him; yet he came and saved all. Hence he is the means to be considered.

We shall next consider 13<sup>th</sup> pāsuram.

## 13. Iyalvaga intulayan adikke

*iyalvāga īntuḻāyān adikkē sella muyalvār iyal amarar munnam iyalvāga nīdiyāl ōdi niyamangaḷāl parava ādiyāy ninṟar avar*

**Пословный перевод:**

munnam – from time immemorial; iyal amarar – nityasūris who are fit; In tuḻāyān adikkē sella – to approach the divine feet of puruśōttama (emperumān) who is adorning tuḷasi garland; iyalvāga – appropriately; muyalvār – will attempt; iyalvāga – appropriately (to others); nīdiyāl ōdi – properly reciting the divine names; niyamangaḷāl parava – praising as per the way śāstras \[scriptures\] ordained; avar – the emperumān; ādiyāy ninṟār – was engaged (in attracting cētanas)

**Перевод:**

**iyalvāga …. muyalvār iyal amarar –   āḻvār says that nityasūris are the apt entities for serving emperumān. The meaning for iyalvāga can be taken as “fit” or as “by nature”. For the others \[samsāris, those who dwell in materialistic realm\], it is a cycle of taking births in various forms (and bodies) due to bad deeds, getting involved with worldly matters and at some point of time, by the mercy of emperumān, getting engaged with matters concerning emperumān. However, for nityasūris, their involvement with emperumān is due to their nature.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*āḻvār was asked, “your tongue will not praise anyone other than him. But, for the others, when they make attempts to attain him they need total involvement. Is it possible?”. āḻvār responds saying “those who attained him, as their nature, are the nityasūris (permanent dwellers of śrīvaikuṇṭam). emperumān will himself make arrangements for the others to attain him” Let us enjoy the pāsuram and its meanings.*

**In tuḻāyān** – emperumān adorns tuḷasi garland to display his aiśvaryam – that he is the Lord of both nitya vibhūti \[spiritual realm\] and leelā vibhūti \[materialistic realm\].

**adikkē sella** – in order to attain his divine feet. śrī rāma tells lakśmaṇa in śrī rāmāyaṇam ayōdyā kāṇḍam 16-29 “*bhāvajñēna krutajñēna darmajñēna ca lakśmaṇa* I *tvayā putrēṇa darmātmā nasamvrutta: pitā mama*” (Oh lakśmaṇa! My father is not dead as he has as his son, one who knows my mind, one who knows (daśarata’s) activities, one who knows the customs of servitorśip) \[in other words, lakśmaṇa is all these to śrī rāma\]. The nityasūris also carry out tasks as per [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/)’s mind.

**muyalvār iyal amarar munnam** – nityasūris are capable, on their own, to take the initiative and serve emperumān. lakśmaṇa says in śrī rāmāyaṇa ayōdyā kāndam 31-25 “*aham sarvam kariśyāmi jāgrata: svapataśca tē*” (I will carry out all kainkaryam (services) to you when you are awake and asleep). nityasūris will also carry out all kainkaryams.

Is it only nityasūris who can carry out all services to [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/)? [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/poigai-azhwar/) says that he will create opportunities for us (the [jīvātmās](https://granthams.koyil.org/2013/03/06/thathva-thrayam-chith-who-am-i/)) to attain him \[and serve him\].

**iyalvāga** – in an appropriate way; reciting his divine names as per śāstram; to learn vēdas by carrying various penances as ordained in śāstras.

**niyamangaḷāl parava** – to be ecstatic after knowing about emperumān through *śravaṇam* (hearing), *mananam* (thinking about what was heard) and *nididyāsanam* (meditating continuously about [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/)) as ordained in śāstras.

**ādiyāy ninṟār avar** – [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/poigai-azhwar/) says that there is no shortcoming in us after emperumān

1. decides to bless us with his mercy by taking the initiative
2. creates desire in us towards him
3. provides all the implements to create that desire
4. enjoys us
5. provides us the end result
6. makes us to attain him

kriṣṇa says in [śrī bhagavat gītā 9-24](http://githa.koyil.org/index.php/9-24/) “*aham hi sarva yajñānām bhōktā ca prabhurēvaca*” (only I am vorśipped in all yāgams (spiritual rituals) and I am the one who gives benefits out of those rituals) and in [10-8](http://githa.koyil.org/index.php/10-8/) “*aham sarvasya prabhavō mattassarvam pravartatē*” (I am the cause of all creations and all entities function because of me). There is no difference between nityasūris and other cētanas (sentient entities) as far as their relationship with [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) is concerned.

We shall move on to the 14<sup>th</sup> pāsuram.

adiyēn kriṣṇa ramanuja dāsan

## 14. Avar avar tam

*āvar avar tām tām aṟindavāṟu ētti īvar ivar em perumān enṟu suvar misaic cārttiyum vaittum toḻuvar ulagaḷanda mūrtti uruvē mudal*

**Пословный перевод:**

avar avar – people who have raJo guṇam (yielding to passion) and tamō guṇam (yielding to laziness); tām tām aṟindavāṟu – appropriate to their qualities and knowledge; ivar ivar em perumān enṟu ētti – praising a few deities as “he is our svāmys (Lords)”; suvar misaic cārttiyum – drawing their figures on walls; vaittum – installing (as an idol); toḻuvar – will worship; ulagu aḷanda – (one who kept his divine feet on the heads of those who worship and those who are vorśipped and) measured all the worlds; mūrtti uruvē – the divine form of trivikrama; mudal – is primary

**Перевод:**

**avar avar – it is said that “lōko binnaruci” (people have different tastes). Some people of the world will have excess of rajō guṇa (such people will be very passionate about everything) while others will have excess of tamō guṇa (such people will be very lazy, will be ignorant and will be disinterested in anything). āḻvār calls such people as avar avar, indifferently.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*When asked “While emperumān stands ahead and creates interest in him among the samsāris, why are these people following various other dēvatās (deities)?”, āḻvār says that even if they do so, the deities that they hold on to, ultimately attain emperumān. Let us see the pāsuram and its meanings.*

**tām tām aṟindavāṟu ētti** – such people who have rajō and tamō guṇas will also have appropriate knowledge only. They will not follow śāstras (scriptures). They will worship as per their knowledge.

**ivar ivar em perumān enṟu** – they will hold on to some deity or the other and say “he is my svāmy (my Lord)”. When the sons of King sakara went searching for their aśvamēda horse (horse let out freely to denote their boundaries), they started catching anyone that they came across as “you only stole our horse”. In the same way, these people will hold on to anyone that they fancy and denote him as their perumān (supreme entity). Just as some people leave out good materials and have liking for neem and onions, these people will leave out the supreme entity [śrīman nārāyaṇa](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) and and go after deities with inferior qualities. rudra (sivan) says in harivamśam 138-8 “*satvaniśtā hi puruśā:*” (people with *satva guṇam* (purely good qualities) should always meditate on [*hari*](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) [(śrīman nārāyaṇa)](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/)) and “*harirēkas sadā dyēya:*” (only hari should be always meditated upon).

**suvar misaic cārttiyum vaittum toḻuvar** – they will worship their deities after drawing their forms on walls and/or installing their deities as idols in some places. \[Even\] If [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) incarnates in some forms to shower his grace on people, they will offer their prayers to these deities instead of to emperumān.  Even if they carry out such worship…..

**ulagu aḷanda mūrty uruvē mudal** – the form of [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) which he took to keep all the people of the worlds under his divine feet \[trivikrama\], is the primary one. Did he not keep his divine feet on those who are holding on to other deities and on those deities too to measure the worlds!

We shall take up the 15<sup>th</sup> pāsuram next.

## 15. Mudal avar muvare

*mudalāvār mūvarē am mūvaruḷḷum mudalāvān mūri nīr vaṇṇan mudalāya nallān aruḷ allāl nāmanīr vaiyagattu pallār aruḷum paḻudu*

**Пословный перевод:**

mūvarē – brahmā, viṣṇu and Siva, the three; mudalāvār – are important; ammūvaruḷḷum – among these three mummūrttis (the three forms); mūri nīr vaṇṇan – emperumān who has the complexion and nature of the vast ocean; mudalāvān – is the causal entity; mudalāya – the cause of everything; nallān – the benefactor’s; aruḷ allāl – apart from his grace; nāmam nīr vaiyagattu – in the famous earth surrounded by ocean; pallār – many others’; aruḷum – grace; paḻudu – is of no use

**Перевод:**

**mudal āvār mūvarē – instead of holding on to everyone saying “he is emperumān” “he is emperumān”, it is good to say that there are three important deities so that it will be easier to discard two.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*āḻvār was asked “you say that emperumān is the primary deity. But in the world we see that there are people who follow other deities and that these deities fulfil their wishes”. āḻvār responds saying “all those are of no use” Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings first.*

**am mūvaruḷḷum mudal āvān mūri nīr vaṇṇan** – there is no one apart from these three who is an important deity. Nor is there anyone else who, along with these three, could be termed as a primary deity. Among these three, the causative entity \[who is the cause for all the worlds\] is the one who has the complexion and form of ocean which keeps throwing out waves. It is he who dwells within the souls of *brahmā* and *Siva* as *antaryāmi* (in-dwelling soul) and manifests himself as the unique viṣṇu, and protects the worlds. In [tiruneduntāṇḍakam 2](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2017/06/thirunedunthandakam-2/), [tirumangai āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thirumangai-azhwar/) says “*ōr uruvam pon uruvam onṟu sendee*” (one of them is of golden hue \[brahmā\] and one is red in colour \[rudra\]). [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/poigai-azhwar/) rejects these two and says that the *tirumēni* (divine form) of [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) is like the ocean.

**mudalāya nallān aruḷ allāl** – emperumān, being the cause for all the worlds as well as having these as his possessions, has deep love for these. Apart from his grace ……

**nāmam nīr vaiyagattu** – this world which is famous and which is surrounded by ocean

**pallār aruḷum paḻudu** – the grace of all others \[deities\] is of no use. The grace of brahmā and Siva as well the grace of many other deities, without any difference, are of no use. Alternatively, we can consider **nāmam nīr vaiyagattup pallār aruḷum paḻudu** as one sequence and say that the grace of all those deities who believe that they have some name on this earth is of no use. **paḻudu** not only denotes that it will not bear fruit but will also be responsible for losing  [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/)’s grace. Giving up the other deities is equivalent to obtaining emperumān’s grace for the [*jīvātmā*](https://granthams.koyil.org/2013/03/06/thathva-thrayam-chith-who-am-i/) [(sentient soul)](https://granthams.koyil.org/2013/03/06/thathva-thrayam-chith-who-am-i/). [tirumaḻisai āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thirumazhisai-azhwar/) in his nānmugan tiruvndādi 68 says “*tiruvadi than nāmam maṟandum puṟam toḻā māndar*” (even if people forget the divine names of emperumān, it could be acceptable; but they should not worship other deities). riśi mārkaṇḍēya who was for a very long time a devotee of Siva, felt ecstatic when Siva gave him up. The riśi felt as happy as vibhīśaṇa felt when rāvaṇa gave him up. emperumān is the *antarātmā* (in dwelling soul) of all deities and all means. If he does not wish to shower his grace on someone, he will create interest for such a person in other matters and remove that person from him. He will remove the desires of his followers in other matters and enable them to enjoy him.

Let us move on to the 16<sup>th</sup> pāsuram.
