## 16. 16- Palude pala pagalum

*paḻudē pala pagalum pōyina enṟu anji aḻudēn aravaṇai mēl kaṇḍu toḻudēn kadal ōdam kāl alaippak kaṇvaḷarum sengaṇ adal ōda vaṇṇar adi*

**Пословный перевод:**

kadal ōdam – the waves in tiruppāṟkadal \[kśīrābdi or milky ocean\]; kāl alaippa – rising against his divine feet; kaṇ valāraum – sleeping because of that \[soft caressing\]; sem kaṇ – having reddish eyes; adal ōda vaṇṇar – emperumān having he complexion of waves which are close together; adi – divine feet; aravu aṇai mēl kaṇḍu – seeing atop the mattress tiruvanantāḻvān (serpent ādiśēśa); toḻudēn – vorśipped (today); pala pagalum – time immemorial; paḻudē pōyina enṟu – that they have been wasted; anji aḻudēn – cried out of fear

**Перевод:**

**paḻudē pala pagalum pōyina enṟu – in the days passed, instead of spending part of the time purposefully and the remaining, purposelessly, āḻvār says that he has wasted the entire time purposelessly. If one were involved with emperumān for some time and not involved with him for rest of the time, one can say that one had spent some of the time purposefully and the rest purposelessly. Instead, the entire time in the past had been wasted, says the āḻvār.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*āḻvār feels regretful that he has wasted lengthy period of time in other means and other deities and thus lost emperumān. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings.*

**pala pagalum** – is it possible to specify this period? īsn’t it without a beginning!

**pōyina enṟu** – is there any purpose in constructing a dam after the floods have receded? In the same way, [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/poigai-azhwar/) says “what is the purpose in talking about something which has passed?”

**anji aḻudēn** – The time spent has been  without a beginning. The time to come now is without an end at all. āḻvār cries for the time that he had spent in the past without any purpose, as he feels that that length of time is equivalent to the supreme time that he now spends experiencing [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/). āḻvār says “*anji aḻudēn*” (cried out of fear). He could cry since he had wasted the time already passed. But why should he fear for the time that is going to come now? He fears for a reason which is going to appear in future. In other words, while his connection with emperumān is permanent, it was he who had lost that connection in the past by not knowing about it. And, he is going to be there in future too! \[He fears that he may again lose that connection\]. Alternatively, instead of crying for the time that had passed, it could be taken that he fears that the future would also be like the past and he may spend it purposelessly and hence cries out of that fear. In śrī rāmāyaṇam ayōdyā kāṇḍam 17-14 it is said “*yaśca rāmam na paścyēttu yanca rāmō na paścayati ninditas sa vasēl lōkē svātmāpyēnam vigarhatē*” (one who has not seen rāma or one who has not been seen by rāma will live in the world as a condemned person. His own mind will condemn him). People who do not see emperumān even for a fleeting moment will suffer like this. When such is the case, is there any need to talk about his losing emperumān since time immemorial?

**aravu aṇai mēl kaṇḍu toḻudēn** –  [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/poigai-azhwar/) says that he saw [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) on tiruvanantāḻvān (ādiśēśa) like the gem carbuncle pressed on a silver plate. As a natural corollary of having his *darśan* (seeing him) he vorśipped him. *aravaṇai mēl* – as mentioned in *paryanka vidyā* he saw emperumān atop ādiśēśa. *kaṇḍu* – even if sees emperumān through the vision of his mind, it appears to him as if he had seen emperumān right in front of his eyes. In śrī rāmāyaṇam, when sītāppirātti was in lankā and śrī rāma was on this side of the ocean, they were able to see each other through the vision of their minds, as if they were seeing each other in person. In the same way, āḻvār too saw emperumān who is in paramapadam (śrīvaikuṇṭam).

**kadal ōdam kāl alaippak kaṇ vaḷarum sengaṇ adal ōda vaṇṇar adi** – the waves in the ocean come and gently caress his divine feet such that he is able to close his eyes and rest awhile; his divine eyes reflect his greatness due to their reddish colour \[people who are magnanimous and who are loving towards others will have reddish eyes, it is said\]; it appears as if one ocean is straddling another; he has a form resembling the beautiful waves; the waves display the divine feet of [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) who is reclining on *tiruppāṟkadal* (milky ocean).  *kāl alaippa* – the tiny droplets of water in the ocean gently rub  on his divine thighs. *sem kaṇ* – eyes appear reddish out of his love.

We shall take up the 17<sup>th</sup> pāsuram next.

## 17. Adiyum padi kadappa

*adiyum padi kadappat tōḷ tisai mēl sella mudiyum visumbu aḷandadenbar vadi ugirāl īrndān iraṇiyanadu āgam irum siṟaippuḷ ūrndān ulagu aḷandanānṟu*

**Пословный перевод:**

vadi ugirāl – with sharp nails; iraṇiyanadu āgam – iraṇiyan (hiraṇyakaśyap)’s heart; īrndan – tore apart; irum siṟai puḷ – huge bird garuda with massive wings; ūrndan – emperumān straddled; ulagu aḷanda nānṟu – on the day when he measured the worlds; adiyum – (his) divine feet; padi kadappa – measured the worlds; tōḷ – the divine shoulders; tisai mēl sella – went beyond the directions; mudiyum – divine crown; visumbu – skies (ether); aḷandadu – pervaded; enbar – so will say (jñānis, the knowledgeable)

**Перевод:**

**adiyum padi kadappa – āḻvār was asked, “is it possible to enjoy emperumān staying in this world, as we wish? Should we not enjoy him only after going to śrīvaikuṇṭam?” āḻvār responds “when his mercy flowed like flood on this earth, did I get it? Did I not lose it even then?”**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*āḻvār says “did I lose emperumān because he is at a long distance at tiruppāṟkadal (milky ocean) and attaining him there would be difficult? Did I not lose him when he came very close?” Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings.*

**adiyum padi kadappa** – [nammāḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/nammazhwar/) says in [tiruvāimoḻi 4-3-6](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2017/01/thiruvaimozhi-4-3-6-kala-chakkaraththodu/) “*kōlamām en sennikku un kamalamanna  kaḻalē*” (your lotus like divine foot is like a decoration on my head). In the same manner, āḻvār says that the earth also donned his divine foot, which he likes immensely, on its head, as a decoration. Alternatively, we can say that the soft divine foot, which the nityasūris\[permanent dwellers of śrīvaikuṇṭam\] wear on their heads, had to measure earth which contains hardened forests and streams.

**padi kadappa** – the divine feet which we had to go and worship, came on their own and measured the earth.

**tōḷ tisai mēl sella** – the divine shoulders, which would normally be embracing pirātti (bhūmidēvi, here), went past the directions.

**mudiyum visumbu aḷandadu** – the divine crown went till the wall of the universe. The divine crown measured the immeasurable skies \[ethereal layers\]. Just as great warriors will win over enemies from the same place where they are standing, [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) also measured the worlds standing in the same place and won them over.

**enbar** – I have heard jñānis (knowledgeable people) say that emperumān did this. But I did not get to see this! [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/poigai-azhwar/) laments, “when the divine feet came on their own and stood over my head also, where had I gone?”

**vadi ugirāl īrndān iraṇiyadu āgam** – [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) tore apart the rough body of *iraṇiyan* (hiraṇyakaśyap).

**irum siṟaip puḷ ūrndān** – emperumān is soft and straddles *periya tiruvadi* (alternative name for garuda)\[in our sampradāyam, hanumān is called as tiruvadi (divine feet) and garuda is called as periya tiruvadi (huge divine feet)\].

**ulagu aḷanda nānṟu** – it is not surprising that I did not get to see [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) when he went to annihilate the enemy of his devotee at one place \[reference to emperumān killing hiraṇakaśyap to protect prahlāda\]. It is not surprising that I could not see him when he went to another place to protect  gajēndra, a devotee \[reference here is to the elephant gajēndar which was troubled by the crocodile\]. [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/poigai-azhwar/) laments that he lost when [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) came, not for one or two individuals, but for all, when he took the incarnation of trivikrama and kept his divine feet atop everyone’s head.

Let us consider the 18<sup>th</sup> pāsuram next.

## 18. Nanra mulaittalai

*nānṟa mulaittalai nanjuṇḍu uṟi veṇṇey tōnṟa uṇḍān venṟi sūḻ kaliṟṟai ūnṟip porududaivu kaṇḍānum puḷḷin vāy kīṇḍānum marudidai pōy maṇṇaḷanda māl*

**Пословный перевод:**

nānṟa mulai talai – from the sagging bosom; nanju uṇḍu – drinking poison; uṟi – kept in the hoop (network of rope for placing pots); veṇṇey – butter; tōnṟa uṇḍān – ate in such a way that everyone knew; venṟi sūḻ kaliṟṟai – elephant kuvalayāpīdam which was both winning and scheming; ūnṟi porudu – fighting, standing firmly; udaivu kaṇḍānum – and killed; puḷḷin vāy – (the form that bakāsura took) crane’s mouth; kīṇḍānum – tore; marudu idai pōy – crawling between the two arjuna trees; maṇṇaḷanda – measuring all the worlds; māl – the supreme emperumān only

**Перевод:**

**nānṟa mulait talai nanju uṇḍu – pūtanā, the demon friend of kamsa, came in the form of yaśōdā, in order to kill kriṣṇa. Since she wanted to feed him, she had excess milk in her bosom, due to which her bosom was hanging. Once kriṣṇa drank the milk that she offered, she turned into her old (demoniacal) form and fell down. The true self of anyone who comes near him \[emperumān\] would be exposed. Thus pūtanā lost the form that she had assumed (of yaśōdā) and attained her demoniacal form.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*āḻvār says that it is not that he had lost his opportunity of having darśan of emperumān because he could  not go to tiruppāṟkadal or because he was not present when emperumān measured the worlds. When he came in an even simpler form as kriṣṇa , he lost, he says. āḻvār mercifully speaks about both vāmanāvatāram and kriṣṇāvatāram in the same breath because in both these incarnations, emperumān (1) embraced all without distinguishing between the great and the lowly people, (2) had beauty, (3) wealth, (4) affection towards followers etc. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings.*

**uṟi veṇṇey tōnṟa uṇḍān** – he caught hold of hoops (a network of ropes to place pots from ceiling) which had butter kept in pots and ate it. Alternatively, not knowing how to hide his stealing, he stole butter such that everyone know that he had stolen and called him a thief. It went to such an extent that even śisupālan (his cousin and a staunch enemy) knew of this and called him names. It is hinted through this that he cannot remain without taking hold of articles that belong to those who are friendly towards him. Since both”*nanju uṇḍu*” (eating poison) and ”*veṇṇey uṇḍu*” (eating butter) are mentioned in the same breath, it is brought out clearly that the belongings of those who are friendly towards him and those who are inimical towards him are treated equally by him – he desires both.

**venṟi sūḻ kaliṟṟai** – the elephant which is always victorious. Instead of being defeated, the elephant, kuvalayāpīdam, had the ability to defeat its enemies. Alternatively, it can be construed as the elephant which was very victorious.

**ūnṟip porudu udaivu kaṇḍānum** – he fought at close quarters with kuvalayāpīdam, as if he is an expert in fighting elephants, and converted its body into an implement for keeping weapons. There is another form for this sequence – **porudu idaivu kaṇḍānum** – here, he fought with the elephant, made it wizened and killed it.

**puḷḷin vāy kīṇḍānum** – the one who killed bakāsura, who came in the form of a crane, by tearing open its mouth is also the one who killed the two demons, yamaḷa and arjuna, who took the form of twin trees by crawling between them and tearing them apart (**marudidai pōy**).

**maṇ aḷanda māl** – the one who went between the arjuna trees and killed the two demons is also the one who measured the worlds with his divine feet. Once [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/nammazhwar/) thought of [kriṣṇa](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/), the form of [trivikrama](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) came to his mind since both were simplicity personified.

We shall move on to the 19<sup>th</sup> pāsuram.

## 19. Malum karum kadale

*mālum karum kadalē  ennōṟṟāy vaiyagam uṇḍu ālin ilait tuyinṟa āḻiyān kōlak karumēnic cengaṇ māl kaṇ padiyuḷ enṟum tirumēni nī tīṇḍap peṟṟu*

**Пословный перевод:**

vaiyagam uṇḍu – after swallowing the world \[after deluge\]; ālin ilai – on the banyan leaf; tuyinṟa – sleeping; āḻiyān – one who reclines on the ocean \[tiruppāṟkadal\]; sem kaṇ – having reddish eyes; māl – one who had affection \[towards his devotees\]; kaṇ padaiyuḷ – when he is reclining in a sleeping posture; enṟum – at all times; tirumēni – his divine form; tīṇḍappeṟṟu – feeling it; mālum – overwhelmed (due to that happiness); karum kadalē – Oh, black coloured ocean!; ennōṟṟāy – what penance did you perform! \[to get this fortune\]

**Перевод:**

**mālum – this word means stupefied or overwhelmed or bewildered. Just as people who get intoxicated after consuming certain types of drinks, the ocean is also feeling intoxicated due to the touch of emperumān constantly. The ocean has the same happiness that muktars (those who are liberated from samsāram (materialistic realm) and have reached śrīvaikuṇṭam) have on enjoying emperumān.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*āḻvār says “When he himself came one day \[as trivikrama\] and approached all, I lost him; this earth, because he came one day and measured it, was touched by him on that day. What fortune do you \[referring to tiruppāṟkadal\] have, that you are always with emperumān!” Let us look at the pāsuram and its meanings.*

**karum kadalē** – vouldn’t the ocean have become pale otherwise? But, emperumān is lying on milky ocean, which is white in colour. How is [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/poigai-azhwar/) then referring to it as the dark coloured ocean? It is said that [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) lies in all oceans and hence it is called as dark coloured ocean. Alternatively, the qualities of an ocean are referred to, in all places. Hence it is called as dark coloured ocean. Another explanation is that since dark coloured emperumān is lying on it, the ocean too has become dark and hence āḻvār is referring to it as dark coloured ocean. Ocean and emperumān are having the complexion of bharata (white) and rāma (dark) respectively.

**en nōṟṟāy** – what penance did you carry out to be inseparable from [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/)? āḻvār wants to rid his attachment toward emperumān and carry out the same penance for a similar result.

**vaiyagam uṇḍu ālin ilait tuyinṟa** – keeping all the worlds within his divine stomach and lying on a banyan leaf which appeared at the same time.

**āḻiyān** – lying atop the banyan tree with all the weapons required for protecting. Holding the sudarśana (disc) weapon on his hand, [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) says that he will release the disc and tear apart the deluge if it comes to swallow the earth. Alternatively, *āḻi* could be construed as referring to ocean and in this case it would refer to his reclining on oceans.

**kōlak karumēni sem kaṇ māl** – eyes which are beautiful, remove the tedium of beholders and shower affection on them. His divine eyes are like those referred to in cāndōjña upaniśat 1-6-7  “*yatā kapyāsam puṇḍarīkamēva akśiṇī*” (eyes like lotus flower just blossomed by sun).

**māl** – if the meaning is considered as pride, this word will refer to *īsvaran* (one who controls). If the meaning is considered as infatuation, it will refer to one who has lot of love.

**kaṇ padaiyuḷ** – where he is sleeping

**enṟum** – at all times. Even if [*pirātti*](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sri-mahalakshmi/) [(mahālakśmi)](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sri-mahalakshmi/) out of a rare discord with [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) were to leave him, the ocean has no separation from emperumān. [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/poigai-azhwar/) is praising the good fortune of ocean which, unlike him, has no separation from emperumān.  For upāsakas (those who attain emperumān through upāsana or bhakti (devotion)), attaining emperumān is the end result; kainkaryam is like a by-product. In the same way, for the ocean, feeling emperumān is the end-result. Just as sweat comes out when one is pounding paddy, kainkaryam, like sweat, comes automatically when emperumān is attained \[through devotion\]. If the underlying knowledge about emperumān, who is the means for attaining him, is obtained, strong belief in him comes on its own.

**tirumēni nī tīṇḍap peṟṟu mālum karum kadalē** – Oh ocean! You are in a stage of stupor after attaining him. I am bewildered because I have not.

**karum kadalē** – He showers qualities which he himself has, on those who he wants to grace. That is why you are also dark, like him. It appears that you have carried out some penance to get this. At the same time, it appears that this has come about because he has graced you especially.

Let us go on to the 20<sup>th</sup> pāsuram next.

## 20. Perrar talaikalala

*peṟṟār taḷai kaḻalap pērndu ōr kuṟaḷ uruvāyc ceṟṟār padi kadanda sem kaṇ māl naṟṟā marai  malarc cēvadiyai vānavar kai kūppi nirai malar koṇḍu ēttuvarāl ninṟu*

**Пословный перевод:**

peṟṟār – dēvaki and vasudēvar, kriṣṇa’s parents’; taḷai kaḻala – chain to snap open; pērndu – coming out of tiruppāṟkadal (milky ocean); ōr – incomparable; kuṟal uruvāy – in the form of a vāmana (dwarf); seṟṟār padi – the world of his enemy mahābali (who thought that it was his); kadanda – one who measured it; sem kaṇ māl – emperumān who has reddish eyes and lot of affection \[towards his followers\]; nal tāmarai adi sē adiyai – the divine reddish feet that resembled beautiful lotus; vānavar – dēvas (celestial persons); kai kūppi ninṟu – standing and folding their palms together (like in anjali posture); nirai malar koṇḍu – with strung flowers; ēttuvar – will praise \[emperumān\]; Al – alas! I did not get this fortune

**Перевод:**

**peṟṟār taḷai kaḻalap pērndu – nanjīyar mercifully said that this line alone \[in this pāsuram\] speaks about kriṣṇāvatāram. There are several interpretations for this line: (1) emperumān came from śrīvaikuṇṭam so that the chains tied to the legs of dēvaki and vasudēvar, his parents, would snap; (2) since their chains snapped, they were able to move out of the prison cell; (3) emperumān uprooted himself and incarnated. Once he was born to them as their child, their problems disappeared. Another interpretation – peṟṟaār also means those who got or attained. Thus, those who attain emperumān have their chains (to samsāram, the materialistic realm) removed. In this case, along with the word pērndu, the words ōr kuṟal uruvāy should be added and the following meaning should be added “emperumān did more than that”. peṟṟār – just as someone gets a treasure, āḻvār says, “didn’t these people get the benefit!”. When the chains of those who deemed emperumān as their son snapped, should one speak of the snapping of chains of samsāram of his followers? Thus it is amply clarified here that all the hurdles of those who connect with emperumān will be removed. He removes whatever his followers deem as a prison \[hurdle\]. The same meaning was conveyed by nammāḻvār in tiruvāimoḻi 2-8-2 “pūṇṭhanduḻāy en tani nāyagan puṇarppu – vīdu mudalām” (the connection with emperumān will grant us śrīvaikuṇṭam).**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*In the previous pāsuram, āḻvār spoke about the happiness that the ocean had in attaining emperumān. In this pāsuram, he says that when emperumān left that ocean and incarnated himself, some persons had the fortune of enjoying him. When he gave happiness to the egoistic celestial people, should we wonder about his grace on the ocean? Let us look at the pāsuram and its meanings.*

**ōr kuṟal uruvāy** – In the earlier sequence, [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/poigai-azhwar/) mentioned about the snapping of chains by [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) by his action of coming \[from śrivaikuṇṭam\]. In this sequence, he mentions about how the chains of celestial persons got snapped by a single act of emperumān. He talks about how his dwarfish figure, which he took by even destroying his great form, became a feast for the eyes. He took such a dwarfish figure that the dwarfs of the world would like his magnificent *trivikrama* form. It is this incomparable form that āḻvār describes by the word *ōr* (unique).

**seṟṟar** – refers to enemies. Does emperumān have enemies? The enemy of his follower (indra, the lord of celestial persons) is his enemy. In mahābhāratam udyōga parvam 91-27, kriṣṇa says “*dviśadannam nabhōktavyam dviśadantam naiva bhōjayēt, pāṇḍavān dviśasē rājan mama prāṇā hi pāṇḍavā:* ” (one should not take food from enemies; one should not offer food to enemies. Oh King! You are inimical to pāṇḍavas. āren’t pāṇḍavas my life!) while speaking to duryōdana. In viṣṇu darmam 37-2 kriṣṇa tells arjuna “*yastvām dvēśti sa mām dvēśti yastvāmanu sa māmanu, abhēdēnātmanō vēdmi tvāmaham pāṇḍunandana*” (Son of pāndu! Whoever treats you as an enemy treats me too as an enemy. Whoever follows you, follows me too. I do not think of you as anyone different from me). Thus, enemies of [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/)’s  followers will be his enemies too. jitantē stōtram 2 says “*dēvānām dānavām ca sāmānyamadidaivatam*” (emperumān is equal toward dēvas \[celestial persons\] and demons). Thus he does not have any enemies of his own! Those who are enemies of his followers, possessing demonical qualities, will become his enemies. In [śrī bhagavat gītā 9-29](http://githa.koyil.org/index.php/9-29/), kriṣṇa says “*samōham sarvabhūtēśu na mē dvēśyōsti na priya:, yē bhajanti tu mām bhaktyā mayi tēśu cāpyaham*” (I am equanimous towards all species; I have neither enemies nor friends. Those who hold on to me with love are with me; so am I with them). Thus he does not see any difference among his followers. [nammāḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/nammazhwar/) says in [tiruvāimoḻi 1-6-3](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2015/08/thiruvaimozhi-1-6-3-idum-eduppum/) “*īdum eduppum il īsan*” and in [tiruvāimoḻi 1-6-5](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2015/08/thiruvaimozhi-1-6-5-kolgai-kolamai/) “*koḷgai koḷāmai ilādān eḷgal irāgamilādān*” conveying the same meanings. Among his followers, he does not distinguish between those whom he considers as being dear to him and those whom he considers are not dear to him; he does not think that he will get some jobs done through certain people and will avoid certain other people; he does not show affection towards some and enmity towards others. It is said that he treats sage vaśiśta and hunter tribal person śabari equally.

**seṟṟār padi kadanda** – he measured the worlds that enemies \[of indra, the lord of celestial persons\] such as mahābali thought were theirs.

**padi kadanda** – he felt ecstatic as if he got what he could never get, after measuring all the forests and hillocks.

**sem kaṇ** – his eyes became reddish in colour due to the happiness that he felt after getting the worlds, as if they were not his in the first place.

**māl** – this word indicates the great stature that he got after getting all the worlds under his divine feet.

**naṟṟāmarai malar sēvadiyai** – his divine feet which became reddish in colour, similar to the fresh lotus flower.

**vānavar** – the celestial people who consider themselves to be in the attic where the simplicity of his incarnation will not be felt on them.

**kai kūppi** – even when he had incarnated on the earth, they stayed where they were and saluted and vorśipped him.

**nirai malar koṇḍu** – with illustrious followers, which have been strung in an orderly way.

**ēttuvarāl** – will worship! They have attained him with flowers, while a sinner such as I have lost him.

**ninṟu nirai malar koṇḍu ēttuvarāl** – similar to people who are able to withstand floods and stand firmly, they will worship him, after having his darśan (vision). The word *nirai* could be compounded with *vānavar* as *nirai vānavar* which will mean lot of dēvas; thus lot of celestial persons will worship him with flowers. The meaning for the word *ēttuvarāl* is as said before – when he incarnated here, those who were in other worlds \[celestial people\] vorśipped him from there, while, I, remaining very much here, lost out.

We shall take up the 21<sup>st</sup> pāsuram next.

## 21. Ninru nilamangai

*ninṟu nilam angai nīrēṟṟu mūvadiyāl senṟu tisai aḷanda sengaṇmāṟku enṟum padai āḻi puḷḷūrdi pāmbaṇaiyān pādam adaiyāḻi nenjē aṟi*

**Пословный перевод:**

ninṟu – standing (near mahābali); nilam – earth; am kai – with beautiful hand; nīr ēṟṟu – taking alms; mū adiyāl – with three steps; senṟu tisai aḷanda – measuring, while walking in all directions; sem kaṇ māṟku – the supreme entity with reddish eyes; enṟum – at all times; padai – weapon; āḻi – cakkaram (divine disc, sudarśan); puḷ – garudāḻvān; ūrdi – vehicle; āḻi nenjē – Oh profound heart!; aṟi – listen (to this); pāmbu aṇaiyān – emperumān who has tivanantāḻvān (ādiśēśa) as his mattress; pādam – divine feet; adai – attain

**Перевод:**

**senṟu ninṟu – emperumān walked up to the yāgasālai (the place where religious rites are conducted) of mahābali and stood in front of the King, displaying clearly his intent of taking alms. His reaching mahābali’s place and standing in front of mahābali are attracting āḻvār.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*āḻvār tells his heart “do not retreat, looking at emperumān’s greatness. Mingling with followers is his nature”. Alternatively, it can be said that āḻvār tells his heart “even though emperumān is great, ignoring that, he will carry out tasks for his followers even if he has to change himself”. Let us enjoy the pāsuram and its meanings.*

**ninṟu** – it appears as if [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) is telling mahābali that  he will not leave until he obtained what he set out for

**nilam am kai nīrēṟṟu** – taking the earth, after wetting his hands with water \[given by mahābali as a traditional system of keeping one’s oath\]. emperumān’s hands know only to give to others; as a result they have grown, by giving constantly. For perhaps the first time, he is taking something. While sītāppirātti was always his (mahālakśmi), [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) (as śrī rāma) took her when janaka maharājan offered him her hand, after king janaka referred to her as “my sītā”. In the same way, emperumān took the worlds, which were always his, when offered by King mahābali.

**mūvadiyāl tisai aḷanda** – he measured all the directions with his three steps. Alternatively, we can consider this as “in every direction, he measured”.

**sengaṇ māṟku** – [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/)’s eyes became reddish after receiving the gift \[from mahābali\], out of happiness. He was happy that he obtained what could not be obtained at all! Is it required of him to display his happiness after obtaining what was always his, from mahābali? Since in the earlier pāsuram (20) [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/poigai-azhwar/) had referred to emperumān measuring the worlds, his thoughts follow him from that pāsuram and he speaks mercifully of the same matter.

**enṟum padai āḻi** – vasn’t it the person who has everything with him, who went to mahābali and asked for alms! [nammāḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/nammazhwar/) says in periya tiruvandādi 87 “*eppōdum kai kaḻaḷa nēmiyān*” (one who always has cakrāyudam (disc as weapon) in his hand). He always has garudan as his vehicle. He always has ādiśēśa as his mattress. (The opinion here is that since he holds one devotee on his hand, one as his vehicle and one as his bed, it is natural that mingling with his followers is his nature). Only the one who is capable of holding the disc on his hand without slipping, one who is capable of riding periya tiruvadi (garudan) astride and who is capable of lying on the lap of ādiśēśa can be the supreme entity!

**pādam adai  āḻi nenjē** – to get the divine feet of such a supreme entity, give yourself unto him; do not be like mahābali who gave a little bit of earth and moved aside.

**āḻi nenjē** – Oh profound heart! āren’t you capable of teaching me too! Do I need to teach you this? Do not think that I am saying these words to impress the gallery, but think that I am seeing these words as the whole truth!

We shall take up the 22<sup>nd</sup> pāsuram next.

## 22. Ariyum ulagellam

*poṟi koḷ siṟai – vings vit many colours uvaṇam – garudāḻvān ūrndāy – riding on veṟi kamaḻum – having sveet fragrance kāmbu ēy men  tōḷi – yaśōdā, having slim śoulders looking like bamboo kadai veṇṇey – butter tat vas curned uṇḍāyai – you, vho ate tat butter tāmbu koṇḍu – vit a small rope ārtta – due to binding taḻumbu – scar yānēyum allēn – vas ī te only one vho knev tis? ulagam ellām aṟiyum – all te people in te vorld knev.*

**Пословный перевод:**

**Перевод:**

**aṟiyum ulagellām yānēyum allēn – ḍoesn’t the whole world know about this! Do not think that only I am talking about it. Even an enemy such as śiśupālan knows about this!**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*emperumān asks āḻvār, “You mentioned that it is my nature to mingle with my followers. I do not know about that. How did you know it?” āḻvār responds saying “You can keep my mouth closed. But can you shut the mouths of the people of this world? (The whole world knows about your nature, after seeing the scars on you)” Let us enjoy the pāsuram and its meanings. aṟiyum ulagellām yānēyum allēn poṟi koḷ siṟai uvaṇam uṟndāy veri kamaḻum kāmbēy mentoḷi kadai veṇṇey uṇḍāyait tāmbē koṇḍu ārtta taḻumbu*

**poṟi koḷ siṟai uvaṇam ūrndāy** – You are capable of guiding garudan who has multi coloured wings. āren’t you the one who controls the entities who have the nature of carrying out kainkaryam \[service\] to you!

**veṟi kamaḻum** – yasōdā pirātti with fragrance. She decorates herself with sweet smelling fragrances so that her little child kriṣṇa will not feel disappointed. Alternative explanation – one who emits sweet fragrance as her nature.

**kāmby ēy mentoḷi  kadai veṇṇey uṇḍāyai** – yaśōdā pirātti has her shoulders like bamboo; it is not hard as bamboo, but soft. Her shoulders are compared to bamboo stick since they are fresh, long and beautiful.

**kadai veṇṇey uṇḍāyai** – the butter churned with so much of difficulty by yaśōdā pirātti without even minding her soft shoulders and which you ate. This action sustains them both – it sustains yaśōdā because she considers kaṇṇan (kriṣṇa) as her life and kriṣṇa considers butter as his life.

**tambē koṇḍu ārtta taḻumbu** – your divine body is so soft especially when you sit astride *periya tiruvadi* (garuda). yaśōdā tied \[such\] you with rope. When she caught kriṣṇa, she tied him with some torn rope that she could lay her hands on. She could have searched for a good rope; but by the time she found such a rope, kriṣṇa would have escaped. Hence she tied him with a torn rope. She cannot lengthen the rope, neither could she contract the *ural* (mortar). When she found that the rope was short, she found space in his own body (by asking him to pull in his stomach). All the [āḻvārs](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/azhwars/) get deeply involved and look at in bewilderment at  the rope which was used to tie him. [tirumangai āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thirumangai-azhwar/) says in periya tirumoḻi 5-9-7 “*kaṇṇiyār kuṟum kayiṟṟāl*” (with a small rope which had several knots); [madurakavi āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/madhurakavi-azhwar/) says in [kaṇṇinuṇ siṟut tāmbu 1](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2015/01/kanninun-chiru-thambu-1-kanninun/) “*kaṇṇi nuṇ siṟut tāmbināl*”(with a small rope which had several knots and which was crossing his body). śrīviṣṇu purāṇam 5-6-14 says “*dāmnā caiva udarē badvā pratyabadnāt ulūkalē kriṣṇamakliśta karmāṇamāhacēdamamarśitā*” (yaśōda, who bound kriṣṇa, who is not bound by karma, with a rope across his stomach to a mortar, said …). [tirumangai āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thirumangai-azhwar/) says in periya tirumoḻi 2-10-6 “*taṟiyārnda karum kaḷiṟē pōla ninṟu tadam kaṇgaḷ pani malgum tanmaiyānai*”- here *taṟiyārnda karum kaḷiṟu pōla ninṟu* refers to kriṣṇa who stood like an arrogant elephant and *tdam kaṇkaḷ pani malgum tanmaiyānai* refers to kriṣṇa standing there, with tears in his eyes, without knowing his strength (just as an elephant stays bound by a chain to a pillar, not knowing its strength). Thus \[poygai\] [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/poigai-azhwar/) tells [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) “it is not only I, but the entire world knows the scar that you had because you were bound to a mortar with a rope”.

Let us move on to the 23<sup>rd</sup> pāsuram.

## 23. Talumbirunda sarnganan

*taḻumbirunda sārnga nāṇ tōyndavām am kai taḻumbirunda tāḷ sagadam sādi taḻumbirunda pūngōdaiyāḷ veruvap pon peyarōn mārvidanda vīngōda vaṇṇar viral*

**Пословный перевод:**

am kai – in beautiful hands; sārnga nāṇ – the cord of bow; tōynda – formed due to constant touching; taḻumbu – scars; irunda Am – were present; tāḷ – in the divine feet; sagadam sādi – formed due to kicking the demon who had entered the wheel; taḻumbu irunda – scar was present; pūm kōdaiyāḷ – piratti with beautifully platted divine hair; veruva – being fearful (of what will happen to the world); pon peyarōn – the demon hiraṇyakaśyap’s; mārbu idanda – tearing his chest; vīngu ōda vaṇṇar – emperumān who has the complexion of ocean with agitating waves; viral – on his fingers; taḻumbu irunda – scars are present

**Перевод:**

**taḻumbu irunda sārnga nāṇ tōynda Am am kai – emperumān’s divine hand will have multiple scars due to the cord of his bow touching his hand constantly. His hand had to bear the scars since he had to hold the divine bow \[constantly to protect his followers\].**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*emperumān tried to hide the scar \[mentioned in the previous pāsuram\] saying “How can I, the sarvēśvaran (supreme entity), be afraid of a woman? How can I be tied down by her? How can the world know about it?”. āḻvār responds saying “if it is a single scar you could probably succeed in hiding it. When there are many scars formed all over your divine form when you carried out tasks to protect your followers, how can you hid them all?” Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**tāḷ sagadam sādit taḻumbu irunda** – his divine feet are so soft and gentle that even pirāttimārs (śrī mahālakśmi and other consorts) would hesitate to touch them. [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) kicked the demon *śakata* with those feet and hence scars were formed there. His purpose in kicking his feet was to cry out for milk, which infants would do when they cry for milk. It so happened that the wheel came there at that point of time and got kicked and the demon, who had pervaded the wheel, died. This, of course, created joy in emperumān.

**vīngu ōda vaṇṇar viral** – how will the fingers of the person whose complexion is like that of the ocean with agitating waves, be? ….

**pūngōdaiyāḷ veruva** – [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) tore the chest of hiraṇyakaśyap in order to help his child devotee prahlāda, so that pirātti will feel happy. But even that pirātti was fearful, wondering what will happen to the world due to narismha perumān’s anger (the form taken by emperumān to kill hiraṇyakaśyap was a combination of human body and lion’s head.

**pūm kōdaiyāḷ** – pirātti feels happy when [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) carries out any task for the sake of his devotees. She knows the greatness of his valour. She once told (śrī rāma) to remain in the garb of riśi (sage) so that he would not harm anyone \[this is a backhanded compliment paid by sītāppirātti in daṇḍaka āraṇyam (forest) when śrī rāma had forgotten to keep his word of protecting the sages there by killing demons who were troubling the sages and she nudged him in a playful way to remind him of his promise, since protecting followers is dear to her heart\].

**vīngu ōda vaṇṇar** – Since [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) narasimha’s anger was tumultuous, [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/poigai-azhwar/) is comparing him to the complexion of an ocean with agitating waves. [tirumangai āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thirumangai-azhwar/) says in periya tirumoḻi 2-5-8 “*adal ariyāyp peruginānai*” (emperumān grew in to the form of powerful narasimha). In nararsimha mantram (hymns in praise of Lord narasimha) emperumān’s ability to pervade everything is identified with the name “*mahāviṣṇum*”

Thus [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/poigai-azhwar/) is asking of emperumān “when scars have been formed on your divine fingers, divine feet and divine hands, in various incarnations, how can you hide them all?”

We shall take up the 24<sup>th</sup> pāsuram next.

## 24. Viralodu vay toynda

*viralōdu vāy tōynda veṇṇey kaṇḍu āycci uralōdu uṟap piṇinda nānṟu kuralōvā dēngi ninaindu ayalār kāṇa irundilaiyē ōngōda vaṇṇā urai*

**Пословный перевод:**

viralōdu vāy tōynda – stains on your fingers and your mouth; veṇṇey – of butter; āycci – yaśōdā pirātti (mother yaśōdā); kaṇḍu – seeing; uralōdu – with mortar; uṟap piṇinda nānṟu – securing well; kural ovādu – without stopping (your) crying; ēngi – yearning; ninaindu – thinking of stealing butter (even then); ayalār – all the others; kāṇa – to look at in amusement; irundilaiyē – were you not!; ōngu ōda vaṇṇā – Oh the one who has the divine form with the complexion of an ocean with agitating waves; urai – please tell the truth yourself

**Перевод:**

**viralōdu vāy tōynda veṇṇey kaṇḍu – kaṇṇan (kriṣṇa) was caught by yaśōdā even as he stole butter and tried to eat by taking it near his mouth. He was caught before the butter, which he relishes the most, could reach his throat! If he had been caught after he ate it, he would have tolerated it. āḻvār says, even as he was about to consume it he got caught red-handed. In one place (siṟīya tirumadal) tirumangai āḻvār says “tārār tadam tōḷgaḷ uḷḷaḷavum kai nītti ārāda veṇṇey viḻungi” (he swallowed the butter, which never gives him satisfaction however much he eats, by dropping his garlanded shoulder down to the bottom of the pot) implying that he ate the entire quantity of butter before being caught. Here, it says “viralōdu vāy tōynda veṇṇey kaṇḍu” which means that he was caught even as he was about to eat the butter. nanjīyar, disciple of parāśara bhattar (celebrated son of kūrattāḻvān) asked of bhattar “the two do not match; which is correct?” to which bhattar responded “until he reached the age when he went after girls, kriṣṇa’s main pastime was stealing butter. Hence, one day he would get caught and one day he would be able to eat the butter before getting caught”. Will a thief, who is used to stealing, give up the habit? Moreover, kriṣṇa was born at midnight, which is the time when thieves are at work. Thus, as nammāḻvār says in tiruvāimoḻi 1-8-5 “vaigalum veṇṇey kai kalandu uṇḍān”, stealing butter is a daily exercise for kriṣṇa.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*emperumān asks āḻvār “I am known for having all my desires fulfilled already. \[When such is the case\] Would I need to steal a material because it is not possible for me to get it and would I need to get caught and be tied?” āḻvār responds saying “Do you not know how you were tied!” Let us look at the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**āycci uralōdu uṟap piṇinda nānṟu** – seeing this, yaśōdā pirātti bound kaṇṇan’s divine body with the mortar in such a way that there were identification marks (scars) on his body. She tied him so tightly that it was difficult to differentiate between kaṇṇan and the mortar.

**kural ōvādu ēngi** – unable to disengage himself from the rope with which yaśōdā tied him, kaṇṇan started crying continuously. Immediately, yaśōdā threatened him with the stick in her hand so that he would stop crying. kaṇṇan shut his mouth but had a forlorn look on his face. Just as [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) needs to take the effort to release us from the bondage that he had put us in, he himself needed the assistance of the one (yaśōdā) to release him from his bondage! Who will believe him if he unties himself, thus disproving that he remains tied to his followers? Only to prove that he does not give up his followers that he puts on acts such as this! For an *avāptasamastakāman* (one who has no unfulfilled desires) such as he, we have to believe that he has a desire to steal a material (butter); that, unable to get it, he gets caught while stealing; that the *omniscient* that he is (one who knows everything), does not know how to untie himself from the bondage that yaśōdā had put him in; that this happens as an everyday affair – what a wonderment is this!

**ninaindu** – even as he remains tied to the mortar, kaṇṇan thinks “there is so much of butter all over tiruvāyppādi (śrī gōkulam). How terrible is it that I am not able to steal them and eat them, being tied down like this”

**ayalār kāṇa irundilaiyē** – is there any one among the five lakhs of girls in tiruvāppādi who did not get to see you being tied to the mortar? All the girls who were troubled by you, were waiting for such a day when you would get caught like this. vihakēśvara samhitai says “*atyutkatai: puṇyapāpai: ihaiva palamaśnutē*” (people experience the results of their puṇya (good deeds) and pāpa (bad deeds) here itself). The girls also got to see you in this condition, so that they could forget all the troubles that they had to endure because of you. Even as kaṇṇan was crying, they were laughing, saying “he deserves this. He needs more of this”.

**irundilaiyē** – you remained like this! Just as rāvaṇa could not forget the arrows of śrī rāma, you also could not forget this incident.

**ōngōda vaṇṇā** – even though you were tied down like this, your divine form was such that it resembled the complexion of an ocean with agitating waves! The act of tying you to a mortar looked like holding all the water in an ocean!

**urai** – please tell the truth. In mahābhāratam udyōga parvam 70-48 kriṣṇa tells draupadi “*dyau patēt prutivī śīryēt himavān śakaleebhavēt, śūśyēt tōya nidi: kriśnē na mē mōgham vacō bhavēt* ” (Oh draupadi! The sky may fall, the earth may crumble, the himālaya mountain may get powdered to pieces, the ocean may dry up; but my word will never be false). Since your words will never be false, you please tell the truth yourself.

**ōngōda vaṇṇā urai** – [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/poigai-azhwar/) implores [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) to tell the truth so that he could enjoy the beauty of his divine form which resembles the ocean and the beauty of his words.

We shall next take up the 25<sup>th</sup> pāsuram.

## 25. Urai merkondu

*uraimēṟkoṇḍu en uḷḷam ōvādu eppōdum varai mēl maradagamē pōla tirai mēl kidandānaik kīndānaik kēḻalāy bhūmi idandānai ētti eḻum*

**Пословный перевод:**

varai mēl – on top of a mountain; maradagamē pōla – embedded like an emerald; tirai mēl – on top of tiruppāṟkadal (milky ocean); kidandānai – reclining; kēḻalāy – in the form of a wild boar; bhūmi – the world; kīndānai – removing from the wall of universe; idandānai – taking it on his tusks; en uḷḷam – my heart; ōvādu – continuously; eppōdum – at all times; urai mēṟkoṇḍu – involved in speaking; ētti – praising (his auspicious qualities); eḻum – will be uplifted

**Перевод:**

**urai mēl koṇḍu – with words of significant meanings; i.e. on matters relating to words. ōr, while speaking about something, carrying on with it. In other words, being fully engaged in it.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*After seeing the continuous crying of emperumān \[in the previous pāsuram\], āḻvār thought about his simplicity and became deeply involved in that. With that, he started praising him. Just as emperumān’s thoughts were on stealing butter, āḻvār’s thoughts were focussed on emperumān. Let us enjoy the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**en uḷḷam ōvādu eppōdum** – my heart is continuously vorśipping him. Since the svarūpam (basic nature) of jīvātmā is to be subservient to emperumān, there will be a change in being subservient only if the svarūpam is destroyed. Since svarūpam cannot be destroyed, this also will not change.

**varai mēl maradagamē pōlat tiraimēl kidandānai** – isn’t he reclining on tiruppāṟkadal just as an emerald gem is embedded on a mountain!

**kidandānaik kīndānai** –  once his follower had any difficulty, he could not stay on his bed. Did he not immediately get up and tear apart the enemy!

**kīndānai** – we can also consider that he tore apart hiraṇyakaśyap (father of his follower prahlāda)

**kēḻalāyk kīndānai bhūmi idandānai** – the two verses can be combined and meaning derived. In this case, it will refer to his varāha avatāram (incarnation as wild boar), with *kīndānai* referring to his removing the earth from the walls of the universe and *idandānai* referring to taking the earth on his tusks.

**ētti eḻum** – by continuously praising these activities in his incarnations, my heart will get uplifted, says āḻvār.

We shall consider pāsuram 26 next.

## 26. Eluvar vidai kolvar

*eḻuvār vidai koḷvār īntuḻāyānai vaḻuvā vagai ninaindu vaigal toḻuvār vinaic cudarai nanduvikkum vēnkatamē vānōr manac cudarait tūṇḍum malai*

**Пословный перевод:**

eḻuvār – the aiśvaryārtis (those who go after wealth) who leave (after obtaining the wealth that they wanted); vidai koḷvār – the kaivalyārtis (those who enjoy their own souls instead of emperumān) who leave (permanently from emperumān); een tuḻāyānai – emperumān who has sweet tuḻāy (tuḷasi) garland; vaḻuvā vagai ninaindu – thinking that they should never leave; vaigal – every day; toḻuvār – the bhagavat prāpti kāmars (those who desire to attain only emperumān) who worship; vinaic cudarai – the fire of pāpa (bad deeds) \[of all the three types of followers mentioned above\]; nanduvikkum – putting out; vēnkatamē – only the tiruvēnkatamalai (tirumalai)!; vānōr – nityasūris’ (permanent dwellers of śrīvaikuṇṭam); manac cudarai – the lamp of their hearts; tūṇḍum malai – is the mountain which stimulates

**Перевод:**

**eḻuvār vidai koḷvār vaigal toḻuvār – kūrattāḻvān once said that these three terms denote aiśvaryārti (those who desire wealth), kailvalyārti (those who desire their own ātmās or souls) and bhagavat prāpti kāmars (those who desire to attain emperumān). From then onvards, these terms carry these meanings. In śrī bhagavat gītā 7.16, emperumān says “caturvidā: bhajantē mām janā: sukrutinōrjuna, ārto jijñāsu: artārti jñānī ca bharatarśabha” (Oh arjuna! Four types of followers worship me – one who wants to get back the wealth that he had lost; one who wants to get wealth for the first time; one who wants to enjoy his own soul and one who wants to attain only me) \[of these four, the two types namely those who want to get back lost wealth and those who want to become wealth for the first time are combined into one and thus we have three types of followers in this pāsuram\].**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*āḻvār says that emperumān entered tirumalai only in order to solve the problems of those who missed out during his various incarnations. Let us enjoy the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**eḻuvār** – Just as [nammāḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/nammazhwar/) says in tiruvāimoḻi 9-1-3 “*poruḷ kai uṇḍāi sellak kāṇil pōṟṟi enṟu ēṟṟu eḻuvar* ” (those who get some wealth in their hands will praise the person who gave it and leave), the *aiśvaryārtis* will leave once their desired wealth has been given to them by emperumān.

**vidai koḷvār** – these are the *kaivalyārtis* who take leave of [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) permanently. They are the ones who tell emperumān “we do not want the experience of enjoying you; it is enough if we are able to enjoy ourselves”. This is similar to a person inviting someone with lot of presents in order to sever his own head! The experience of enjoying one’s own [ātmā](https://granthams.koyil.org/2013/03/06/thathva-thrayam-chith-who-am-i/) is endless. Thus there is nothing like redemption from *kaivalyam*. Hence these people have to leave emperumān permanently.

**een tuḻāyānai vaḻuvā vagai ninaindu vaigal toḻuvār** – these are the followers who desire to be with [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) permanently. They like to experience emperumān who has decorated himself with tuḷasi garland, without any separation at any point of time. Just as it says in paramasamhitā “*yēna yēna dātā gaccati tēna tēna saha gaccati* ” (in whichever way emperumān goes, these muktars will follow him in all these ways), these followers would also want to enjoy being with emperumān like *iḷaiya perumāḷ* (lakśmaṇa, in śrī rāmāyaṇam). Is it possible for them to experience this in tirumalai? One could say that it is only in śrīvaikuṇṭam that everyone’s thoughts and words would be aligned. But the nityasūris, who dwell permanently in śrīvaikuṇṭam, also come to tiruvēnkatam to enjoy the simplicity of emepeumān. Hence [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/poigai-azhwar/) says that it is possible in tiruvēnkatam.

**vinaic cudarai nanduvikkum** – their *vinaic cuddar* is nothing but the brightness of their sins. That is put out by tiruvēnkatam. The enemies for getting wealth, the enemies for getting to enjoy oneself and the enemies for attaining [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) are removed by tiruvēnkatam like putting out a fire. For those who desire wealth, the hurdles are inimical persons and sorrows. For those who desire to enjoy themselves, the enemies are the sensory perceptions. For those who desire to enjoy emperumān, the separation from him is the hurdle.

**vānōr manac cudarai tūṇḍum malai** – for those who have hurdles, tiruvēnkatam hill will remove these hurdles. For nityasūris who do not have any hurdles, the hill will stimulate them to come down and enjoy [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/)’s qualities such as simplicity (of giving darśan (audience) to all his followers) etc.   tiruvēnkatam will make people, who are here, to say as in [tiruvāimoḻi 3-3-1](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2016/08/thiruvaimozhi-3-3-1-ozhivil-kalam/) “*oḻivil kālamellāl udanāy manni vaḻuvilā adimai seyya vēṇḍum nām*” (we should carry out continuous service to emperumān at all times, by being with him) and it will make those in śrīvaikuṇṭam to say as in tiruvāimoḻi 6-10-10 “*agalagillēn iṟaiyum*” (we cannot go away from him even for a moment).

We shall take up pāsuram 27 next.

## 27. Malaiyal kudai kavittu

*malaiyāl kudai kavittu māvāy piḷandu silaiyāḷ marāmaram ēḻ seṟṟu kolaiyānaip pōrkkōdu osittanavum pūm kurundam sāyttanavum kārkkōdu paṟṟiyān kai*

**Пословный перевод:**

malaiyāl – with gōvardana hill; kudai kavittu – inverted like an umblrella; mā vāy piḷandu – tearing apart the mouth of demon kēśi who came in the form of a horse; silaiyāl – with a bow; marāmaram ēḻ seṟṟu – piercing seven peepal trees; kolai yānai – the elephant which was placed to kill; pōr kōdu osittanavum – breaking the tusks which engage in fighting; pūm kurundam sāyttanavum – pushing the kurunda tree; kār kōdu – śankam (conch pāncajanya) which reverberates like the cloud; paṟṟiyān kai – is the divine hand of one who holds it

**Перевод:**

**malaiyāl kudai kavittu – it is natural for people to open an umbrella when it rains. But what did emperumān do when indra, the chieftain of dēvas (celestial beings), started a hailstorm? He took out a hill and held it as an umbrella! emperumān reasoned  “indra is not a demon. Since I have eaten the food meant for him, he is angry and is showering the hailstorm. Let me not harm him. Let me hold this mountain as an umbrella and protect the covherds and the cows” and hence held the gōvardana hill as an umbrella.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*āḻvār, after mercifully mentioning about the way emperumān came to tirumalai, further says that by looking at the divine hands of tiruvēnkatamudaiyān (the Lord who has tiruvēnkatam as his dwelling place) one can state with assurance that they have grown in order to topple the enemies. Let us look at the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**mā vāy piḷandu** – when the demon kēśi came in the form of a horse and started indulging in extensive harm, [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) tore his mouth and killed him. Even sages such as nārada et al screamed in fear on seeing the demon. But emperumān tore him like one would tear a blade of elephant grass by thrusting kēśi’s mouth with his hands.

**silaiyāl marāmarm ēḻ seṟṟu** – with the help of a bow, [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) pierced holes in seven peepal trees in rāmāvatāram. It appears to [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/poigai-azhwar/) that both rāmāvatāram and kriṣṇāvatāram are alike since he is speaking about the activities of kriṣṇa both before and after this incident but in between he mentions an activity in rāmāvatāram. Wasn’t this action done by emperumān only in order to create belief in sugrīva (the monkey king), his devotee, on his strength!

**kolai yānaip pōrkkōdu osittanavum** – in rāmāvatāram, since emperumān was born in the lineage of warriors, he used a bow which is the weapon for warriors. But in kriṣṇāvatāram, he was a covherd and depended on his physical strength (his hand) to kill the elephant *kuvalayāpīdam*.  This elephant was killing those that it happened to encounter with its tusks, which are useful to it in times of war. kriṣṇa caught hold of those tusks and broke them and pushed the elephant to its death.

**pūngurundam sāittanavum** – the tree kurundam (a type of tree) was full of flowers from close to its roots to the top. It was such that children were very much attracted to it. [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) went to it as if he were going to pluck flowers from it and pushed it completely.

**kārrkōdu paṟṟiyān kai** – [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/poigai-azhwar/) says that all these activities \[mentioned above\] have been carried out by one whose hands hold *kārkkōdu*. *kār* means cloud. emperumān’s hand is holding the conch *pāncajanyam* which looks like a cloud. Looking beautiful and making loud noise are common to both cloud and *pāncajanya*. Alternative explanation – the conch *pāncajanya* is holding on to emperumān whose complexion is like the cloud. In this case, emperumān’s divine form looks like cloud in terms of its colour and beauty. Thus the meaning here will be that emperumān is holding the conch with his hand which looks like cloud. His hands are so tender that they will become red by merely holding the conch. With such soft hands, āḻvār says, he has carried out the difficult tasks as mentioned above. In this pāsuram, in the terms *osittanavum* and *sāyttanavum* reference is to plural number \[of hands\] while in the end the reference is to the singular form *kai* \[hand\]. There is nothing contrary in these terms as what is meant is that [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) effortlessly carried out difficult to achieve tasks which require many hands, with his single, soft hand.

We shall enjoy the 28<sup>th</sup> pāsuram next.

## 28. Kaiya valampuriym

*kaiya valampuriyum nēmiyum kārvaṇṇat taiya malar magaḷ nin āgattāḷ seyya maṟaiyān ninnundiyān māmadiḷ mūnṟeyda iṟiyān nin āgattu iṟai*

**Пословный перевод:**

kār – like a (rain bearing) cloud; vaṇṇattu – having the complexion and nature of; aiya – my benefactor; valampuriyum – śrī pāncajanyam (divine conch); nēmiyum – the divine sudarśana cakram (disc); kaiya – are present in your hands; malar magaḷ – śrīdēvip pirātti (mahālakśmi), who was born in a flower; nin āgattāḷ – resides in your heart; seyya maṟaiyān – nānmukan (one with four faces), brahmā, who keeps chanting the great vēdas which identify you; nin undiyān – was born from your divine navel; mā madiḷ – having huge protective walls; mūnṟu – the three cities; eyda – shot arrows; iṟaiyān – rudra (Sivan) one who considers himself as God; nin āgattu – in your divine form; iṟai – in a corner

**Перевод:**

**kaiya valampuriyum nēmiyum – śrī pancajanyam (divine conch) and sudarśanam (divine disc) are in your hands. Is there anything higher than these two for aiśvaryam (wealth)!**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*āḻvār says that even as emperumān is divining from tiruvēnkatam, he is displaying all his wealth. It could also be construed as emperumān is displaying all his beauty or all his simplicity. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**kār vaṇṇattu aiya** – you are like the rain bearing cloud which removes one’s tiredness by merely looking at it. As a result, you are the natural relative to everyone, rendering help without expecting anything in return. āren’t you such a relative that all your wealth also becomes my wealth!

**malar magaḷ nin āgattāḷ** – periya pirātti (śrī mahālakśmi) is residing on your divine chest. [nammāḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/nammazhwar/) in his tiruvāimoḻi 6-10-10 has said the same thing “*alarmēl mangai uṟaimārbā*” (you have the chest on which alarmēl mangai, śrī mahālakśmi, resides). Is there anything above being the consort of mahālakśmi, to become the boundary of wealth!

**seyya maṟaiyān nin undiyān** – [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/poigai-azhwar/) says that [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) has everyone with him,  without making any distinction between śrī mahālakśmi and others. vēdas are the pramāṇams (authentic texts) which identify you precisely. brahmā who has the ability to provide these vēdas, lives on your divine navel and attains his abilities.

**māmadil mūnṟu eyda iṟaiyān nin āgattu iṟai** – Sivan is popularly known as *īśvaran* (Lord) in the world. Such a Sivan, who burnt down the three towns which had huge protective walls, resides in a corner of your divine form and attains his abilities as a result. Just as a few people get a name by talking ill of others, Sivan too got a name by burning the three towns. Another interpretation – Sivan, who burnt the three towns, resides in a corner of your divine form and hence has acquired the pride as *īśvaran* and is proudly roaming consequently.

We shall move on to the 29<sup>th</sup> pāsuram next.

## 29. Iraiyum nilanum

*iṟaiyum nilanum iru visumbum kāṟṟum aṟai pulanum sem tīyum āvān piṟaimaruppin paim kaṇ māl yānai padu tuyaram kāttaḷitta sem kaṇ māl kaṇḍāy teḷi*

**Пословный перевод:**

iṟaiyum – he is the paramapadanātan (the Lord of śrīvaikuṇṭam); nilanum – he is also the bhūmi (earth); iru visumbum – he is also the well spread skies; kāṟṟum – he is also the wind; aṟai pulanum – he is also the oceans which keep making noise continuously; sem tīyum – he is also the red hot fire; āvān – he is all of these; piṟai maruppil – tusks in the shape of the moon’s crescent; paim kaṇ – having beautiful eyes; māl yānai – huge elephant; padu tuyaram – its deep sorrow; kāttu – removing it; aḷitta – protecting it; sem kaṇ māl kaṇḍāy – behold the paramapuruśa (supreme entity) with lotus like eyes; teḷi – (Oh my heart!) know this well

**Перевод:**

**iṟaiyum – just as river kāviri flows transgressing both sides, without any shores, emperumān also has a similar quality, being the paramapadanātan (Lord of śrivaikuṇṭam).**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*āḻvār tells his heart, in this pāsuram “Oh heart!  don’t retreat from approaching sarvēśvaran (supreme entity) because he is great. He has announced to everyone that he is devoted to his followers by showering his grace on gajēndra \[the elephant in distress\]” Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**nilanum iru visumbum kāṟṟum aṟai punalum sem tīyum āvān** – by referring to the five elements, [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/poigai-azhwar/) thinks of him as having pervaded all over the universe. Thus he owns the entire leelā vibhūti (materialistic realm). By mentioning him as paramapadanātan in the previous verse, āḻvār says he owns śrīvaikuṇṭam (spiritual realm) as well. Thus he owns both spiritual and material realms. Could there be anything more than these to draw the boundary of wealth!

**piṟai maruppil paingaṇ māl yānai** – the elephant has its tusks in the shape of the crescent and beauty in the eyes, typical of elephants. Being in rut, without knowing the truth, it entered the pond and got caught by the crocodile.

**padutuyaram kāttaḷitta** – [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) rescued the elephant gajēndran from the crocodile’s grip and took the flower that the elephant wanted to submit to him, on his divine feet, thus removing its sorrow \[that it could not submit the flower at the divine feet of emperumān\].

**padutuyaram** – it was a sorrow that even the elephant had to feel hurt

**sengaṇ māl kaṇḍāy teḷi** – isn’t [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) such that he clearly manifests his quality of vātsalyam (motherly affection) towards his followers through his eyes and is loving towards them! \[Oh heart\] know this well.

**sengaṇ māl** – the reddish eyes denote that he showers his vātsalyam on his followers.

**māl** – one who has deep love. Here it denotes the love with which he came to save gajēndran \[the elephant\] with his yellow dress worn from waist down and the crown on his head in utterly diśevelled condition. He is very simple towards his followers and isn’t he the one who always removes their hurdles!

**teḷi** – Oh my heart! Please know this. While [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) told [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/poigai-azhwar/) “*mā śuca:*” (don’t feel sad) \[śrī bhagavat gītā 18-66\], [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/poigai-azhwar/) tells his heart now “*mā śuca:*” In order to get emperumān’s grace there is no need to search out and go to his place. It is enough to keep the door to the mind open, just as people open the doors to their houses for inviting someone, and inform emperumān of our acceptance \[of him\]. If he doesn’t come by himself, is there any other way to get him who is incomparable? āḻvār says that out of habit, he called out to his mother “*ammā*” when in distress. emperumān has to come on his own on hearing our call.

Let us move on to the 30<sup>th</sup> pāsuram.

## 30. Telidaga ullattai

*teḷidāga uḷḷattaic cenniṟeei jñānat teḷidāga nanguṇarvār sindai eḷidāgat tāy nādu kanṟē pōl taṇḍuḻāyān adikkē pōy nādikkoḷḷum purindu*

**Пословный перевод:**

teḷidāga – to ensure that bewilderment is removed; sem niṟeei – establishing firmly; jñānattu – through knowledge; eḷidāga – with ease; nangu uṇarvār – knowing well; sindai – \[their\] minds; tāy nādu – \[among the (herd of) cattle\] attaining its mother; kanṟē pōl – like the calf; taṇ tuḻāyān adikkē – the divine feet of sarvēśvaran (supreme entity) who adorns a cool tuḷasi garland; purindu – with liking; pōy – seeking; nādik koḷḷum – will join together

**Перевод:**

**teḷidāga uḷḷattaic cennireei – establishing the heart such that it attains clarity. Removing the bewilderment in worldly matters and establishing the heart in the one entity (emperumān) that is of importance to it.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*āḻvār tells his heart not to get bewildered that there are many entities in the world that one can hold on to. Once we understand our connection with emperumān, the heart will understand him on its own and hold on to him. He says that for those who have faultless devotion towards him, it is impossible to wait patiently for him to come to them. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**sem nireei** – instead of controlling the sensory perceptions by trying to win over them, involving the sensory perceptions in matters related to [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) and win them over. In other words making the heart to have the ability to distinguish \[between good and bad\], to be free from sensual pursuits \[desires for worldly matters\] and to be humble.

**jñānatteḷidāga nangu uṇarvār sindai** – the knowledge that is born in such a purified heart is bhakti (devotion), says [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/poigai-azhwar/). With the aid of that bhakti, people will be able to feel him clearly through the methods ordained in śāstras (sacred texts) – through *śravanam* (hearing about [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/)) *mananam* (thinking about him) and *nididyāsanam* (meditating continuously about him). The heart of such a person would be able to see him clearly as mentioned in muṇḍaka upaniśat 2-2-9 “*tasmin druśtē parāvarē*” (if that supreme entity who is above everyone else is seen).

**teḷidāga** – to know clearly that “he is the Lord and I am his servitor”

**jñānattu eḷidāga** – once the heart realises this, it will be able to hold on to [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) just as it is holding on to all the worldly matters now.

**tāy nādu kanṟē pōl** – if a calf is let out towards a herd of cattle, it will avoid all other cows, identify where its mother is and go towards it.  In the same way, the heart will also avoid all other entities who appear superficially to be the ones to be held on to and hold on only to emperumān.

**taṇ tuḻāyān** – he is the one who is beautiful to look at with his tuḷasi garland. īsn’t he the one who is like a mother to everyone!

**adikkē** – just as the calf forgets all other parts of the cow’s body and reaches for the udder \[which sustains it\], the heart will also forget all other divine limbs of [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/)’s divine form and hold on only to his divine feet. It will hold on to the divine feet of the only entity who is fit to be attained.

**purindu** – this means “with desire”. Alternatively it could also be construed as “not minding about the intervening hurdles”

**tāy nādu kanṟē pōl**  is a simile in this context.

We shall take up the 31<sup>st</sup> pāsuram next.

## 31. Puriyoru kai parri

*puriyoru kai paṟṟi ōr ponnāḻiyēndi ariyuruvum āḷuruvumāgi eriuruva vaṇṇattān mārbidanda māladiyai allāl maṟ ṟeṇṇattāmō imai*

**Пословный перевод:**

puri – the conch pāncajanya, which is turned to the right side; oru kai paṟṟi – holding in one hand; ōr ponnāḻi ēndi – (in the other hand) holding the unique, beautiful sudarśana chakra (disc); ari uruvum āḷ uruvum āgi – being in the form of narasimham, which is a combination of human body and lion’s face; eri uruva vaṇṇattān – hiraṇya kaśyap’s form, which is like fire; mārbu idanda – tearing the heart; māl – biased towards his followers; adiyai allāl – other than his divine feet; maṟṟu – another entity; imai – even for a moment; eṇṇattān āmō – is it possible to think?

**Перевод:**

**puri oru kai paṟṟi – puri is the shortened form for valampuri which refers to emperumān’s conch pāncajanya which is turned towards the right side. He is having the conch in his left hand and the beautiful-to-look-at disc in his right. Since āḻvār is going to say later “eri uruva vaṇṇattān mārbidanda” (killing hiraṇya kaśyap) emperumān had to hold tightly the conch which was raring to fall on hiraṇya kaśyap and kill him. emperumān could not allow the conch to kill the demon in order to ensure that the boon that hiraṇya kaśyap had obtained that he would not be killed by any weapon, is not belied. The term paṟṟi refers to this state of emperumān’s holding the conch tightly so that it does not fall atop hiraṇya kaśyap. In the same way, the term ēndi used in combination with the cakram (disc) refers to the fact that emperumān could not engage the disc for killing hiraṇya kaśyap. Since hiraṇyan could not be killed by the disc, emperumān held it not as a weapon but as an aid to beauty. ponnāḻi refers to the disc being beautiful as well as being made of gold.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*āḻvār says that one should not think of any matter other than carrying out bhakti (devotion) towards such emperumān. He asks whether it is possible to think of anyone else when one considers emperumān’s nature of being totally devoted to his followers. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**ari uruvum āḷ uruvum āgi** – Since [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) could not kill hiraṇyan with the disc or conch, he had to take a form, suitable for killing the demon. Since hiraṇyan had been granted a boon that he will not be killed by human or animal, emperumān had to assume a form which is a combination of both – narasimha (man-lion interface)

**eri uruva vaṇṇattān mārbidanda** – [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) tore apart the chest of hiraṇyan who had a form which was like fire, and which was terrible to look at. Due to extreme anger, hiraṇyan’s  face was reddened and could not be looked at. Alternatively, one can consider the meaning for hiraṇyan, which is gold, and say that his form was like gold which had shone like fire.

**māl adiyai allāl** – if we consider the meaning for *māl* as affection, this verse would mean the divine feet of narasimha perumāḷ who incarnated to remove the enemy of his staunch child-devotee prahlāda and be loving towards his devotee. If we consider the meaning for *māl* as being proud, this term will refer to the divine feet of [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) who pervaded through all places in the context of the word *mahāviṣṇum* which is found in the *narasimha anuśtup mantra*.

**maṟṟu eṇṇatān āmō imai** – Is it proper to think of anything other than the divine feet of such an entity, even for a moment? [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/poigai-azhwar/) says that when one thinks of the divine feet of [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) who came to the rescue of the hapless child \[devotee\] when the child was considered as a foe by his own father, such divine feet will not allow one to think of anything else. ōr, as in the earlier few pāsurams, we can construe that āḻvār is telling his heart “Oh heart! could you think of anything other than such an entity even for a moment?”

We shall move on to the 32<sup>nd</sup> pāsuram.

## 32. Imaiyada kannal

*imaiyāda kaṇṇāl iruḷ agala nōkki amaiyāp poṟi pulangaḷ aindum namaiyāmal āgattu aṇaippār aṇaivarē āyira vāy nāgattu aṇaiyān nagar*

**Пословный перевод:**

imaiyāda kaṇṇāl – with the inner eye \[heart\] which does not have any contraction of knowledge; iruḷ agala – the darkness (of ignorance) to leave; nōkki – seeing the svarūpam (basic nature) of self as well as of emperumān; amaiyā – uncontrollable; poṟi pulangaḷ aindum – the five sensory organs such as eyes etc and the five matters such as sound, taste etc \[connected to the five sensory organs\]; namaiyāmal – even if not controlled; āgattu aṇaippār – those who think of emperumān \[when he, of his own volition, comes with causeless mercy\]; āyira vay nāgattu aṇaiyān – emperumān who is lying on the mattress of ādiśēśan with thousand hoods; nagar – his dwelling place śrīvaikuṇṭam; aṇaivarē – will attain

**Перевод:**

**iamiyāda kaṇṇāl – here, the word kaṇ \[eye\] refers to the mind. Mind is the eye which does not blink \[unlike the external eye\]. One should refer to the external eye when one is talking of removing the darkness outside. When one is referring to removing ignorance, one should talk about the inner eye or mind. śrī viṣṇu darmam 275-2 says “manasā tu viśuddēna”(he could be seen only with the pure mind). nammāḻvār, in periya tiruvandādi 28 says “katkaṇṇāl kāṇāda avvuruvai nenjennum utkaṇṇēl kāṇum uṇarndu” (emperumān cannot be seen with the external eyes but only with the internal eye).**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*āḻvār says that even if one does not have any connection with the means prescribed in śāstras (sacred texts) such as karma yōgam, jñāna yōgam and bhakti yōgam (being involved in carrying out deeds, having knowledge of the deeds carried out and having total devotion to  respectively), if one has the knowledge “emperumān is the Lord and I am the servitor” he would attain the high place of paramapadam (śrīvaikuṇṭam) mentioned in the sacred texts. Alternatively, one could construe that āḻvār is wondering whether those who do not follow the means prescribed in the śāstras can attain him. Let us enjoy the pāsuram and its meanings.*

**iruḷ agala nōkki** – thinking in such a way that *avidyā* (ignorance) etc would leave. Knowing self and [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) (true nature) after the darkness of ignorance is removed.

**amaiyāp poṟi pulangaḷ aindum** – the five sensory organs of ears, mouth, eyes, nose and body and the five matters of sound, taste, light, smell and feel, which cannot be controlled and which cannot be satiated. The word *poṟi* also means trap and in this case, it would convey the meaning that we get trapped by the five matters of sound, taste etc.

**namaiyāmal** – without control. In other words, the sensory organs such as ears etc reaching their targets.

**āgattu aṇaippār aṇaivarē** – even if they are not able to control their sensory organs, having the knowledge that they are subservient to [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/). Those who keep him in their hearts will reach paramapadam (śrīvaikuṇṭam). Alternatively, if one considers the word *aṇaivarē* as conveying the meaning – will such people reach emperumān? – the meaning will change as: can those who keep emperumān in their hearts but are unable to control their sensory organs,  reach paramapadam \[the answer is that they cannot\]. In the first case, all that is required is not to shun emperumān when he himself comes and showers his grace \[on the jīvātmā\].

**āyiravāy nāgattu aṇaiyān nagar** – it is said that the entity attained by muktātmā (liberated from material realm) is [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) who is lying on the 1000 hooded *anantāḻvān* (ādiśēśan). This same has been mentioned in *paryankavidyai* also. The service rendered by one *anantāḻvān* is the sum total of all the services by all the others in śrīvaikuṇṭam.

**nagar** – as [nammāḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/nammazhwar/) mentioned in [tiruvāimoḻi 2.8.4](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2016/05/thiruvaimozhi-2-8-4-pulan-aindhum/) “*nalamantam illadōr nādu*” (it is a unique place where there is no end to kainkaryam ). Instead of calling it as a *nādu* (country) [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/poigai-azhwar/) is referring to it as *nagar* (city). It is to emphasise that it is common to all to attain the country paramapadam.

We shall move on to the 33<sup>rd</sup> pāsuram.
