## 33. Nagaram arul purindu

*nagaram aruḷ purindu nān mugaṟkup pūmēl pagara maṟai payanda paṇban peyarinaiyē pundiyāl sindiyādu ōdi uru eṇṇum andiyāl ām pana angu en*

**Пословный перевод:**

pū mēl – on top of the lotus originated from navel; nān mugaṟku – for brahmā; nagaram aruḷ purindu – providing dwelling place; pagara – to teach (others); maṟai payanda – one who taught vēdas (sacred texts); paṇban – benefactor with simplicity; peyarinaiyē – \[his\] divine names; pundiyāl sindiyādu – instead of thinking with the mind; ōdi uru eṇṇum – reciting hymns and counting them; andiyāl – carrying out sandyāvandanam (a ritual carried out thrice every day in salutation of dēvatās); angu Am payan en – what is the benefit going to be derived?

**Перевод:**

**nānmugaṟkup pūmēl nagaram aruḷ purindu – emperumān gave to brahmā, who is the deity for carrying deeds such as creating the worlds etc, his lotus originated from navel as the dwelling place. For brahmā to carry out the huge task of creation he needs a place to stay. emperumān gave him the lotus flower itself on his navel, where brahmā was born, as his dwelling place. Just as kings would provide dwelling places near their palaces for key persons in their courts, emperumān also gave a dwelling place close to himself, viz. lotus originated from his navel itself, to brahmā.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*āḻvār says that instead of knowing that it is emperumān who is being vorśipped through all the karmas \[deeds\], there is no point in reciting some mantrams (ślōkams or hymns) through the mouth and counting the number of times that it is done with the help of hands, as being done in sandyāvandanam (salutation carried out thrice a day). Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**pagara maṟai payanda** – what is the purpose in providing only the dwelling place if knowledge is not provided? Hence, in order to enable brahmā to teach others, [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) taught brahmā all the vēdas; he has such simplicity. Just as indulging parents will keep their wards in their houses instead of sending them to schools, and arrange for teachers to come to their house and teach them, emperumān also kept brahmā near him and taught him vēdas. śvētāśvatara upaniśat 6-18 also says this “*yō brahmāṇam vidadādi pūrvam yō vai vēdāmśca prahiṇōti tasmai*” (he who first created brahmā and taught him vēdas).

**paṇban peyarinaiyē pundiyāl sindiyādu** – instead of thinking constantly with the mind, the divine names of the entity who provided dwelling place and knowledge.

**paṇban peyarinaiyē** – the divine names which reveal his auspicious qualities

**pundiyāl sindiyādu** – instead of thinking about the relationship between [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) and ourselves ([jivātmās](https://granthams.koyil.org/2013/03/06/thathva-thrayam-chith-who-am-i/)) and making him happy through our deeds

**ōdi uru eṇṇum andiyāl Am payan angu en** – what is the purpose in counting with the left hand and right hand and murmuring some hymns? When the heart is not thinking about [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/), is there any purpose in thinking about worldly pursuits and mouthing some hymns and counting them with hands? By doing such deeds, the only benefit will be moving away from emperumān. Here, *sandyāvandanam* has been mentioned only as an example. Any deed carried out without considering it as an offering in worship to emperumān is useless.

We shall move on to the 34<sup>th</sup> pāsuram.

## 34. Ennoruvar meyyenbar

*ennoruvar meyyenbar ēḻulaguṇḍu ālilaiyil munnorovanāya mugil vaṇṇā nin urugip pēyttāy mulai tandāḷ pēndilalāḷ pēramarkkaṇ āyttāy mulai tandavāṟu*

**Пословный перевод:**

ēḻu ulagu uṇḍu – keeping all the worlds in his stomach \[during deluge\]; Ala ilaiyil – on a banyan leaf; mun – once upon a time; oruvanāya – sleeping without a companion; mugil vaṇṇā – having the complexion of cloud; mulai tandāḷ – the one who suckled you; pēyt tāy – demon pūtanā, who came in the form of your mother; pērndilaḷ Al – was immobile, having died; pēr amar kaṇ – having eyes which appear to be fighting with each other; āyt tāy – yaśōdā, your covherd mother; nin urugi – with boundless love towards you; mulai tanda āṟu – the way she suckled you; oruvar – the incomparable sages; mey enbar – will say it is true; en – how is (this) possible?

**Перевод:**

**oruvar mey enbar – the incomparable great sages say that this is true. Sages such as vyāsa, parāśara et al who know this fully, say that there is no flattery in this, and that it is fully true.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*āḻvār says that yaśodāppirātti is the only one who enjoyed emperumān fully, being fully devoted to him. It appears that she got him without even thinking about him in her mind! This is indeed a wonder! ōr else, we can say that āḻvār is saying, how to say that this is the truth. Let us enjoy the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**ēḻu ulagu uṇḍu ālilaiyil mun oruvanāya mugil vaṇṇā** – At the time of deluge, you swallowed all the seven worlds and kept them in your stomach. At that time, when there was no mother such as yaśōdā to shower her affection on you, you were lying on a banyan leaf, with the form of a cloud that would remove sorrows. One can reason out the deeds of your devotees only if it is possible to reason out your deeds!

**nin urugi** – having affection towards you such that the sorrow that happened on that day is removed

**pēyt tāy mulai tandāḷ pērndilaḷ Al** – the demon pūtanā who came in the form of your mother, having suckled you, could not leave; she died there itself. In the same place where pūtanā was lying as a corpse…

**pēr amark kaṇ** – in the same battle field \[the place where pūtanā lay as a corpse appeared like a battlefield\]; in the same rattling place. ḍidn’t she come to create a danger for the entity who protects the worlds when they are in danger and removes the danger, and ended up in danger to herself? At that same time, your covherd mother yaśōdā, despite seeing that pūtanā who had come to suckle you is dead, without any fear, out of great affection for you, suckled you. What an incident is this! Those who know this fully, say that this is true. [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/poigai-azhwar/) wonders, how great is the motherhood of yaśōdā and her deep affection, unlike pūtanā who came in the guise of a mother! The affection that yaśōdā had for [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/), the supreme being, who ate all the worlds and lay down on a banyan leaf and who killed the demon pūtanā even as an infant and carried out such difficult to perform tasks, is indeed the real affection, says the āḻvār.

We shall consider the 35<sup>th</sup> pāsuram next.

## 35. Ariya anbil

*āṟiya anbil adiyār tam ārvattāl kūṟiya kuṟṟamāk koḷḷal nī tēṟi nediyōy adi adaidaṟkanṟē īraindu mudiyān padaitta muraṇ*

**Пословный перевод:**

āṟiya anbu il – without the bhakti which had simmered after reaching a crescendo; adiyār – followers; tam ārvattāl – with their friendliness; kūṟiya – the words spoken; nī kuṟṟamāk koḷḷal – you should not mistake; īr aindu mudiyān – the ten headed rāvaṇan; padaitta – carried out; muraṇ – enmity; tēṟi – after clarifying; nediyōy adi – the divine feet of emperumān; adaidaṟku anṟē – did it not become a reason for attaining!

**Перевод:**

**āṟiya anbil adiyār – followers who do not have the bhakti (devotion) which had simmered. just as the sea becomes calm after being turbulent. āṟiya anbu is showing affection in such a way that the person does not worry about himself but only wonders as to what will happen to emperumān. Showing affection (devotion) towards emperumān is the svarūpam (basic nature) of ātmā; hence not showing affection towards him is equivalent to displaying enmity towards him.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*āḻvār knows that compared to the love showered by yaśōdāp pirātti on emperumān, the love shown by people such as he himself would be equivalent to being inimical to him. Yet, he feels that there is no alternative to holding on to his divine feet and praying to him to forbear his false love. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**tam ārvattāl kūṟiya kuṟṟamāk koḷḷal** – whatever the devotees said out of their affection should not be considered as fault. Those words have been spoken by your followers so that they can sustain their affection towards you (if they had not spoken those words, they would have died perhaps). [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/poigai-azhwar/) says that even such words spoken so affectionately about [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) stand nowhere in comparison to the love shown by yaśōdā.

**kuṟṟamāK koḷḷal** – you should not consider such words as a fault ([āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/poigai-azhwar/) says that emperumān should forbear these words as the followers did not say them consciously and would not have sustained themselves had they not stated such words). [tirumaḻisai āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thirumazhisai-azhwar/) says in tiruccanda viruttam 111 “*seyda kuṟṟam naṟṟamāgavē koḷ*” (you should consider the fault as a good deed). āḻvār says that by saying that you should not consider them as fault, he is not saying that he has not committed any fault. It is just that he is praying that he should not consider them as faults. Once [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) says that he has forgiven his follower’s faults, the faults do not remain as faults anymore and become good deeds.

How is it possible to tell emperumān not to consider as faults when faults have been committed?….

**tēṟi nediyōy adi adaidaṟkanṟē īraindu mudiyān padaitta muraṇ** – the enmity that rāvaṇan had towards emperumān, over a period of time, became the reason for śiśupālan to reach emperumān’s divine feet! If we consider the meaning for *tērī* as being happy, it could be construed that [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/poigai-azhwar/) is requesting emperumān not to consider as faults whatever he did, out of happiness. If we consider the meaning for *tēṟi* as clarity, (after rāvaṇan was reborn as śiśupālan), in his last days when kriṣṇa stood in front of him with his divine cakram (disc), śiśupālan got the clarity in his mind as to who emperumān was and wondered at the sight “how beautiful is this!” (this itself became the reason for śiśupālan to attain liberation from materialistic realm and reach śrīvaikuṇṭam as he was able to envision [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) is his final moments).

**nediyōy** – [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/poigai-azhwar/) wonders whether it is possible to estimate the greatness of emperumān going by his affection towards those who had a little bit of affection towards him.

**īraindu mudiyān padaitta muraṇ nediyōy adi adaidaṟku anṟē** – [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/poigai-azhwar/) says “vasn’t it due to the devotion that rāvaṇan had towards you (later as śiśupālan) which made him attain your divine feet? Wasn’t it due to your mercy that he attained you? What prevents you from showering that affection towards me? Is my fault worse than his enmity? When you had treated his enmity as real affection, is there any barrier for you to consider my falsified affection as real affection towards you?”

We shall take up the 36<sup>th</sup> pāsuram next.

## 36. Muranai vali

*muraṇai vali tolaidaṟkām enṟē munnam taraṇi tanadu āgattānē  iraṇiyanaip puṇṇiranda vaḷ ugirāl pon āḻikkaiyāl nī maṇṇirandu koṇḍa vagai*

**Пословный перевод:**

munnam – in earlier times itself; daraṇi tanadu āgattānē – one who had presumed that the earth is his; iraṇiyanai – hiraṇya kaśyap; puṇ niranda – to cause hurt by breaking; vaḷ ugirāl – with sharp nails; pon – being resplendent; āḻi kaiyāl – with the divine hand holding cakkaram (divine disc); nī – you; maṇ irandu koṇḍa vagai – the way you begged for and obtained earth; muraṇai vali tolaidaṟkām enṟē – was it not to remove the ahankāram (egotism) and mamakāram (possessiveness) \[of samsāris such as ourselves\]!

**Перевод:**

**muraṇai vali tolaidaṟkām enṟē – In śrī rāmāyaṇam yudda kāṇḍam 36-11 it says “dvidābhajyēyamapi na namēyam tu kaśyacit” (even if I am cut into two, I will not worship anyone). In order to make such people who  do not have humility in them \[like rāvaṇa in the above mentioned ślōkam\], to be your own after removing their egotism, you were expectantly looking for them so that they would soften themselves and become your followers. This applies to be people like those mentioned in tiruvāimoḻi 2-9-9 “yānē endanadē enṟirundēn” (I remained as if I were everything and everything were mine).**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*āḻvār says that in order not to let others come to him and beg him not to take as truth whatever they had said in falsified affection, emperumān himself went to mahābali \[the King who had usurped the three worlds from indra, \[ the deity of celestial people\] and begged him. Thinking about this, he says “vasn’t this action carried out by you in order to attract the people of this world and to make them your own!” Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**munnam daraṇi tanadu āgattānē** – hiraṇyakaśyap who thought that your property, the earth, was his.

**tāne** – there was no need for someone to give it in writing to him. He thought so himself.

**puṇ niranda vaḷḷugirāl** – with the aid of sharp nails cut \[hiraṇyakaśyap into two\]. ōr, we could consider this as “with the help of sharp nails, tore the injured heart of hiraṇyakaśyap”.

**munnam daraṇi tanadu āgattānē** – going to the *yāgasālai* where mahābali, who thought that the whole world was his,  was carrying out rituals.

**pon āḻikkaiyāl nī maṇ irandu koṇḍa vagai** – the way you went begging for alms with the hand that held the disc which assists you in protecting the worlds

**muraṇai vali tolaidaṟkām enṟē** – you, who are the leader of all the worlds, went like a mendicant to the place where mahābali was carrying out the rituals. [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/poigai-azhwar/) says “Wasn’t this to make people admire your simplicity and melt down consequently!” Wasn’t it to destroy their ego which was like a fortress around them! While mahābali felt happy that he was giving away in alms to you, the worlds which were actually yours but were his as per his thinking, you also received them from him thinking as to why you should not receive it from him even if he thinks that the worlds are his. Just like janaka maharāja thought that sītāp pirātti ([mahālakśmi](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sri-mahalakshmi/) who never leaves you even for a moment) was his daughter and offered her hand in marriage to you, mahābali also thought that the worlds were his and was offering them to you. āḻvār says “was it not to attract and engage the people of this world towards you that you also took those worlds which were offered!”

We shall move on to the 37<sup>th</sup> pāsuram.

## 37. Vagai aru nun kelvi

*vagaiyaṟu nuṇ kēḷvi vāyvārgaḷ nāḷum pugai viḷakkum pūm punalum ēndi tisaitisaiyil vēdiyargaḷ senṟu iṟainjum vēnkatamē veṇ sangam ūdiya vāy māl uganda ūr*

**Пословный перевод:**

vagai aṟu – cutting off other kinds, such as other deities and other means; nuṇ kēḷvi vāy vārgaḷ – those who have subtle knowledge through hearing; vēdiyargaḷ – brāhmaṇas; nāḷum – everyday; pugai viḷakkum – dūpam (fragrant smoke, incense) and dīpam (lamp); pūm punalum – flower and water; ēndi – holding; tisai tisaiyil – from all directions; senṟu – going to (tirumalai); iṟainjum – worship; vēnkatamē – tiruvēnkatam!; veṇ sangam ūdiya vāy – having divine lips which blew the white coloured conch; māl – emperumān; uganda – relished; Ur – living place

**Перевод:**

**vagai aṟu nuṇ kēḷvi vāy vārgaḷ – those who have heard the fine aspects of vēdas (sacred texts) which give out many varieties of benefits. kriṣṇa says in śrī bhagavat gītā 2-41 “vyavasāyātmikā buddirēkēha kurunandana \| bahusākhā hyanantāśca buddayō’vyavasāyinām \|\|” (Oh arjuna! The knowledge about the truthfulness of ātmasvarūpam (basic nature of soul) will be focussed as it has as its goal only mōkśapalam (the result of attaining śrīvaikuṇṭam). The knowledge of those who are wavering will be having many types of offshoots and will be countless). vēdas reveal not only countless results but also results of very low value such as longevity, progeny etc. If the mind is not steady, such people will take dip in sacred rivers similar to others who do so for attaining lowly results; similarly they will also offer prayers like others who pray for lowly results. But, in the case of those who are desirous of mōkśam (liberation) their focus will be only on mōkśam. The knowledge of such people ……**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*āḻvār says “is it that emperumān came as a mendicant for one day? Has he not made himself as a mendicant for all times to come, for all people to come and attain him, telling people ‘will you not come and attain me?’ by remaining at tirumalai?” āḻvār says “he remains at tirumalai at all times, as if telling people, don’t regret that you were not there when I incarnated as vāmana, that my period was over and how do we enjoy him?” Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**vagaiyaṟu nuṇ kēḷvi** – will be such that it will avoid results which are of varied types.

**vāyvārgaḷ** – brāhmaṇas who have such knowledge

**nāḷum pugai viḷakkum pūm punalum ēndi** – Since they have heard \[vēdas\] well earlier, there will not be a need to hear again. They will get into the act of carrying out kainkaryam immediately. Since the entity which is to be attained and which helps in attaining it are both [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/), all the deeds which are carried out towards him will become kainkaryam. Since kainkaryam is very enjoyable, they will carry all the ingredients for vorśipping emperumān (such as sweet fragrance, lamp, flower, water etc) and reach tirumalai from various directions for the *brahmōtsavam* (a festival conducted over a period of 10 days, normally, to enable devotees to have *darśan* (to see emperumān) in different types of attires/decorations).

**vēdiyargaḷ** – who are these people who congregate for such festivals? They are those who have learnt the inner meanings of vēdam, the meaning of *praṇavam* (this is a single syllable word , composed of three letters, which signifies the servitorśip of [jīvātmās](https://granthams.koyil.org/2013/03/06/thathva-thrayam-chith-who-am-i/) to [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/)).

**senṟu iṟainjum** – isn’t it the objective of learning the meanings of *praṇavam* to reach tirumalai!

**veṇ sangam ūdiya vāy māl uganda Ur** – tiurmalai is the divyadēśam (sacred abode where [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) dwells) where kaṇṇapirān (kriṣṇa), who blew the pāncajanya in mahābhārata war, resides. When the enemies of arjuna were not felled by his arrows, the sound of pāncajanya which kriṣṇa created by blowing it in his mouth, felled them instantaneously at the same place. tirumalai is the abode where such kaṇṇapirān, who is completely biased towards his devotees, resides.

**veṇsangam ūdiya vāy māl uganda Ur tisai tisaiyil vēdiyargaḷ senṟu iṟainjum vēnkatamē** – since it is the abode where kaṇṇapirān, who destroyed the enemies, resides, and since it is the abode which protects his followers, his followers, who are learned in the vēdas, come to that abode from all directions with total desire.

We shall move on to the 38<sup>th</sup> pāsuram next.

## 38. Urum vari aravam

*ūrum vari aravam oṇ kuṟavar māḷ yānai pēra eṟinda peru maṇiyai kārudaiya min enṟu puṟṟadaiyum vēnkatamē mēla surar em ennum māladu idam*

**Пословный перевод:**

ūrum – that which crawls; vari aravam – snake with lines (on its body); oṇ kuravar – wise inhabitants (hunters) of tirumalai hill; māl yānai pēra – making the huge elephants, which are grazing in the fields, to leave; eṟinda – thrown (on those elephants); peru maṇiyai – huge carbuncle gems; kār udaiya min enṟu – thinking that it is the lightning amidst clouds; puṟṟu adiyum vēnkatamē – (fearing the thunder) such snakes entering their anthills in tiruvēnkatam; mēla surar – distinguished celestial beings \[nityasūris\]; em ennum – thinking that this is ours; māladu idam – is the place desired by emperumān as his divyadēśam

**Перевод:**

**ūrum – The snakes in tirumalai will crawl around, as they like. Without any specific target, they will keep crawling, as a pastime. sumitrā tells lakśmaṇa in śrī rāmāyaṇam ayōdyā kāṇḍam 40-5 “rāmē pramādam mā kārśi: putra bhrātari gaccati” (Oh dear child! By watching the gait of śrī rāma when he is valkling, don’t get involved in that and slip from protecting him). Similar to that beautiful gait of śri rāma, the beauty of the snakes crawling all over the hills engages āḻvār.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*Since emperumān came to dwell in tirumalai, with desire, āḻvār, on seeing this, describes with joy, all the entities which have connection with emperumān in that place. If a particular place is desired, all the things associated with that place also become desirable! Just as all the people in paramapadam (śrīvaikuṇṭam) are desired since paramapadam is a place to be attained. Let us enjoy the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**vari aravam** – The lines on the body of the snake are beautiful to look at. [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/poigai-azhwar/) involves with the snake as if it is a part of emeprumān’s divine body and it is the mattress *tiruvanantāḻvān* (ādiśēśan) for [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/).

**oṇ kuṟavar** – great inhabitants of the hill, the hunters. The greatness about these people is that they do not leave tirumalai and go down the hills. They think that leaving the hills will be termed as a great calumny to their heritage.  If someone says “such and such person’s great  grandfather’s great grandfather had left the hill once”, they will consider that as an affront on their clan. Without inviting such insults, these inhabitants have been residing in the hills. Unlike *muktars* who had once lived in the samsāram and then got liberated to reach paramapadam, these inhabitants, very much like the nityasūris who had always lived in paramapadam, always lived on the tirumalai hills (they are thus different from the brāhmaṇas (that we had seen in the previous pāsuram) who come to tirumalai hills from all directions to worship and carry out kainkaryam to [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) there).

**māl yānai pēra eṟinda perumaṇiyai** – elephants looking like mēru mountain, would be straying in the fields.

**pēra eṟinda** – In order to drive away the elephants, the hunters of the hills will throw carbuncles on them. Afraid of the radiance of the carbuncles, the elephants will get bewildered and run wildly, like mountains moving suddenly.

**perumaṇiyai** – the inhabitants throw the invaluable carbuncles as if they are throwing them on mountains.

**kār udaiya min enṟu puṟṟadaiyum** – When these carbuncles hit the elephants, it would appear as if lightning is streaking across dark clouds. Snakes will mistake this action as if it is the clouds which are moving with lightning flashing across them and in fearful anticipation of the subsequent thunder, they will enter their protective anthills. It would appear as if lightning carries the cloud as lightning flashes across the clouds! Thus, [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/poigai-azhwar/) engages deeply with the tirumalai hills, the snakes which keep moving around there, with the elephants which stray into the fields, with the inhabitants who drive away the elephants, with the carbuncle gems which are used to drive them, with the snakes which are afraid of this simulation and reach their anthills, without any difference, since all these entities are in tirumalai hills.

**meḷā surar emmennum māladidam** – the nityasūris who are permanent dwellers of paramapadam are the distinguished celestial people, unlike the dēvars (celestial persons in indra’s place of *svargam* / heaven) who are created. Each of these nityasūris will come to tirumalai hills claiming “this is ours; this is ours” and reside there with affection.

Even though the term “*ūrum variaravam*” appears to be in singular, the opinion is that all the snakes in tirumalai would act like this.

We shall move on to the 39<sup>th</sup> pāsuram next.

## 39. Idandadu bhumi

*idandadu bhūmi eduttadu kunṟam kadandadu kanjanai mun anja kidandaduvum nīrōda mā kadalē ninṟaduvum vēnkatamē pērōda vaṇṇar peridu*

**Пословный перевод:**

pēr ōdam vaṇṇan – emperumān having the complexion of a large ocean; mun – in earlier times; idandadu – (in varāha form) dug out; bhūmi – was the earth; eduttadu – held as umbrella; kunṟam – was the gōvardana hill; anja kadandadu – destroyed through fear; kanjanai – was (king) kamsa; kidandaduvum – reclined; nīr ōdam mā kadal – ocean which is full of water and which keeps throwing up waves; peridu ninṟaduvum – stood always; vēnkatamē – only at tiruvēnkatam

**Перевод:**

**idandadu bhūmi – when the world drowned during deluge, emperumān took the form of varāha (wild boar) and dug out the earth.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*Is there any place which is not his? Is there any activity that he has not carried out for his followers? That being the case, what is special about tirumalai? āḻvār says that unlike other places, emperumān has come to this place with lot of affection and has made it his permanent dwelling place and this speciality is exclusive to tirumalai. Let us first go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**eduttadu kunṟam** – when indra (lord of celestial people) became angry \[on being denied food\] and created hailstorm, [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) lifted the gōvardana hill like an umbrella and stopped the hailstorm, thus protecting the covherds.

**kadandadu kanjanai mun anja** – in earlier times when kamsa tried to destroy kriṣṇa, he made kamsa to shiver on merely seeing him and killed him.

**kidandaduvum nīr ōdam mā kadalē** – as the origin of these incarnations, [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) reclined on *tiruppāṟkadal* (the milky ocean) which has large amount of liquid and throws up waves.

During each of these incarnations, very few people only were able to see him and enjoy him. Unlike that, he came to tiruvēnkatam to be seen and enjoyed by everyone at all the times.

**pērōda vaṇṇar peridu ninṟaduvum vēnkatamē** – [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) who has the complexion of large ocean with agitating waves, does not return to *paramapadam* after his incarnations but goes to tiruvēnkatam to take permanent residence, with full desire. Just as he himself has divined in [śrī bhagavat gītā 4-8](http://githa.koyil.org/index.php/4-8/) “*paritrāṇāya sādūnām vināśāya ca duśkrutām, dharma samstāpanārttāya sambhavāmi yugē yugē*” (in every yugam, I incarnate myself in order to protect the good people, destroy the bad people and to establish righteousness), his incarnations are only to remove the dangers that could occur to his followers. But is it not true that tiruvēnkatam is such that he comes ahead and stays there so that it will prevent dangers that would set in, in future! There is not a deed that emperumān has not carried out for our sake, with immeasurable love. But, we \[the samsāris\] do not have real involvement in anything. While professing that we enjoy him, we relish food. But we do not have total involvement even in that. We observe fast on some days because we do not like that food. We like women. But on occasions, we forget them too and play games like *caturangam* (modern day chess) or sleep, as a pastime. We do not have real love towards anything. This is a major difference between him and us.

**idandadu bhūmi eduttadu kunṟam kadandadu kanjanai mun anja** – he overcame the danger that befell earth during deluge; he removed the danger due to hailstorm; he removed the danger due to kamśan too. He reclined on his tirupāṟkadal after completing incarnations so that he could rest awhile and be alone for some time. We can see the beauty of his digging out bhūmi which got drowned during deluge; the beauty when he lifted  the gōvardana hill as an umbrella; the beauty which came as a victory upon destroying kamśan; the beauty of his reclining in tirupāṟkadal – all these beauty can be seen together at tiruvēnkata hills, in the divine form of tiruvēnkatavan.

We shall next take up the 40<sup>th</sup> pāsuram.

## 40. Peru vil pagali

*peruvil pagaḻik kuṟavar kaiccendī veruvip punam tuṟanda vēḻam iruvisumbil mīn vīḻak kaṇḍu anjum vēnkatamē mēl asurar kōn vīḻak kaṇḍu ugandān kunṟu*

**Пословный перевод:**

peru vil – with a large bow; pagaḻi – and arrows; kuṟavar – hunters’; kai – held in the hand; sem tī – to the red hot fire; veruvi – being fearful; punam tuṟanda – leaving the fields; vēḻam – elephant; iru visumbil – from the expansive sky; mīn vīḻa – with the shooting star falling; kaṇḍu – seeing that; anjum – fearful place (that it is the burning firewood thrown by the hunters); vēnkatamē – Oh tirumalai!; mēl – in earlier time; asurar kōn vīḻa – hiraṇyan, the leader of demons, falling down; kaṇḍu – seeing (that); ugandān – one who felt joyful; kunṟu – is tirumalai

**Перевод:**

**peru vil – the hunters hold a large bow like śrī rāma, as mentioned in śrī rāmāyaṇam yudda kāṇḍam 100-12 “sa dadarśa tatō rāmam tiśtantamaparājitam \| āligantamivākāśam avaśtabhya mahad tanu: \|\|” (rāvaṇa saw śrī rāma, who is unconquered and who is holding a large bow that reaches up to the sky).**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*āḻvār says that the purpose of dwelling permanently at tirumalai, as thought by emperumān, is to destroy the enemies of followers and be joyful. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**pagaḻi** – [āḻvārs](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/azhwars/) and [ācāryars](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/full-list/) take heart from the sharp arrows of śrī rāma and get rid of their fear as mentioned by [tirumaḻisai āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thirumazhisai-azhwar/) in nānmugan tiruvandādi 8 “*ilai tuṇai maṟṟu en nenjē kūr amban allāl*” (Oh my heart! There is none to support other than the one with sharp arrows). In the same way, this [āḻvār \[poygai āḻvār\]](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/poigai-azhwar/) also talks about the sharpness of the hunters’ arrows. The way the hunters of tirumalai are holding the bow is similar to the way śrī rāma and lakśmaṇa are holding! The hunters who are holding the bow and sharp arrows in one hand are holding torches \[burning firewood\] in the other, in order to remove the darkness, while going to their fields for protecting them.

**veruvip punam tuṟanda vēḻam** – elephants which foray into the fields for food, on looking at the torches and the bow / sharp arrows that the hunters are holding, will become fearful and will run away, thinking that they should never ever come to these fields.

**iru visumbil mīn vīḻak kaṇḍu anjum vēnkatamē** – even as the elephants are running away from the fields fearing the torches, they see shooting starts falling from the expansive sky towards the earth. Thinking that these are the torches being thrown at them by the hunters, they stand transfixed, unable to go further. It appears to them that it was better in the fields as the torches would have been brought by the hunters for some other purpose whereas here, the shooting stars \[which they think are torches\] are being aimed at them by the hunters and they become more fearful.

**mēl asurar kōn vīḻak kaṇḍu ugandān kunṟu** – [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/poigai-azhwar/), on seeing the fear that the elephants have is reminded of the fear that hiraṇyan had on seeing narasimha. tirumalai is the place that [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) came to, after destroying hiraṇyan and feeling happy that he was able to destroy the enemy of his child devotee prahlāda. He stands in tirumalai in order to eliminate enemies of his followers in future too.

Let us consider the 41<sup>st</sup> pāsuram next.

## 41. Kunru anaiya

*kunṟu anaiya kuṟṟam seyyinum guṇam koḷḷum inṟu mudalāga en nenjē enṟum puṟan uraiyē āyinum pon āḻik kaiyān tirṟn uraiyē sindittu iru*

**Пословный перевод:**

en – my; nenjē – heart!; inṟu mudalāga – starting from now; enṟum – at all times; puṟan uraiyē āyinum – even if it were spoken as a lip service; pon āḻikk kaiyān tiṟan uraiyē – matters related to emperumān who is holding the beautiful disc on his hand; sindittu iru – keep meditating; kunṟu – looking like a hill; kuṟṟam seyyinum – if sins are committed; guṇam koḷḷum – (without considering such sins, emperumān) will only look at the good deeds

**Перевод:**

**kunṟu anaiya kuṟṟam seyyinum – Just as it says in viṣṇu darmam “mēru mantara mātrō’pi rāśi: pāpasya karmaṇa: kēśavam vaidyamāsātya durvyātiriva nacyati ” (just as a serious disease disappears on seeing a good medical practitioner, the bundles of sins of a person, even if they are as much as mēru mountain, will disappear once the person attains kēśavan), even if one had done sins to the extent of a mountain.  Due to their greatness and unshakable nature, sins and mountains are comparable and hence āḻvār compares sins to mountains. Sins cannot be got rid of any means other than by experiencing them. If emperumān says “I tolerate your sins” all the sins will disappear.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*āḻvār is fearful in his heart as to whether we, who are habituated to carrying out misdeeds, can go near him even if we have a strong relationship with him and he is capable of eliminating our enemies. He himself gives the reconciliation, saying “he has come here only to forgive us of our faults. There is nothing else that you need to do to obtain his grace. Just create an involvement towards him and try to enjoy him. That alone will enable you to attain his grace” Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**guṇam koḷḷum** – emperumān considers even a little bit of something good among the bad deeds carried out by the person unknown to himself, as a mountain. Alternatively, emperumān forgets sins which are the size of a mountain and considers only the good done by the person even if it is the size of a mustard. Just as [periyāḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/periyazhwar/) says in periyāḻvār tirumoḻi 4-9-2 “*seydārēl nanṟu seydār*” (if a person carries out a faulty deed thinking that emperumān is there to tolerate it, emperumān would say that he had done only good), emperumān considers even a fault as a good deed. [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) is always permanent. [jīvātmā](https://granthams.koyil.org/2013/03/06/thathva-thrayam-chith-who-am-i/) is also permanent. And emperumān is the one who considers even a bad deed as a good one. If this indeed were true, why did the jīvātmā lose this benefit all along?

**inṟu mudalāga** – [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/poigai-azhwar/) says that this benefit will accrue from the day jīvātmā realises that [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) has this quality in him \[of considering the fault as good deed\].

**en nenjē** – Oh my heart, which follows my way instead of taking me in your way! Is it possible to talk wholeheartedly about his qualities? ….

**enṟum puṟan uraiyē āyinum** – even if meaningless gibberish were spoken in praise of emperumān instead of wholeheartedly praising him. Has it not been said “*vrutaiva bhavatō yātā bhūyasi janmasandati tasyāmanyatamam janma sancitya śaraṇam vraja* ” (your series of births had gone wasted. Choose a distinguished birth from among them and surrender to emperumān)!

**ponnāḻikkkaiyān** – one who holds the beautiful sudarśana cakkaram (disc). nenjē! You have seen [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) with his disc and hand. After seeing this, is it possible to forget him? His beauty is such that even if you were involved with him in some way (without being in a wholehearted manner) the involvement is taken by him as if it were done wholeheartedly.

**tiṟan uraiyē sindittiru** – keep thinking about him and be without any fear. Alternatively, we can consider “*puṟan uraiyē āyinum*”to mean “Oh heart! Even though you were involved with other matters all along, now do what I had told you”. The powerful message from the one who is holding the disc \[[emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/)\] is his divine words in śrī bhagavat gītā 18-66 “*sarva darmān parityajya māmēkam śaraṇam vraja, aham tvā sarva pāpēbhyō mōkśayiśyāmi mā suca:*” (leave aside all righteous paths, as means to attain me, and surrender only to me. I shall release you from all sins. Do not worry).

**sindittiru** – keep thinking about that word. Just as arjuna says in śrī bhagavat gītā 18-73 “*stitōsmi gata sandēha*” (I am standing devoid of doubt), remain happy.

**tiṟan urai** – In śrī rāmāyaṇam śrī rāma says “*mitrabhāvēna samprāptam natyajēyam katancana, dōśō yadyapi tasya syāt satāmētadagarhitam*” (I will never give up a person even if he comes in the garb of a friend. Even if faults are there in him, let them be. This is not something to be condemned by good persons). [tiruvāimoḻi 2-7-3](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2016/04/thiruvaimozhi-2-7-3-madhavan/) says “*mādavan enṟadē koṇḍu ennai ini ippāṟpattadu yādu avangaḷum  sērkodēn enṟu ennuḷ pugundirundu tīdu avam kedukkum amudam* ” (just because I said *mādavan* \[consort of mahālakśmi\] *he entered my heart with a vow that henceforth he will not allow any fault to accrue to me. He is the most enjoyable entity and removed all my varied sins*).

Let us take up the 42<sup>nd</sup> pāsuram next.

adiyēn kriṣṇa ramanuja dāsan

## 42. Tirumagalum manmagalum

*tirumagaḷum maṇmagaḷum āymagaḷum sērndāl tirumagatkē tīrndavāṟu enkol tirumagaḷ mēl pāl ōdam sindap pada nāgaṇaik kidanda mālōda vaṇṇar manam*

**Пословный перевод:**

pāl ōdam sinda – droplets to fall on the milky ocean; padam nāgaṇaikkidanda – reclining on the mattress of tiruvandāḻvān (ādiśēśan) with hoods; māl ōdam vaṇṇar – emperumān with the complexion of large ocean; tirumagaḷ mēl manam – divine mind which is (full of love) on tirumagaḷ (śrī mahālakśmi); tirumagaḷum maṇmagaḷum āymagaḷum sērndāl – if śrīdēvi, bhūdēvi and neeḷā dēvi are together; tirumagatkē tīrnda āṟu enkol – what a surprise that it is totally involved with śrī dēvi!

**Перевод:**

**anantāḻvān, one of our ācaryars (a disciple of bhagavad rāmānuja) would explain the meaning of this pāsuram as: When tirumagaḷ (śrī mahālakśmi), maṇmagal (bhūdēvi) and āymagal (neeḷādēvi) get together, emperumān with the complexion of a large ocean, who is reclining on the mattress of ādiśēśan, would be such that he is (a) totally dedicated to mahālakśmi,  (b) totally dedicated to bhūdēvi and (c) totally dedicated to neeḷā dēvi. In other words, when he is with each of his consorts, it would appear that he is totally involved with and dedicated to that consort. anantāḻvān would say, how wondrous it is that he is involved equally with all the three consorts!**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*What is the need for emperumān to put up with our faults? āḻvār says that since the pirāttimārs (his consorts), who make him tolerate, are beside him, he has necessity to do this. When pirātti says, \[as sītāp pirātti said\] in śrī rāmāyaṇam yudda kāṇḍam 116-44 “pāpānām vā śubhānām vā vadārhāṇām plavangama \| kāryam karuṇamāryēṇa na kaścinnāparādyati \|\|” (Oh monkey! Whether they are sinners or virtuous persons, mercy should be shown on those who are fit even to be killed. There is none who has not committed a sin), is there any impediment for emperumān to say “dōśōyadyapi tasa syāt” (even if he has faults, let them be)? Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings.*

[**parāśara bhatta**](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/parasara-bhattar/)[r](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/parasara-bhattar/) (divine son of [kūrattāḻvān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/kuraththazhwan/)) would give a different meaning, it is said.

**pāl ōdam sinda** – in such a way that tiny droplets of *tiruppāṟkadal* (milky ocean) would come and touch [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/)’s divine feet as if to massage

**pada nāgaṇaik kidanda** – since emperumān is reclining on him, ādiśēśan is spreading his hoods in ecstasy. Such emperumān who is …

**māl ōda vaṇṇar** – having the complexion of a large ocean which throws waves

**tirumagaḷ mēl manam** – his divine mind which is affectionate towards periya pirātti ([śrī mahālakśmi](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sri-mahalakshmi/))

**tirumagaḷum maṇmagaḷum āymagaḷum sērndāl tirumagatkē tīrndavāṟu** – when he is with śrī mahālakśmi, bhūdēvi and neeḷādēvi, his mind will be totally engaged with śrī mahālakśmi.

In śrīguṇaratnakōśam, [śrī parāśara bhattar](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/parasara-bhattar/) has said mercifully while talking about the other consorts of [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) except [śrī mahālakśmi](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sri-mahalakshmi/) that they will be like “*puśpāngarāgais samam*” (similar to flower and sandalwood paste). In other words, when emperumān is with śrī mahālakśmi, the other consorts will be like aids such as the moon, sandalwood paste, flower, gentle breeze etc in enhancing his delight. The other consorts of emperumān are like the divine limbs of śrī mahālakśmi. Hence when emperumān is with śrī mahālakśmi, she would think that other consorts of emperumān are also with her in partaking the experience. In samsāram, when a man has more than one wife, there will be constant fight among his wives as each wife feels that she has not had enough of her husband. In the case of emperumān, since the experience is like a great flood, even if his consorts come together to experience him, their experience will not be sated. Hence there is no competition or jealousy amongst them.

We shall take up the 43<sup>rd</sup> pāsuram next.

## 43. Manamasu tirum

*manamāsu tīrum aruvinaiyum sārā danamāya tane kaikūdum punamēya pūntuḻāyān adikkē pōdodu nīr ēndi tām toḻā niṟpār tamar*

**Пословный перевод:**

punam mēya pūm tuḻāyān – emperumān who is adorned with tuḷasi garland which blossoms as if it is on its own land \[even when it is on emperumān’s divine form\]; adikkē – to his divine feet; pōdodu nīr ēndi – taking flower and sacred water; tamar – his followers; tām – themselves; toḻā niṟpar – will keep vorśipping; manammāsu tīrum – all the faults in their minds such as ignorance etc will exit; aru vinaiyum sārā – sins which cannot be got rid of, will not come anywhere nearby; danamāya – the wealth of kainkaryam (service to emperumān); tāne kaikūdum – will come on its own

**Перевод:**

**manamāsu tīrum – (if emperumān is vorśipped) all the faults in the mind would go away. Hurdles such as ignorance etc which prevent thinking about emperumān would leave.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*āḻvār says that since emperumān’s consorts are always with him, it is not necessary that to attain him we have to get rid of all our sins. Even as we carry out the deeds that we like, our sins will disappear on their own. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**aruvinaiyum sārā** – It is not enough if only the middle portion of the weed is thrown out. Even the root has to be removed as otherwise it will grow again. Hence, deeds such as puṇya (virtue) and pāpa (sin) which are the roots for ignorance, will not come anywhere near \[the person who worships [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/)\].

**danamāya tane kaikūdum** – once the faults leave the mind and puṇya and pāpa deeds which are the reasons for samsāram (being born in materialistic realm) also leave, the wealth for such a person namely *jñāna* (knowledge about emperumān) and *bhakti* (devotion towards emperumān) which are the harbingers of kainkaryam (service) would come to him on their own. Did not vālmīki call vibhīśaṇa \[younger brother of demon rāvaṇa\] as a wealthy person in śrī rāmāyaṇam yudda kāṇḍam 16-17 “*antarikśagata: śrīmān*” (vibhīśaṇa, who went through the path of sky and who is wealthy)! This was after he had left all his wealth behind in lankā and gone with empty hands to śrī rāma. samam (control over mind) and damam (control over the five senses) which are the components of *bhakti* will also come.

**punamēya pūnduḻāyān adikkē** – in order to obtain whatever has been said so far, one has to consume this margosa decoction! (the opinion here is that to get the result, the means is also sweet) \[margosa decoction is considered to be very good for health and is hence deemed as sweet\].

**punam mēya pūm tuḻāyān adikkē** – tuḷasi will remain fresh on [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/)’s divine locks just as it remains fresh in its own ground! For those followers who take flowers etc to lay at the divine feet of emperumān, all the faults and bad deeds in the mind will disappear. When the follower does what he is expected to do, vouldn’t emperumān do what he is supposed to do? It is the *svarūpam* (basic nature) of the follower to carry out kainkaryam to emperumān and it is emperumān’s *svarūpam* to accept the kainkaryam.

We shall move on to the 44<sup>th</sup> pāsuram.

## 44. Tamar ugandadu

*tamar ugandadu evvuruvam avvuruvam tānē tamar ugandadu eppēr maṟṟu appēr tamar ugandu evvaṇṇam sidittu imayādu irupparē avvaṇṇam āḻiyānām*

**Пословный перевод:**

āḻiyān – emperumān who holds the cakkara (divine disc) in his hand; tamar – devotees; ugandadu evvuruvam – whichever form they desire \[for emperumān\]; avvuruvam tānē – he attains that form; maṟṟu – moreover; tamar ugandadu eppēr – whichever divine name they desire \[for emperumān\]; appēr Am – emperumān attains that name; tamar – devotees; ugandu – with desire; evvaṇṇam sindittu – in whichever way they think; imaiyādu irupparē – meditating without a break; avvaṇṇam Am – he transforms himself that way

**Перевод:**

**tamar ugandadu evvuruvam avvuruvam tāne – whichever divine form his followers assume for him, emperumān adopts that form itself as his, willingly. īsn’t he taking up various forms in his incarnations for the sake of his followers! śrī rāma tells the celestial persons in śrī rāmāyaṇam udda kāṇḍam 120-11 “ātmānam mānuśam manyē rāmam daśaratātmajam” (I am happy in considering myself as a human being, as the son of daśarata). In śrī bhagavat gītā 4-11 emperumān himself says “yē yatā mām prapadyantē tāmstataiva bhajāmyaham” (in whichever form my followers think of me and attain me, I attain them in arcā (idol) form taking the same form). He says “I do not have friend or enemy. There is no compulsion that they should attain me only in this method. I attain them in the status that they are in, in the way that they hold me” śrī viṣṇu darmam 103-16 says “surūpām pratimām viṣṇō: prasannavadanēkśaṇām, krutv(A)ātmāna: prītikarīm suvarṇarajatādibhi: ” (let him worship viṣṇu as an image made of materials such as gold, silver etc with a beautiful form, bright face, eyes etc which he desires). Thus it is mentioned \[in several scriptures\] that emperumān’s divine form can be made with any material. Since some people \[contemporary with him\] only enjoyed when he took incarnations and others who came subsequently lost out on him, emperumān keeping that loss in his heart, comes down in images or shapes that these \[later-day\] followers desire in whichever material they choose and worship him.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*āḻvār says that when these people \[jīvātmās\] carry out kainkaryam in keeping with their nature, emperumān also shows himself to them appropriately. Let us enjoy the pāsuram and its meanings, first:*

Once when [emperumānār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/emperumanar/) (bhagavat rāmānujar) had gone out \[at śrīrangam\] for bikśā (alms), little children who were playing on the street, scrawled a few lines on the sand with their legs and told him “here is your perumāḷ \[[emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/)\]” emperumānār immediately kept the vessel that he was having in his hands on the sand and did a sāśtānga praṇāmam (prostrated) to that figure on the sand (this would imply that whatever the children scrawled as emperumān’s image, emperumānār also accepted that it is his image).

**tamar ugandadu eppēr maṟṟu appēr** – it is not only form, but even in the case of his divine names, he accepts whatever names his followers give him. Leaving aside names such as *nārāyaṇa* etc which are exclusive for him, he also accepts names given by his followers.

In a place called kanjanūr, there was a follower by name **vaṇṭhuvaraip perumāḷ**. He called his *tiruvārādanap perumāḷ* (the idol that he vorśipped everyday) by the divine name *sadangai aḻagiyār* (beautiful person with anklets). [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/), to show him how much he liked this name, enacted a play. One day, he showed himself with anklets in the follower’s dream and started dancing. His follower asked him “who are you?” emperumān responded “I am *sadangai aḻagiyār*”

[**nanjīyar**](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/nanjiyar/) had given the divine name “*āyar dēvu***”** to  his tiruvārādanap perumāḷ.  emperumān, to show him that he relishes that name, appeared in the dream of [engaḷāḻvān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/engalazhwan/) and demanded blue berries. When engaḷāḻvān wanted to know as to who he was, he replied “I am nanjīyar’s son āyar dēvu” Not only did he mention the name that nanjīyar gave him, but he also played by that name \[āyar dēvu refers to kriṣṇa, who was the lord of covherds and liked blue berries very much\]. Wondering at this, engaḷāḻvān told nanjīyar “your son is not allowing me to sleep. He keeps pestering to be given this and that”.

**tamar ugandu evvaṇṇam sindittu imaiyādu irupparē avvaṇṇam āḻiyānām** – he remains with the same qualities and activities that his followers think of and keep meditating on. The one who creates all the worlds and who controls everything, becomes the form, name etc which his followers desire and thus becomes created and controlled. Just as ādiśēśan makes himself the way [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) desires of him, as said in the 52<sup>nd</sup> pāsuram of this tiruvandādi “*senṟāl kudaiyām irundāl singādanamām ninṟāl maravadiyām*” (ādiśēśan becomes emperumān’s umbrella when he goes out, his throne while sitting and his sandals when standing), emperumān also makes himself as the way his followers desire of him. While the followers carryout the tasks because of their *śēśatvam* (being servitor) emperumān does all the things for the followers because of his love for them.

**āḻiyān** – though only sudarśana cakkara (the divine disc) has been mentioned, it is implied that the above would hold good for all nityasūris (permanent dwellers of paramapadam). [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/poigai-azhwar/) says that while there are many like sudarśana cakkara to carry out tasks based on his thoughts, [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) himself leaves them all and adapts in the way that his followers think of him and acts accordingly. [nammāḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/nammazhwar/) says in [tiruvāimoḻi 3-6-9](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2016/09/thiruvaimozhi-3-6-9-thanjam-agiya/) “*nenjināl ninaippān evan avanāgum nīḷkadal vaṇṇanē*” (emperumān who has the complexion of a large ocean, transforms himself into whatever his followers think of him). It is not only in form and name, but also in terms of qualities, activities etc that he goes by what his followers desire of him.

We shall take up the 45<sup>th</sup> pāsuram next.

## 45. Ame amararkku

*āmē amararkku aṟiyavadu niṟka nāmē aṟigiṟpōm nannenjē  pūmēya mādavattōn tāḷ paṇinda vāḷarakkan nīṇmudiyai pādamattāl eṇṇinān paṇbu*

**Пословный перевод:**

nal nenjē – Oh \[my\] good heart!; pū mēya mādavattōn – brahmā, who has done lot of penance and who resides in the lotus growing out of \[emperumān’s\] navel; tāḷ paṇinda – one who came and vorśipped at his feet; vāḷ arakkan – demoniacal rāvaṇan; nīḷ mudiyai – extended (ten) heads; pādam attāl eṇṇinān – counted with his divine feet; paṇbu – auspicious qualities; amararkku aṟiya āmē – are they such that celestial people can know?; adu niṟka – moreover; nāmē aṟigiṟpōm – only we (who are blessed by his causeless mercy) know

**Перевод:**

**āmē amararkku aṟiya – Is it possible for dēvas (celestial people) such as brahmā, rudra et al to know emperumān? He is such simple, with no distinction between his simplicity and greatness, that they can not know him.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*The simplicity of emperumān is such that it can be known only to his followers to whom he decides to manifest and not even to dēvatās (deities) such as brahmā et al. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**adu niṟka** – moreover

**nāmē aṟigiṟpōm** – There are two interpretations: (1) can we, who are ignorant, know him when even very knowledgeable persons such as brahmā et al are unable to know him? (2) While persons such as brahmā et al do not know him, aren’t we going to know him! When he shows himself up to us \[his devotees\] can we not know him!

**nannenjē** – īsn’t the reason for brahmā et al not to know him, their hearts which do not obey them! But my heart! Is there any shortcoming for me when I have you who obeys me?

**pū mēya mādavattōn tāḷ paṇinda vāḷarakkan nīṇ mudiyai pādam attāl eṇṇinān** – [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/poigai-azhwar/) gives out the reason for their not knowing [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/). When rāvaṇa was begetting all his boons from brahmā, who has as his residence the lotus coming out of emperumān’s navel, and who has carried out lot of penance, emperumān came in the form of a child and lay on the lap of brahmā. He counted the ten heads of rāvaṇa with his divine feet and warned brahmā “do not grant all the boons that he is asking for. Know that the person who has come here is rāvaṇan. If you grant him all the boons, you will go the wrong path” and disappeared. He pointed out to brahmā “these are heads which will be severed. If you add strength to these through your boons, you will also be in deep trouble”

**pādam attāl eṇṇinān** – he showed the heads by scrawling on them with his enjoyable divine feet. Will brahmā, who does not know that it is not going to benefit him any way but is only going to cause him trouble, know [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/)’s qualities?

We shall take up the 46<sup>th</sup> pāsuram next.

## 46. Pan purinda

*paṇ purinda nānmaṟaiyōn sennip paliyēṟṟa veṇpurinūl mārban vinai tīra puṇ purinda āgattān tāḷ paṇivār kaṇḍīr amarar tam bhōgattāl bhūmi āḷvār*

**Пословный перевод:**

paṇ purinda nān maṟaiyōn – brahmā, who was taught the four vēdas, along with their \[musical\] tunes; senni – on the head; pali yēṟṟa – one who took alms; veṇ puri nūl mārban – rudra who has white coloured yajñōpavīdam (sacred thread) worn across the chest; vinai tīra – to remove the brahmahatti curse (due to harming a brāhmaṇa; in this case brahmā); puṇ purinda āgattān – emperumān who harmed his divine heart; tāḷ – divine feet; paṇivār kaṇḍīr – see those who worship; amarar tam bhōgattāl – along with enjoyment of nityasūris; bhūmi āḷvār – will rule this earth too

**Перевод:**

**paṇ purinda nān maṟaiyōn sennip pali yēṟṟa – Siva had severed one of the heads of brahmā, who had learnt the vēdas with all the intonations. He severed the head of the one who was not only his father, but father of all the people in the world. Siva himself says in mātsya purāṇam 1-8-2 “vāmānguśta nakāgrēṇa cinnam tasya śirō mayā” (that brahmā’s head was severed by the edge of the nail on my left hand). brahmā who was angered by this cursed him saying “kapālee tvam bhaviśyasi” (you will be with the skull) due to which the skull got stuck well with Siva’s hand. Since brahmā had cursed him saying that he has to beg till the skull becomes full, Siva went on begging for a very long period with that skull to rid himself of the curse.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*āḻvār says in this pāsuram that emperumān not only does good to these people \[brahmā et al\] by being in the forefront, but when they leave him of their own accord and indulge in deeds which are harmful to them, he removes their faults and protects them. In the earlier pāsuram brahmā’s ignorance was mentioned. In this pāsuram, Siva’s ignorance is highlighted. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**veṇ puri nūl mārban vinai tīra** – In order to atone for his bad deed, Siva wore a white coloured sacred thread and stood in front of [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) with the skull. Just as those who go about life as they like, indulging in sinful deeds, start taking dip in holy rivers and oceans once the effects of the sins become severe and trouble them, Siva also came in front of emperumān with lot of humility. It is not only Siva who had severed his father’s head. pārvati, his consort, was also responsible for the death of her father dakśa. Siva’s follower saṇḍēśvaran had also severed the foot of his father (all these persons are disrespectful of their fathers). It is opined here that since the word “*vinai tīra*” has been used it is clear that Siva is subordinated to the results of bad deeds and he is incapable of removing the effects of his sins. Thus there is no doubt that he is not īśvaran (supreme lord).

**puṇ purinda āgattān** – After seeing Siva, who thinks highly of himself, going around with the skull begging for alms, [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/)’s divine mind was hurt. Alternatively we can consider the meaning of puṇ purinda āgattān as “*alangal mārvil vāsanīr koduttavan*” spoken mercifully by [tirumaḻisai āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thirumazhisai-azhwar/) in tiruccanda viruttam 113, which means that emperumān made an incision on his chest and took out fragrant blood to fill the skull in Siva’s hand. brahmā suffered by having his head severed. Siva severed a head and became a sinner. emperumān removed the pain of both.

Those who attain such [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/)’s divine feet ……

**amarar tam bhōgattāl bhūmi āḷvār** – instead of suffering like Siva et al, they will experience the joy of nityasūris (permanent dwellers of śrīvaikuṇṭam) in paramapadam and also experience the happiness on this earth. Hasn’t [nammāḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/nammazhwar/) mentioned in tiruviruttam 79 “*viṇṇuḷārilum sīriyar*” (those who worship emperumān on this earth are superior to nityasūris in paramapadam)! ōr, as mentioned by nammāḻvār in [tiruvāimoḻi 4-3-11](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2017/01/thiruvaimozhi-4-3-11-uyvupayam/) “*vaiyam manni vīṟṟirundu viṇṇumāḷvar maṇṇūdē*” (they will rule the earth and also be happy in śrīvaikuṇṭam).

Let us take up the 47<sup>th</sup> pāsuram next.

## 47. Vari surukki

*vāri surukki madak kaḷiṟu aindinaiyum cēri tiriyāmal senniṟīi kūriya mey jñānattāl uṇarvār kāṇbarē mēl oru nāḷ kai nāgam kāttān kaḻal*

**Пословный перевод:**

madak kaḷiṟu aindinaiyum – the five sensory perceptions which are like elephants in rut; vāri surukki – reducing the water \[which is like enjoyment\]; cēri tiriyāmal sem niṟīi – ensuring that they do not loiter in the streets of worldly objects such as sound, smell etc; kūriya – subtle; mey jñānattal – true knowledge which takes the form of devotion; uṇarvār – those who can know \[emperumān\]; mēl oru nāḷ – in earlier time; kai nāgam kāttān – the one who protected the elephant, gajēndrāḻvān with the trunk \[in our sampradāyam, it is common to denote certain entities as āḻvān to bring in a distinction of superior knowledge about emperumān, over other entities; gajēndra is one such\]; kaḻal – the divine feet; kānbar – they will see and enjoy

**Перевод:**

**vāri surukki – to control a rutting elephant, people would reduce its intake of water. The worldly pleasures are to sensory organs, much similar to water for an elephant in rut. To control the five sensory organs which are like elephants, one has to reduce gradually the pleasures that they enjoy. If they are completely stopped immediately, they will tear us apart. At the same time, if we allow them to go their way , they will become very arrogant and will become more roused. In śrī bhagavat gītā 2-62, emperumān says “sangāt sanjāyatē kāma: kāmāt krōdōpi jāyatē: krōdāt bhavati sammōha: sammōhāt smruti vibhrama: smrutibhramśāt buddināśō buddināśāt praṇaśyati” (due to attachment towards worldly pleasures lust is formed; lust begets anger; anger leads to inability to discriminate; indiscrimination leads to loss of memory; that results in loss of intelligence; loss of intelligence results in the persons’s destruction in samsāram (material realm)). Hence one should not indulge too much in sensory pleasures and should not reduce them at once. There should be a balance between the two extremes for sustaining oneself and they should be reduced slowly.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*āḻvār says that it is not possible for anyone to attain his divine feet other than by those who control their sensory organs such that they do not go after worldly pleasures, and who have full knowledge about him. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**madak kaḷiṟu aindinaiyum** – the five sensory organs of ears, mouth, eyes, nose and body which are like five rutting elephants.

**cēri tiriyāmāl sem niṟīi** – if the elephant strays within one’s area of vision, it can be caught. If sensory perceptions are controlled such that they do not stray in worldly pleasures, like the example of the elephant, it would lead to sharp, true knowledge. True knowledge results in capability to know the subtle meanings \[of śāstras\] and to know [ātmā (soul)](https://granthams.koyil.org/2013/03/06/thathva-thrayam-chith-who-am-i/) and [paramātmā (emperumān)](https://granthams.koyil.org/2013/03/12/thathva-thrayam-iswara-who-is-god/). Those who carry out deeds which are prescribed in śāstras, without any attachment in the deeds will have their sins removed, leading to knowledge about ātmā and paramātmā, knowing further that jīvātmā is a servitor to paramātmā and would finally be able to see him. It is said “*upāsanam druvāsmruti: darśana samānākāratā pratyakśāpati*” (devotion is the steady level of thoughts. It will create the impression of seeing \[[emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/)\] directly. This will lead to attaining the goal of seeing directly). In śrī bhagavat gītā 18-55, emperumān says “*tatō mām tatvatō jñātvā viśatē tadanantaram*” (he knows about me truly through devotion and after that he attains me through that devotion).

**mēl oru nāḷ kai nāgam kāttān kaḻal** – āḻvār gives example to show that they attain [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/). In earlier times, an elephant by name gajēndra, avoiding its indulgence in worldly pursuits, entered a pond with the intention of plucking a lotus to place at the divine feet of emperumān. However it was caught by a crocodile in the pond. To overcome the hurdle created by the crocodile and to remove its sorrow, emperumān killed the crocodile and placed the lotus plucked by the elephant on his divine feet. Those who have devotion towards such emperumān will get to attain his divine feet. āḻvār says that just as emperumān removed the enemy of elephant and gave himself to the elephant, he would remove the enemies of his followers (being caught by sensory indulgence) and give himself to them.

**cēri tiriyāmal senniṟīi** – has it not been said in [śrī bhagavat gītā 2-59](http://githa.koyil.org/index.php/2-59/) “*viśayā vinivartantē nirāhārasya dēhina: rasavarjam rasō’pyasya param druśtvā nivartantē*  ” (for a person who has come out of worldly pursuits which are the prey to sensory perceptions, those pursuits will leave. The desire in worldly pursuits does not leave him. Once he attains the higher level of seeing the basic nature of [ātmā](https://granthams.koyil.org/2013/03/06/thathva-thrayam-chith-who-am-i/), this desire also leaves)!.

**kūriya mey jñānam** – this is defined as thinking of [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) as the sweet entity to be attained and thinking of him as the path and object to be attained.

We shall next consider the 48<sup>th</sup> pāsuram.

## 48. Kalal onreduttu

*kaḻal onṟu eduttu oru kai suṟṟi ōr kai mēl suḻalum surāsurargaḷ anja aḻalum seruvāzi  ēndinān sēvadikkē sella maruvāḻi nenjē magiḻndu*

**Пословный перевод:**

āḻi nenjē – Oh my heart, which is as deep as the ocean!; kaḻal onṟu – one leg of namuci \[son of mahābali\]; eduttu – taking hold of; oru kai – with one divine hand; suṟṟi – spinning him around; suḻalum – (demons who were) scared (as to what will happen); sura asurargaḷ – both celestial persons and demons; anja – to be afraid of; ōr kai mēl – on top of the other hand; aḻalum – glowing red hot; seru āḻi ēndinān – emperumān who is holding his sudarśana cakkara (disc); sēvadikkē sella – to attain his fresh divine feet; magiḻndu maruvu – enjoy with happiness

**Перевод:**

**kaḻal onṟu eduttu oru kai suṟṟi – he stretched one divine leg and spun around his enemies with one divine hand. Alternatively, we can consider that he took hold of one leg of mahābali’s son namuci and spun him around with one divine hand and threw him.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*āḻvār tells his heart “Oh heart! We do not have to observe the difficult-to-practice controls of sensory perceptions etc. Since emperumān comes to where we are and offers himself to us, you try and enjoy him with affection” Let us enjoy the pāsuram and its meanings.*

**ōr kai mēl aḻalum seru āḻi ēndinān** – [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/)’s sudarśana cakkaram (divine disc) spits fire on both dēvas (celestial entities) who are loving towards emperumān and are fearful about any danger that may happen to him and demons, who are out to cause harm to emperumān, without any difference, due to his deep affection for emperumān.  emperumān holds such divine disc which is always battle ready, on his other divine hand. emperumān’s action then was such that his followers were afraid of any harm that could befall him and his enemies were afraid as to what harm he will create for them! On my heart! Proceed towards his divine feet to attain them. It is enough if you do not reject his divine feet when they come on their own towards you.

**āḻi nenjē** – Oh my heart! You are as deep as the ocean. When you are ready to attain [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/)’s  divine feet, do not do that as a routine.

**magiḻndu maruvu** – attain his divine feet with total affection.  Will you not feel happy and enjoy emperumān  when he removes our enemies and offers his divine feet to us by coming to us, and who also feels exhilarated that he has now attained us?

Let us take up the 49<sup>th</sup> pāsuram next.

## 49. Magilalagonre pol

*magiḻ alagu onṟē pōl māṟum pal yākkai negiḻa muyalgiṟpārkkallāl mugiḻ virinda sōdi pōl tōnṟum sudar pon nedu mudi em ādi kāṇbārkkum aridu*

**Пословный перевод:**

magiḻ alagu onṟē pōl – just as the seed of the magiḻam tree (a kind of flower bearing tree with sweet fragrance) can be kept in a very restricted location as well as in an expansive location; māṟum pal yākkai – with various forms which keep changing constantly; negiḻa – to be rid of; muyalgiṟpārkku allāl – other than those who attempt to; kāṇbaārkkum – the others who desire to see; mugiḻ virinda sōdi pōl tōnṟum – that which appears like a brightened radiance; sudar pon nedu mudi – the one who has radiant, beautiful and large crown; em ādi – sarvēśvaran (supreme being), who is the cause for my existence; aridu – is rare to attain

**Перевод:**

**magiḻ alagu onṟē poḷ māṟum pal yākkai – in mats, the seed of the magiḻam (bearing flowers with a sweet fragrance) tree can be placed either in a very small area or in a very large area. In the same way, the ātmā (soul) based on its past deeds, can enter either the form of an ant or the form of a brahmā (one who creates all the worlds at the command of emperumān) and takes birth repeatedly for innumerable times. nammāḻvār mercifully spoke of the same concept in tiruviruttam 95 “yādānum ōr ākkaiyil pukku” (entering in some form or the other).**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*āḻvār says in this pāsuram that irrespective of whatever means people employ, apart from those who develop hatred for worldly pursuits and attain him, others cannot see him. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**negiḻa muyalgiṟpārkku allāl** – apart from those who make an attempt to get rid of such births, [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) cannot be seen by the others. These forms \[physical bodies inside which the soul pervades\] have a special property. They will desire those who desire them. They will not like those who do not like them. There is nothing softer than a body. [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/poigai-azhwar/) says that those who develop a hatred for worldly pursuits and who develop a desire to envision emperumān can easily see him.

**mugiḻ virinda sōdi pōl tōnṟum sudar pon nedu mudi** **em ādi** – *ādi* means the causative factor for all. emperumān’s crown has a radiance that has just blossomed, is very beautiful to look at and points out that he is the Lord of all.

**kāṇbārkkum aridu** – even for those who desire to see him, unless they rid themselves of desire for worldly pursuits, it is very difficult to see him. Even if one were to think “īsn’t he ours! Is it difficult to see him! “as mentioned above, unless the desire in worldly pursuits is eradicated totally, it is very difficult to see him.

**ādi** – He is such an entity who gives us physical forms, gives us sensory perceptions, awaits our consent for him to uplift us, expectantly awaits to accept our service to him after we have rid ourselves of our connection with this world. He will not show himself up to those who still desire worldly pursuits. Thus the opinion here is that unless we remove our desire for worldly pleasures, we cannot attain him.

We shall take up the 50<sup>th</sup> pāsuram next.

## 50. Ariya pulanaindadakki

*ariya pulan aindadakki āy malar koṇḍu ārvam puriya parisināl pulgil periyanāy māṟṟādu vīṟṟidunda māvalipāl vaṇkai nīr ēṟṟānaik kānbadu eḷidu*

**Пословный перевод:**

ariya – difficult to control; pulan aindu – the five sensory perceptions; adakki – to bring under one’s control; āy malar koṇḍu – taking flowers after proper selection, in the hand; ārvam puriya parisināl – in the path of love; pulgil – if vorśipped; periyanāy – as a great person (in both wealth and magnanimity); māṟṟādu vīṟṟirunda – without change (in donating); māvali pāl – towards mahābali; vaṇ kai – with his munificent hands; nīr ēṟṟānai – emperumān who received water \[which is given as indication of commitment to donating\]; kāṇbadu – to worship; eḷidu – will be easy

**Перевод:**

**ariya pulan aindu adakki – controlling with difficulty the five sensory organs of ear, mouth, eye, nose and body \[skin\] which are uncontrollable. If water, meant for one channel, is diverted into two channels, it will become insufficient for both channels. In the same way, the sensory perceptions which are meant for enjoying emperumān should be controlled, without going into worldly pursuits.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*āḻvār says that it is very easy to see emperumān for those who are without involvement in worldly pursuits. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**ariya pulan** – in [śrī bhagavat gītā 6-34](http://githa.koyil.org/index.php/6-34/) arjuna tells kriṣṇa “*cancalam hi mana: kriṣṇa pramāti balavad drudam \| tasyāham nigraham manyē vāyōr iva suduśkaram \|\|*” (kriṣṇa! I think that this mind is unsteady, makes me agitated, strong, steady on lowly pursuits. Controlling it is as difficult as controlling wind). Thus, isn’t mind very difficult to control?

**āy malar koṇḍu ārvan puriya parisināl pulgil** – if we approach [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) with flowers appropriate to him, with love

**āy malar koṇḍu** – with the best flowers

**ārvam puriya parisu**  – in a loving way

**pulgil** – if we embrace. Since attaining him is sweet, [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/poigai-azhwar/) lovingly equates attainment as embracing him. Just as we desire to attain good things when we come across such things, it is enough if we desire to attain [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/). There is no need for any special rituals.

**periyanāy māṟṟādu vīṟṟirunda māvalipāl** – thinking of himself as being superior to emperumān, mahābali sat \[on his throne\] as if he would give to everyone whatever they wanted without any refusal.

**vaṇ kai nīr ēṟṟān** – it is easy to see someone who is habituated to giving to others with his hands, but who goes to get seek alms from another. Is he not someone who will go and seek alms from someone who says that he will give something! Hence there is nothing difficult in attaining him; it is very easy.

We shall move on to the 51<sup>st</sup> pāsuram next.

## 51. Elidil irandadiyum

*eḷidil iraṇḍadiyum kāṇbadaṟku ennuḷḷam teḷiyat teḷindoḻiyum sevvē kaḷiyil porundādavanaip poraluṟṟu ariyāy irundān tirunāmam eṇ*

**Пословный перевод:**

en uḷḷam – Oh my heart!; eḷidil – easily; iraṇḍu adiyum – both divine feet; kāṇbadaṟku – appropriate to worship; teḷiya – if (you) attain clarity; sevvē teḷindu oḻiyum – (he also) will become very clear \[and happy\]; kaḷiyil – due to vanity; porundādavanai – one who doesn’t come to him; poral uṟṟu – being intent on waging war; ariyāy – as narasimhan (face of lion and body of human); irundān – one who incarnated; tirunāmam – beautiful divine names; eṇ – keep meditating

**Перевод:**

**eḷidil iraṇḍu adiyum kāṇbadaṟku – the word eḷidil can be interpreted in two ways – (1) not  simple and (2) easy. If it is taken to mean that it is not simple, the meaning for this phrase would be “to see the two divine feet which are very exalted”. If it is taken to mean easy it would mean “to see the divine feet of emperumān without any effort”**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*In this pāsuram, āḻvār says if we are devoted to emperumān, he will destroy our enemies and show himself to us just as he destroyed prahalāda’s enemy hiraṇyan and showed himself to prahalāda. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**ennuḷḷam teḷiya** – Oh my heart! If you leave aside your devotion to worldly pursuits and get clarity \[that he is the one to be devoted to\] emperumān will be happy. He will be happy, without being angry at the sins that we have been committing since time immemorial, which had prevented us from seeing him. He will not see our faults if you stop indulging in worldly pursuits.

**sevvē teḷindu oḻiyum** – [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) will become very clear. Instead of doing something superficially, he will also become wholeheartedly very clear. Earlier, looking at the countless sins that I was committing, he was very disturbed. Now, after I become clear in my mind and approach him, he will also become very clear and happy.

[āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/poigai-azhwar/) recounts an incident that happened earlier when his devotee recited his divine name and he went to his help.

**kaḷiyil porundādavanaip poraluṟṟu** – engaging in fight with hiranya kaśyap who was not amicable with [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/). hiraṇyan, owing to his boons and his strength, became very vain and said that he will not bow down to anyone. A person who says that he himself is God cannot be amicable with emperumān, can he! Hence, in order to do good to him, emperumān destroys his physical body.

**ariyāy irundān tirunāmam eṇ** – since [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) was in the form of narasimhan to remove the sadness of his devotee, meditate on the divine names of this form. Another interpretation – he was lazing since there were no more enemies, who would become fodder to his anger, to destroy. What we need to do is to meditate on emperumān’s divine names, just as his child-devotee prahlāda did. If we did that, emperumān will himself protect us, instead of driving us to other deities. Oh Heart! Don’t you know the difficulties that hiraṇyan had to endure because he did not meditate on emperumān’s divine names! [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/poigai-azhwar/) says, meditate on his divine names and gain the result that prahlāda gained instead of suffering like hiraṇyan,

We shall take up the 52<sup>nd</sup> pāsuram next.
