## 52. Enmar padinoruvar

*eṇmar padinoruvar īraṟuvar ōriruvar vaṇṇa malar ēndi vaigalum naṇṇi oru mālaiyāl paravi ōvādu eppōdum tirumālaik kai toḻuvar senṟu*

**Пословный перевод:**

eṇmar – the eight vasus; padinoruvar – the 11 rudras; īr aṟuvar – the 12 ādityas; ōr iruvar – the twin aśvinī dēvas; vaṇṇam malar ēndi – holding flowers with multiple colours; vaigalum naṇṇi – approaching \[emperumān\] everyday; oru mālaiyāl – with the incomparable garland of words, puruśa sūktam; ōvādu eppōdum – always, without any break; paravi – worship; tirumālai – the consort of śrī mahālakśmi; senṟu kai toḻuvar – will approach and worship

**Перевод:**

**eṇmar padinoruvar īraṟuvar ōriruvar – the eight vasus, eleven rudras, twelve ādityas and the twin aśvinī dēvas**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*When asked, “Is it not possible to live without reciting his divine names?”, āḻvār says that those who are egotistical and who occupy high posts, exhibit their authority only because they are devoted to him. Let us see the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**vaṇṇa malar ēndi** – holding multi coloured flowers \[appropriate to emperumān’s divine form\]

**vaigalum naṇṇi** – approaching him all the time

**oru mālaiyāl paravi** – with the great composition *puruśa sūktam* they will worship him, even without following the ordained way of recitation

**ōvādu eppōdum** – all the time, without any break

**tirumālaik kai toḻuvar senṟu** – they approach śrī mahālakśmi’s consort and worship.

**tirumāl** – pirātti (mahālakśmi) is there to grant the boons sought by each one of them. The dēvas, who sit egotistically waiting for people to come to their doorsteps to seek alms, rid themselves of their ego and go to emperumān on their own. Is there a necessity for the dēvas to attain him? brahmā sūtram 1-3-25 says “*taduparyapi bhādarāyaṇassambhavāt*” (bhādarāyaṇar (a great sage) says that *brahmōpāsanam* (devotion to emperumān) is apt for even dēvas, who are superior to human beings). Thus, since it is ordained in vēdam, in  order to get mōkśam (liberation from materialistic realm and reaching śrīvaikuṇṭam) at some point of time in future, dēvas also approach emperumān who is at the centre of the galaxy and worship him.

We shall take up the 53<sup>rd</sup> pāsuram next.

## 53. Senral kudaiyam

*senṟāl kudaiyām irundāl singāsanamām ninṟāl maravadiyām nīḷ kadaluḷ enṟum puṇaiyām maṇiviḷakkām pūm pattām pulgum aṇaiyām tirumāṟku aravu*

**Пословный перевод:**

tirumāṟku – for emperumān who is with pirātti (śrī mahālakśmi); aravu – tiruvanantāḻvān (ādi śēśan); senṟāl kudaiyām – is an umbrella when (emperumān) goes out; irundāl singāsanamām – (he) is the throne when he sits; ninṟāl – when emperumān stands; maravadiyām – (he) is the divine sandals; nīḷ kadaluḷ – in the expansive ocean; enṟum – whenever emperumān reclines; puṇaiyām – (he) is the float; maṇi viḷakkām – (he) is the auspicious lamp; pūm pattam – (he) is the beautiful divine dress; pulgum aṇaiyām – (he) is the pillow that emperumān embraces

**Перевод:**

**senṟāl kudaiyām – tiruvanantāḻvān assumes various forms appropriate to emperumān’s needs to carry out service. When emperumān goes out on foot, he will be his umbrella. ādiśēśan cannot tell emperumān “I want to be an umbrella. Come and stand under me” as it will be contrary to servitorśip. Hence when emperumān leaves for some place, he becomes his umbrella. The opinion here is that services rendered to emperumān would be as per emperumān’s wishes and not as per the servitor’s.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*In this pāsuram āḻvār describes the state of nityasūris (permanent dwellers of śrīvaikuṇṭam) who carry out service to emperumān as their svabhāvam (nature), unlike dēvas (celestial people) who come to emperumān for seeking solution to their problems and leave. In the earlier pāsuram, āḻvār spoke about the dēvas who carry out service to emperumān in order to get some benefits from him. In this pāsuram he speaks about nityasūris who transform themselves in order to serve emperumān. Let us enjoy the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**irundāḷ singāsanamām** – when [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) desires to sit, ādiśēśan will be his throne.

**ninṟal maravadiyām** – when emperumān desires to stand, ādiśēśan will be his sandals.

**ninṟal** – *after* emperumān stands, ādiśēśan will be his sandals.

**nīḷ kadaluḷ enṟum puṇaiyām** – in the expansive ocean, when emperumān wants to rest awhile, he will be the soft mattress. *puṇai* would also mean a float (the meaning then will be that he will be a float in *tiruppāṟkadal* (milky ocean) for emperumān)

**maṇi viḷakkām** – ādiśēśan will be an auspicious lamp. There is a variant in the phrase, with **aṇi viḷakkām** also used. In this case the meaning will change to beautiful lamp. If [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) wants to see objects, ādiśēśan will become auspicious lamp.

**pūm pattām** – if emperumān desires in his divine mind to wear a beautiful dress, ādiśēśan will be his dress. He will become his yellow silky dress which indicates that emperumān is the supreme entity.

**pulgum aṇaiyām** – when [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) desires to recline,  he will become the pillow. If there is any (feigned) discord between emperumān and [pirātti](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sri-mahalakshmi/), ādiśēśan will become the pillow for emperumān to embrace and forget the discord. Alternatively, it can be construed that emperumān will embrace ādiśēśan (as a pillow) due to which discord will arise between emperumān and pirātti.

**tirumāṟku** – ādiśēśan carries out service to both [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) and [pirātti](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sri-mahalakshmi/). Just as lakśmaṇa carried out service to rāma when he was joined by sītā, ādiśēśan also carries out service to emperumān when he is conjoined with pirātti. Just as golden bar can be converted into ornaments as we desire, ādiśēśan also transforms into umbrella, throne, sandals, float, lamp, divine dress, pillow etc while carrying out service to emperumān \[and pirātti\]. emperumān and pirātti need not feel shy due to the presence of ādiśēśan when they are engaged in playing with each other and he remains as their mattress. Just as dwarfs and humpbacks go around the private quarters of royalties when the King and Queen are experiencing pleasurable moments without disturbing them, ādiśēśan also remains without intruding.

What has been mentioned for ādiśēśan is applicable to other nityasūris too.

We shall consider the 54<sup>th</sup> pāsuram next.

## 54. Aravam adal velam

*aravam adal vēḻam ān kurundam puḷ vāy kuravai kuda mulai maṟkunṟam karavinṟi vittiṟuttu mēyttosittu kīndu kōttādi uṇḍu attedutta sem kaṇ avan*

**Пословный перевод:**

sem kaṇ avan – emperumān with reddish lotus like eyes; karavu inṟi – without hiding; aravam vittu – driving away the snake kāḷiyan \[from the river yamunā\]; adal vēḻam iṟuttu – breaking the tusk of the warring elephant kuvalayāpīdam; An mēyttu – grazing cows; kurundam osittu – uprooting the tree kurundam (a flower bearing tree) in which a demon had entered; puḷ vāy kīndu – tearing the beak of a crane in which a demon had entered; kuravai kōttu – carrying out rāsakrīdai with covherd girls \[rāsakrīdai is a game in which two persons grip their hands in a crossed way, together, and go in a circular motion, with their planted feet acting together as the centre\]; kudam ādi – dancing with pots; mulai uṇḍu – suckling (pūtanā’s) milk (along with her life); mal attu – killing wrestlers (cāṇura and muśtika); kunṟam edutta – lifting (gōvardana) hill

**Перевод:**

**aravu ….. – emperumān who has eyes similar to reddish lotus, engaged in many activities when he took his incarnation as kriṣṇa.  āḻvār describes some of those activities in this pāsuram. In the first two lines of the pāsuram are the subjects aravam, adal vēḻam, An, kurundam, puḷ vāy, kuravai, kudam, mulai, mal and kunṟam and in the last two lines are the activities associated with these subjects in the same order – vittu, iṟuttu, mēyttu, osittu,kīṇḍu, kōttu, ādi, uṇḍu, attu and edutta. The pairs will thus be**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*āḻvār says in this pāsuram that emperumān, leaving the comfort of the mattress that ādiśēśan provides (as part of all the services that he willingly carries out to emperumān), comes down to the materialistic realm and protects it. His nature is such that in order to make the people of this realm also to be his servitors and get them to carry out services like ādiśēśan does, he sets aside his greatness and comes to lower levels. As per tamiḻ grammar, this poem falls under the category of niral nirai aṇi in which each of the terms in the first part of the poem corresponds to each of the terms in the second part in the same order that the terms appear. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**aravam vittu** – driving away the snake kāḷiyan and not killing him as requested for, by his wives

**adal vēḻam iṟuttu** – when kamsan set his royal elephant kuvalayāpīdam on kriṣṇa to kill him, kriṣṇa broke off its tusks and killed it

**An mēyttu** – though he is [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/), he did not consider his greatness but came down as a covherd boy and took the cattle for grazing.

**kurundam osittu** – when a demon entered a tree *kurundam* in order to kill kriṣṇa, kriṣṇa uprooted the tree, killing the demon.

**puḷ vāy kīṇḍu** – kamsan asked his friend bakāsuran, a demon, to take the form of a bird \[crane\] to kill kriṣṇa. But, kriṣṇa, knowing the disguise of the demon, killed the bird by tearing its beak.

**kuravai kōttu** – he engaged in dancing with the covherd girls in a type of dance called rāsakrīdai (explained above)

**kudam ādi** – dancing with pots \[in the middle of the road\]

**mulai uṇḍu** – kamsan first set off pūtana, his friend, to kill infant kriṣṇa. She took the form of yaśōdā, kriṣṇa’s mother and offered her poisoned milk to him, and he drank both her milk and her life, thus killing her.

**mal attu** – kamsan, after kuvalayāpīdam was killed, set off his champion wrestlers against kriṣṇa. But both cāṇura and muśtika, the wrestlers, were killed by kriṣṇa in the wrestling match.

**kunṟam eduttu** – indra, the deity of celestial persons, was denied food by the people of gōkulam. In anger, he rained hailstorm on gōkulam. kriṣṇa lifted gōvardanam, a hill around gōkulam and saved the herds-people.

**karavinṟi vittu** – [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) did not hide anything  while engaging in the above mentioned acts. There are two meanings given for *karavinṟi* – allowing someone to escape without killing him, and acting honestly without anything deceitful.

emperumān takes all these efforts after leaving the comfortable mattress of *tiruvantāḻvān* (ādiśēśan) and his services. In irandām tiruvandādi 15, [bhūtattāḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/bhuthaththazhwar/) says “*nāgattait taṇ paḷḷi koḷvān tanakku*” (emerpumān has ādiśēśan as his comfortable bed).

Let us take the 55<sup>th</sup> pāsuram next.

## 55. Avan tamar

*avan tamar evvinaiyar āgilum engōn avan tamarē enṟu oḻivadallāl naman tamarāl ārāyappattum aṟiyār kaṇḍīr aravaṇai mēl pērāyaṟku ātpattār pēr*

**Пословный перевод:**

aāvan tamar – the followers of that paramapuruśan (supreme soul or emperumān); evvinaiyar āgilum – irrespective of their deeds; “em kōn avan tamarē” enṟu – praising them by saying “aren’t they the followers of my svāmy (lord) sarvēśvaran (supreme being)”; oḻivadallāl – other than leaving that place; aravaṇai mēl pēr āyaṟku ātpattār pēr – those who have the names of the followers of emperumān, who came out of the mattress of ādiśēśan in order to protect his followers, as a covherd; naman tamarāl – by the messengers of yama; ārāyappattu aṟiyār kaṇḍīr – you would see that they are not questioned \[by the messengers of yama\]

**Перевод:**

**avan tamar evvinaiyar āgilum – In the words of the messengers of yama, “What does it matter as to what sins are committed by the followers of emperumān? When those who are designed to question the sins of everybody cannot question the followers of emperumān, can anyone else question them?” If one were to ask whether one cannot look for virtues or faults in the followers of emperumān, the response will be that those who are his followers, even if they carry out certain deeds which are forbidden by śāstras (sacred texts) no one can question them.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*āḻvār asks in this pāsuram as to who can look at emperumān‘s followers, with anger in their eyes, when he is there to look for the enemies of his followers to destroy them. He says that yama’s (deity for dharma, righteousness) messengers cannot judge whether emperumān’s followers have carried out any sins. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**engōn avan tamarē enṟu oḻivadallāl** – Other than saying that these persons are the followers of [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/), nothing else can be done. In śrī viṣṇu purāṇam 3-7-15 yama says “*prabhavati samyamanē mamāpi viṣṇu:*” (viṣṇu is capable of appointing me too). In the same ślōkam he further says “*parihara madusūdana prapannān prabhuraham anya nruṇām na vaiṣṇavānām*” (Leave aside those who have surrendered to madusūdanan (emperumān). I am the Lord of others, but not of vaiṣṇavas). He further says in śrī viṣṇu purāṇam 3-7-33 “*kamalanayana! vāsudēva! viṣṇō! daraṇidara! śankacakrapāṇē! bhavaśaraśamitīrayanti yē vai tyaja bhata dūratarēṇa tānapāpān* ” (yama tells his messengers, “whoever says ‘Oh one with the lotus eyes! Oh vāsudēva! Oh viṣṇu! Oh one who supports earth! Oh one who never lets down his followers! Oh one who holds the conch and disc! You are my saviour’, leave that person, who has not committed any sin, and go far away from him”)

**naman tamarāl ārāyappattu aṟiyār kaṇḍīr** – these people \[who are followers of [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/)\] have not been questioned by even those who have been appointed to look into the sins of others and who are close to yama. Who are such people who cannot be questioned? Are they those who are followers of emperumān? No, they are those who have as their names, the names of emperumān’s followers. If a person, who is not emperumān’s follower, has the name of his follower, his name will not be read out in yama’s court \[among the names of those who have committed sins\].

**aravaṇai mēl pērāyaṟku ātpattār pēr** – these people (followers of [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/)) can be searched only on the day when emperumān’s bed is searched! \[meaning that they can never be searched\]. Just as [periyāḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/periyazhwar/) says in periyāḻvār tirumoḻi 4-9-2 “*tāmaraiyāḷāgilum sidaguraikkumēl en adiyār adu seyyār seydārēl nanṟu seydār enbar*” even if [śrī mahālakśmi](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sri-mahalakshmi/) complains about followers to emperumān, he will deny that. When such is the case, can the messengers of yama question them! Again, periyāḻvār in his periyāḻvār tirumoḻi 4-6-1 says “*nāraṇan tam annai naragam pugāḷ*” – if a newly born infant has been named after nārāyaṇan, the mother of that infant becomes the mother of nārāyaṇa himself. Will such a mother be ever go to narakam (hell)!

Let us move on to the 56<sup>th</sup> pāsuram.

## 56. Pere vara

*pērē varap pidaṟṟal allāl em pemmānai ārē aṟivār adu niṟka nērē kadikkamalattuḷ irundum kāṇgilān kannan adikkamalam tannai ayan*

**Пословный перевод:**

pērē – with the divine name (of emperumān); vara – such that he comes; pitaṟṟal allāl – apart from saying in disorderly manner; em pemmānai – my emperumān (lord); ārē aṟivār – who can know; adu niṟka – let it be so; kadi kamalttu uḷ – floating inside the fragrant lotus (which came out of emperumān’s navel); nērē irundum – being very close (to emperumān); ayan – brahmā who was born to emperumān; kaṇṇan – that emperumān’s; adi kamalam tannai – the divine lotus-like feet; kāṇkilān – would not know

**Перевод:**

**pērē varap pidaṟṟal allāl – Not saying something or the other, other than reciting emperumān’s divine name and making this as a habit. Has it not been divined by kulaśēkara āḻvār in perumāḷ tirumoḻi 2-4 “nāttaḻumbeḻa nāraṇā enṟaḻaittu” (speak out emperumān’s name of nārāyaṇa till scar forms on the tongue)! Just as the elephant gajēndrāḻvān called out to him, even if in a disorderly way, such that emperumān came hurrying to the bund of the pond where gajēndra was caught by a crocodile.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*āḻvār says that to be subservient to emperumān is nothing but not rejecting him when he comes to shower his mercy on us. Nobody can estimate his greatness, he says. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**em pemmānai ārē aṟivār** – who can know the entity who is my Lord? [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/poigai-azhwar/) knew about [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) since emperumān himself manifested to him.

**adu niṟka** – let the enquiry into who can know wait for some time.

**nērē kadikkamalattuḷ irundum kāṇgilān** – Let us see who did not know him. Even brahmā, who was born in the fragrant lotus which shot off from [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/)’s navel and made that itself as his dwelling place, who has the greatness of having been taught all the vēdas (sacred texts) by emperumān himself, has not seen the divine feet of emperumān despite being so close to him.

**kadikkamalattuḷ irundum kāṇgilān** – are they so difficult to see? Yes, just as one cannot estimate the depth of an ocean by building a house near the shore and staying there for a very long time.  At the most, one can know that it is not possible to know. In śrī rāmāyaṇam yudda kāṇḍam 120-3, it is said of brahmā “*brahmā brahmavidām vara:*” (brahmā is the greatest among those who know brahmam (supreme entity)). Thus, if the most knowledgeable brahmā himself does not know [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/), is it possible for others to know? [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/poigai-azhwar/) says that when vēdam itself says that unless emperumān comes and shows himself up, it is not possible for anyone to know him.

Let us take up the 57<sup>th</sup> pāsuram next.

## 57. Ayal ninra

*ayal ninṟa valvinaiyai anjinēn anji uya nin tiruvadiyē sērvān naya ninṟa nanmālai koṇḍu namō nāranā ennum sonmālai kaṟṟēn toḻudu*

**Пословный перевод:**

ayal ninṟa – standing close (to me); val vinaiyai – looking at the extremely powerful sins; anjinēn – I was scared; anji – being afraid (like this); uya – to get emancipated (after ridding of the sins); nin – your; tiruvadiyē sērvān – in order to attain divine feet; nayam ninṟa – being in the form of śāstras (sacred texts); nal mālai koṇḍu – with beautiful garland; toḻudu – vorśipped; namō nāranā ennum – tirumantram with eight syllables; solmālai – garland of words; kaṟṟen – I practised

**Перевод:**

**ayal ninṟa valvinaiyai anjinēn – I was scared of my sins which kept troubling me, without leaving me, entering wherever I entered and constricting me (this is considering the meaning for the term ayal ninṟa to be ‘nearby’). Alternative explanation – I was scared as to what would I do if the sins, which had left me on my gaining knowledge about emperumān, would come back (this is considering the meaning for the term ayal ninṟa to be ‘at a distance’).**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*When āḻvār was asked as to how he knew emperumān when even brahmā could not know him, he responds saying “after I held on to him by reciting his divine names, he showed himself to me; thus I knew him. I knew that life in this world is susceptible to danger and that only if I hold on to him could I extricate myself from it” Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**anji uya nin tiruadiyē sērvān** – being scared and in order to get emancipated from those sins, I recited your divine names in order to attain your divine feet, which  will not give any place for fear. It is not that I was capable of reciting your divine names. Due to fear  and incapacity, in order to sustain myself I recited something.

**nayam ninṟa** – being very simple to recite. Alternatively, being an explanation for *tirumantram* (the eight syllable phrase based on the name nārāyaṇa).

**namō nārāṇā ennum sol mālai kaṟṟēn** – I learnt about *tirumantram* which reveals about him from this *prabandam* (*mudal tiruvandādi*, a collection of divine verses) which is an explanation for *tirumantram* (at the beginning of this *prabandam*, āḻvār had mercifully used the term  “*sol mālai sūttinēn*” (I adorned him with a garland of verses) in the very first pāsuram. Hence in the verse “*sol mālai kaṟṟēn*” he says that he spoke about this *prabandam*).

**son mālai kaṟṟēn toḻudu** – I learnt about the garland of verses, and appropriate to that, I also vorśipped him in line with the meaning of the verse.

We shall take up the 58<sup>th</sup> pāsuram next.

## 58. Toludu malar kondu

*tozudu malar koṇḍu dūpam kai ēndi eḻudum eḻu vāḻi nanjē paḻudinṟi mandirangaḷ kaṟpanavum māladiyē kai toḻuvān andaram onṟu illai adai*

**Пословный перевод:**

nenjē – Oh \[my\] heart!; vāḻi – let benefits happen (to you); malar koṇḍu – taking fragrant flowers; dūpam kai ēndi – holding the fragrant smoke stand in the hand; toḻudu – worship; eḻudum – let us get uplifted; eḻu – please get up; paḻudinṟi – without any defect; mandirangaḷ – ślōkas (hymns about emperumān); kaṟpanavum – to learn; māl adiyē kai toḻvān – to worship the divine feet of emperumān with hands; andaram onṟu illai – there is no reason for delaying this; adai – approach (emperumān)

**Перевод:**

**toḻudu – folding the hands together as anjali (vorśipping by keeping palms together)**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*āḻvār tells his heart, in this pāsuram, that there is no time to waste and that it should approach emperumān without any further delay. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**malar koṇḍu** – with beautiful, fresh flowers

**dūpam kai ēndi** – holding the stand for developing fragrant smoke, in the hand \[This implies taking other materials also for performing [*tiruvārādanam*](http://ponnadi.blogspot.in/2012/07/srivaishnava-thiruvaaraadhanam.html) (vorśipping of [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/)) such as lamp, sandal paste etc\].

**eḻudum** – \[we will\] get uplifted

**eḻu** – get up fast

**vāḻi nenjē** – [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/poigai-azhwar/) blesses his heart saying that this benefit will stay with it forever.

**paḻudinṟi mandirangaḷ kaṟpanavum** – learning hymns on [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/), without any defect, is to carry out *gurukulavāsam* with an ācāryan and obtaining the hymns from him as spiritual instruction \[*gurukulavāsam* is a concept practised in the past when the student would stay with the teacher for about 12 years and learn from him all the teachings of various sacred texts in order to prepare him to lead a righteous life\]. Learning the hymns on his own instead of through *gurukulavāsam* will be considered as a faulty method of learning. It is similar to mounting an elephant which is sleeping on one’s own instead of mounting it with the help of the mahout, as the lying elephant will throw him and stamp him out with his trunk. Hymns should be learnt through an ācāryan in the method of *santai* \[in this method, the ācāryan will recite each word / line of the hymn once and the disciple will recite it thrice. This process will be repeated until the disciple is able to recite the entire hymn on his own, without faltering\].

**māl adiyē kai toḻuvān** – the purpose of learning the hymns is to worship the divine feet of [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) without any fault. It is to carry out continuous service at the divine feet of emperumān.

**andaram onṟillai adai** – hence, there is no time to lose. [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/poigai-azhwar/) implores his heart to go fast and approach emperumān.

We shall consider the 59<sup>th</sup> pāsuram next.

## 59. Adainda aruvinaiyodu

*adainda aruvinaiyōdu allal nōy pāvam midaindu avai mīṇḍoḻiya vēṇḍil nudangidaiyai munnilangai vaittān muraṇ aḻiya munnoru nāḷ tanvilangai vaittān caraṇ*

**Пословный перевод:**

adainda – that which came in-between \[not part of one’s nature\]; aru vinaiyōdu – dangerous entities such as ignorance; allal – sorrows \[disease\] of the mind; nōy – disease of the śarīram (physical body); pāvam – bad deeds; midaindavai – sins which are the causative factor for getting the physical body; mīṇḍu oḻiya vēṇḍil – to make them go back; nudangu idaiyai – sītāp pirātti who had slender waist; mun – once; ilangai vaittān – rāvaṇa who had kept her in ilangai (lankā); muraṇ aḻiya – to annihilate his strength; mun oru nāḷ – when he incarnated as śrī rāma; than vil am kai vaittān – the one who adorned the bow in his beautiful hands; caraṇ – he is the means (path to attain)

**Перевод:**

**adainda aruvinai – these are entities \[sins\] which came in between \[and not part of the soul as its nature\]. ātmā (soul) is the epitome of knowledge and happiness; it is unchanging. Since these sins have been following the soul for a long time, it would appear as if these are also a part of soul. Souls are such that they do not allow lowly entities such as sins to stick to them; hence these are called vandēṟi (something which came in between). Even though paramātmā (supreme entity) also dwells within the ātmā, these sins will not affect him.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*āḻvār says in this pāsuram that to enjoy emperumān, do as mentioned in the previous pāsuram. In order to get rid of the sins which are hurdles to this experience, approach śrī rāma, the son of emperor daśarata. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings.*

**allal** – sorrows of the mind called *ādi* resulting from these sins

**nōy** – diseases of the physical body

**pāvam** – the lowly deeds carried out because of assuming the physical body

**midaindavai** – all of these aforementioned aspects encircle the soul such that it cannot manifest its true nature. Just as the worm *samitaiyidāmāṇi* (silkworm) and spider create webs around them so tightly that they themselves cannot extricate from such webs, these sins also encircle the soul tightly. However, since these are not part of the soul, it is possible to get rid of the aforementioned entities.

**mīṇḍu oḻiya vēṇḍil** – in order to extricate from them. This is similar to making a snake which is chasing a person to turn back and escape. [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/poigai-azhwar/) says further that that one has to surrender to [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) in order to get rid of these entities, just as emperumān himself said in śrī bhagavat gītā 18-66 “*mām ēkam śaraṇam vraja – sarva pāpēbhyō mōkśayiśyāmi* ” (surrender only to me. I shall rid you of all your sins). If these are not to trouble oneself again, one has to take the help of the omnipotent emperumān, just as connection with the royal clan (in those days) would ensure total security for a person.

**nudangu idaiyai mun ilangai vaittān** – rāvaṇa imprisoned sītāppirātti who is always inseparable from emperumān and who has a slender waist. Just as one breaks a single entity into two, rāvaṇa separated śrī rāma and sītāppirātti.

**muraṇ aḻiya** – to destroy the pride of rāvaṇa, who separated the mother and father \[of all the worlds\]

**mun oru nāl than vil angai vaittān** – he who once kept his beautiful hand on his bow, is the appropriate entity to surrender to. Alternatively, it can be considered that [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/poigai-azhwar/) is saying that he attained such an entity. [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) takes several attempts to protect us. All that is required is to have firm faith in him. Just as sītāppirātti said in śrī rāmāyaṇam yudda kāṇḍam 30-30 “*śaraistu sankulām krutvā lankām parabalātdana:, mām nayēdyadi kākutsta: tat tasya sadruśam bhavēt*” (it will be appropriate for śrī rāma, who is capable of annihilating enemies, to agitate lankā with his arrows and redeem me), we have to trust his sharp arrows. If we do this, he will also carry out deeds with affection towards us just as he did towards sītāppirātti. Only if sītāppirātti showers arrows and protects herself does ātmā too needs to carry out one deed. (In other words, just as sītāppirātti did not make any attempt to protect herself, the ātmā also should not attempt to carry out any deed). pirātti herself would take the role of *puruśakārabhūtai* (recommendatory role) and enable us to attain our desires. Those who think that the ātmā is theirs will suffer the fate of rāvaṇa who tried to make his own, the property of emperumān. The *sangati* \[connection\] between the earlier pāsurams and this pāsuram is that in the earlier pāsurams [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/poigai-azhwar/) mercifully told us to worship emperumān \[58\], recite his divine names \[51\], meditate on him \[41\] etc. In this pāsuram, he tells us that these are not the means to attain him and that emperumān himself is the means. Whatever he had mentioned in the earlier pāsurams are descriptive of the characteristics of the [jīvātmā](https://granthams.koyil.org/2013/03/06/thathva-thrayam-chith-who-am-i/), similar to the ones mentioned in the three *śatkam* (group of 6 chapters) in [śrī bhagavat gītā](http://githa.koyil.org/), viz. explaining the means such as *karma, jñāna* and *bhkti yōgams*  (the paths of deeds, knowledge and devotion respectively). What he has mentioned in this pāsuram is the divine instruction of emperumān in śrī bhagavat gītā 18-66 when he says that whatever he had mentioned earlier in the gītā are not the means but that he himself is the means to attain him. The day the jīvātmā realises his true nature, he would know that there is no means other than emperumān. This is the essence of this pāsuram and this is the essence of this *prabandam*.

Let us move on to the 60<sup>th</sup> pāsuram.

## 60. Carana marai payanda

*caraṇā maṟai payanda tāmaraiyānōdu maraṇāya mannuyigatkellām araṇāya pērāḻi koṇḍa pirān anṟi maṟṟu aṟiyādu ōrāḻi sūḻnda ulagu*

**Пословный перевод:**

caraṇa – being the means for all the four puruśārtams (benefits) \[righteousness, wealth, lust and liberation\]; maṟai – vēdam (sacred texts); payanda – one who was taught; tāmaraiyānōdu – along with brahmā, who was born in the lotus, shooting from emperumān’s navel; maraṇāya – those who have death as a natural phenomenon; man uyirgatku ellām – for all the jivātmās; araṇ āya – those which are protective devices; pērāḻi koṇḍa pirān anṟi – other than emperumān who has the large sudarśana cakkaram (divine disc); maṟṟu – being different (from emperumān); ōr āḻi sūḻnda – surrounded by the unique ocean; ulagu – those who live in this world; aṟīyādu – will not know

**Перевод:**

**caraṇā maṟai payanda tāmaraiyānōdu –  śrī rāmāyaṇam yudda kāṇḍam 120-3 says “brahmā brahmavidām vara:” (brahmā is the greatest among those who know brahmam (supreme entity)) since he revealed to the world, the means for obtaining whatever they needed among the four puruśārtams (benefits) as mentioned in the vēdas (sacred texts). brahmā feels very proud that he gave vēdas to the world \[he thinks\] as mentioned in the usage of the term “mantra krutbhyō nama:” (salutations to the one who composed mantrams) and he thinks that he is very knowledgeable. Along with such brahmā…**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*āḻvār was asked “the benefit of attaining mōkśam (śrīvaikuṇṭam) is with cētana (sentient entity (jīvātmā). When this is the case, the cētana only should observe the means for attaining mōkśam. What is the reason for saying that emperumān is the means?” āḻvār responds in this pāsuram “Only the person who knows everything can perform the means and not one who does not know anything” cētanas do not know what happened in the past; they do not know what they are suffering now; and they do not know what is going to happen in the future. Only emperumān knows these. āḻvār says, as mentioned in nānmugan tiruvandādi – 22 “paḻagiyān” (he is our protector from time immemorial), he is omniscient and has the knowledge as well as the power to remove the troubles that the cētana has suffered in the past, is suffering now and will suffer in future.  Hence he is the means. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**maraṇāya mannuyirgatku ellām** – Even though ātmā (soul) is permanent, since the body dies, [cētanas](https://granthams.koyil.org/2013/03/06/thathva-thrayam-chith-who-am-i/) are referred to as “they died” “they were born” Along with brahmā, who appears at the beginning of a kalpam (this is a period of time extending over several crores of years covering mahāpraḷayam, or the total deluge) and dies at the end of that kalpam, all the species of life including those which are referred to as “*jāyatē mriyantē* ” (born and die instantaneously), without any difference among them, have ignorance and powerlessness in them.

**araṇāya** –  their protection

**pērāḻi koṇḍa pirān anṟi** – other than [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) who has the large sudarśana disc, with which he protects

**maṟṟū aṟiyādu ōrāḻi sūḻnda ulagu** – the earth, surrounded by ocean, does not know anything else. Irrespective of whether they are great people or ordinary people, they will not know what protects them. By rearranging the words of the verse to get “*ulagu pirānaiyanṟi maṟṟu aṟiyādu*” if we can say that world does not know anything other than emperumān, it will not be correct. This is because, as per lexicon “*ulagam enbadu uyarndōr mātte*”(world is the place for  knowledgeable people). But, since the term “*ōrāḻi sūḻnda ulagu*” (world surrounded by ocean) is used here, it would refer to all the people without any distinction between the great and the lowly people. Hence we cannot take it that the term world refers only to great people. Thus, we can say that irrespective of whether they are great or lowly, only emperumān knows the means of protecting all. The [cētanas](https://granthams.koyil.org/2013/03/06/thathva-thrayam-chith-who-am-i/) only know how to develop this samsāram (material realm) and the means for causing them harm; only emperumān knows the means that they must adopt in order to reach *mōkśam* (śrīvaikuṇṭam). As said in the saying “*mūda: ayam nāpi jānāti*” (the ignorant will not know) these cētanas do not know the means.

Once a śrīvaiṣṇava by name āppān enquired of [nanjīyar](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/nanjiyar/) (disciple of [parāsara bhattar](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/parasara-bhattar/)) the meaning for this pāsuram. nanjīyar sought clarification from his teacher, bhattar who gave the aforementioned meanings for the pāsuram. Alternatively, combining with the previous pāsuram where śrī rāma was mentioned, one could say for “*pērāḻi koṇḍa pirān*” the meaning “the benefactor who threatened the ocean and built the dam to lankā”

We shall move on to the 61<sup>st</sup> pāsuram.

## 61. 61 -Ulagum ulagiranda uliyum

*ulagum ulagiṟanda ūḻiyum oṇkēḻ vilagu karungadalum veṟpum ulaginil sendīyum mārudamum vānum tirumāltan pundiyilāya puṇarppu*

**Пословный перевод:**

ulagum – all the worlds; ulagu iṟanda ūḻiyum – time which remains when the world is destroyed \[during deluge\]; oṇ kēḻ vilagu karum kadalum – ocean which is beautiful, throws waves and is black in colour; veṟpum – mountains (which are the supports for the world); ulaginil – seen in the world; sem tīyum – reddish fire; mārudamum – breeze; vānum – sky \[ethereal layer\]; tirumāl than – emperumān’s, who is with mahālakśmi; pundiyil āya – due to his sankalpa (vow or will); puṇarppu – creation

**Перевод:**

**ulagum – worlds or the substances in the worlds**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*In the earlier pāsuram, āḻvār said that samsāris (jīvātmās who inhabit the materialistic realm) do not know what benefits they need and only emperumān knows those. In this pāsuram, to the question as to why emperumān should protect them, āḻvār says that since emperuman sired them, he has to protect them. Other than the one who fathered them, is there anyone who can nurture them? He says that these people not only do not know what is good for them, they do not even know their births. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**ulagu iṟanda ūḻiyum** – time which does not get destroyed and remains when the entire worlds are destroyed during deluge. Alternatively, during deluge, those substances which are in subtle form indicated by time.

**oṇ kēḻ vilagu karum kadalum** – ocean which has beautiful colour, which throws out waves and which is invigorating.

**veṟpum** – the mountains which appear to be holding (sustaining) earth

**ulaginil sem tīyum mārudamum vānum** – all the reddish coloured objects in the world related to light, breeze and sky (ether). [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/poigai-azhwar/) mentions the causative factors by stating these 5 elements.  Alternatively, he is mentioning a few resultant objects.

**tirumāl than pundiyil āya puṇarppu** –  all these have been created for the pastime of [perumāḷ](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) and [pirātti (śrī mahālakśmi)](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sri-mahalakshmi/). āḻvār says that [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) created these to make pirātti happy, through his *sankalpa* (vow).Just as [śrī parāśara bhattar](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/parasara-bhattar/) says in śrī rangarāja stavam 2-88 “*upāyōpēyatvē tadiha* ” (both the means of attaining you and the resultant benefit that you grant are natural to you), emperumān himself is the means and the benefit! He needs our assistance in protecting us only if he needs our assistance when creating us! \[in other words he does not need our assistance\]. emperumān who creates the worlds for pirātti’s enjoyment remains the means for knowing their protection too. [nammāḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/nammazhwar/) in his [tiruvāimoḻi 3-7-8](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2016/10/thiruvaimozhi-3-7-8-nambanai/) says that the one who creates the worlds is emperumān himself “*nambanai jñālam padaittavanai tirumārvanai*”

We shall take up 62<sup>nd</sup> pāsuram next.

## 62. Punar marudinudu

*puṇar marudinūdu pōyp pūngurundam sāyttu maṇam maruva māl vidai ēḻ seṟṟu kaṇam veruva ēḻulagam tāyanavum eṇḍhisaiyum pōyinavum sūḻaravap pongaṇaiyān tōḷ*

**Пословный перевод:**

puṇar marudin – two marudam trees \[a species of trees\] which are joined (together) without any gap; ūdu pōy – crawling between them; pūm kurundam sāyttu – pushing a beautiful kurundam tree \[another species of trees, with beautiful flowers\]; maṇam maruva – in order to marry (nappinnaip pirātti) \[kriṣṇa’s maternal uncle’s daughter\]; māl vidai ēḻ seṟṟu – killing seven huge bulls; kaṇam veruva – making the dēvas (celestial persons) and asuras (demons) get scared as to what would follow; ēḻ ulagum tāyanavum – going past seven worlds; eṇ disaiyum pōyinavum – pervading all 8 directions; sūḻ arava – tiruvanantāḻvān (ādiśēśan) who has spread himself expansively; pongu aṇaiyān – having resplendent mattress; tōḷ – divine shoulders

**Перевод:**

**puṇar maruduin ūdu pōy – going between two marudam trees which have grown such that they appear to be one tree, without any gap between them \[this was to kill two demons who had entered the trees\]**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*āḻvār responding to the query whether these entities, who are created by emperumān, take care of further activities themselves, says in this pāsuram that this is not the case; emperumān himself removes their enemies and protects them. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**pūm kurundam sāyttu** – pushing a kurundam tree which was full of flowers from top to ground level \[here again, a demon had entered the tree. All these demons were waiting for an opportune moment to kill kriṣṇa, on the orders of kamsa\]

**maṇam maruva māl vidai ēḻ seṟṟu** – killing seven huge, cruel bulls in order to marry his maternal uncle’s daughter nappinnai \[nīḷā dēvi as per our sampradāyam\]

**kaṇam veruva ēḻulagum tāyanavum** – viṣṇu darmam says “*sangaissurāṇām divi bhūtalai: tatā manuśyai: gaganē kē carai:, stuta: ya: prakāra sarvadā mamāstu māngaḷya vivruddayē hari:* ” (let that hari, who kept his divine feet and blessed the worlds, who was vorśipped by the celestial persons in *svargam* (heaven), by the human beings on earth and by appropriate persons in the sky, bless me for the improvement of my auspicious life!). Thus, the celestial persons who are well disposed towards [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/), were scared as to what will befall emperumān when he measured the worlds effortlessly. By mentioning about the trees being broken or pushed, the way he removed danger to himself was mentioned. By mentioning about the bulls, the danger to nappinnaip pirātti was mentioned. In a similar vein, the way the danger that fell on indra (head of celestial entities) was removed, is being mentioned next.

**eṇ disaiyum pōyinavum** – spreading in the eight directions

**sūḻ aravap pongu aṇaiyān tōḷ** – [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) who is supposed to sleep on the soft mattress provided by *tiruvanantāḻvān* (ādiśēśan)  who, by himself has qualities such as strength, fragrance, comfort and softness, carried out all the above-mentioned activities! His divine shoulders did all these! emperumān, who has such softness that his divine body cannot tolerate that, even if he lies on such soft mattress, carried out all these activities! [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/poigai-azhwar/) is desolate that instead of lying comfortably on his mattress, he is carrying out all these deeds. In śrī rāmāyaṇam ayōdyā kāṇḍam 2-5, daśarata cakravarty says “*pāṇḍurasya ātapatrasya cāyāyām jariratam mayā*” (my physical body has withered under the royal umbrella). Since he was constantly moving about to protect his country, he had no time to rest on his throne. Only when he sits on his throne could his servants hold the pearl umbrella for him. He did not have time even for that and was busy protecting his kingdom. āḻvār says that in the same way, emperumān, without any time for resting on ādiśēśan, was busy removing the enemies of his followers.

Let us consider the 63<sup>rd</sup> pāsuram next.

## 63. Tol avanai allal tola

*tōḷ avanai allāl toḻā en sevi iraṇḍum kēḷ avanadu inmoḻiyē kēttirukkum nānāḷum kōṇāgaṇaiyān kurai kaḻalē kūṟuvadē nāṇāmai naḷḷēn nayam*

**Пословный перевод:**

tōḷ – (my) hands; avanai allāl toḻā – will not worship anyone other than him \[emperumān\]; en sevi iraṇḍum – my two ears; kēḷ avanadu – his, who is all relationships (to me); in moḻiyē – sweet words; kēttu irukkum – will hear and sustain themselves; nā – my tongue; nāḷum – everyday; kōḷ nāgaṇaiyān – one who has his mattress, the radiant ādiśēśan; kurai kaḻalē – (due to the sounds of ornaments) resounding divine feet; kūṟuvadē – will keep saying; nayam – the matters desired (by samsāris); nāṇāmai naḷḷēn – (I) will not approach, without any shame

**Перевод:**

**tōḷ avanai allāl toḻā – my shoulders, even if told to worship someone else, will not worship anyone other than emperumān. It does not matter whether emperumān protects or does not protect, my shoulders will not worship anybody other than emperumān.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*āḻvār says that his sensory perceptions as well as himself are now fully engrossed with emperumān who, not minding his soft nature, transforms himself completely, in order to carry out tasks for his followers. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**en sevi iraṇḍum kēḷ avanadu inmoḻiyē kēttirukkum** – my ears will listen only to [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/)‘s sweet words since he is naturally related to me, and will sustain themselves due to this.

**nā nāḷum** – my tongue, will always

**kōḷ nāgaṇaiyān kurai** – emperumān who has ādiśēśan, who has strength and radiance, as his mattress. ādiśēśan has the skill to sustain the union of [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) and [pirātti (śrī mahālakśmi)](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sri-mahalakshmi/). [āḷavandār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/alavandhar/) says of ādiśēśan in his [stōtra ratnam – 39](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2017/01/sthothra-rathnam-39/) “*prakruśta vijñāna balaikadāmani*” (tiruvanantāaḻvān who is the repository of both sharp intelligence and strength). He gets the radiance due to his connection with emperumān. [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/nammazhwar/) says that his tongue will sing the praises of the divine feet of emperumān who has such ādiśēśan as his mattress.

**nāṇāmai naḷḷēn nayam** – the people of this world shamelessly desire worldly pursuits! [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/nammazhwar/) says that he will not, so shamelessly, desire such objects. These objects are such that one has to withdraw from them with shame, once true knowledge has been obtained \[about [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/)\]. Following the worldly pursuits is much similar to following a eunuch, who is much inferior to a male, thinking that it is a female, in the darkness of the moon.

We shall  take up the 64<sup>th</sup> pāsuram next.

## 64. Nayaven pirar porulai

*nayavēn piṟar poruḷai naḷḷēn kīḻārōdu uyavēn uyarndavarōdu allāl viyavēn tirumālai alladu deyvam enṟu ēttēn varumāṟu en enmēl vinai*

**Пословный перевод:**

piṟar poruḷai – ātmā, which is the possession of distinguished emperumān; nayavēn – I will not desire as mine; kīḻārōdu – with samsāris (dwellers of materialistic realm who think that ātmās are their own); naḷḷēn – I will not move closely; uyarndavarōdu allāl uyavēn – I will not speak to anyone other than śrīvaiṣṇavas (who are servitors of emperumān); tirumālai alladu – other than śrī mahālakśmi’s consort; deyvam enṟu ēttēn – I will not respect as deity; en mēl – on me (having qualities as mentioned above); vinai varum āṟu en – how will sins come?

**Перевод:**

**nayavēn piṟar poruḷai – I will not desire other’s possession as mine. Has it not been said in mahābhāratam udyāna parvam 42-35 “yō(a)nyatā santamātmānam anyatā pratipadyatē, kim tēna na krutam pāpam cōrēṇa ātmāpahāriṇā” (what sin has not been committed by the one who thinks that ātmā (soul), which is the property of emperumān, is his own and steals such ātmā?)! It is said that if the material which is stolen is of high value and is stolen from an exalted person such as brāhmaṇa, the sin which accrues due to that will also be very grave. Here, when the object which is stolen happens to be ātmā, and the person from whom it is stolen happens to be emperumān, will not the resultant sin be very grave? Hence, I will not think of emeprumān’s possession of ātmā as mine, says āḻvār.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*When asked as to how he could so boldly say that he will not desire worldly pursuits and what he would do if they come on their own and beseech him, āḻvār explains his position and says “when this is my position how can worldly pursuits beseech me? Appropriating ātmā which is emperumān’s possession as my own, associating with non-followers \[of emperumān\], not associating with followers, reducing engagement with emperumān, losing faith in pirātti \[śrī mahālakśmi\]’s recommendatory nature etc are the causative factors for accruing sins. When I do not have any of these, how will the worldly pursuits chase me?” Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**naḷḷēn kīḻārōdu** – I will not get close to people who think that of others’ properties as their own.

**uyavēn uyarndavrōdu allāl** – I will not speak to anyone other than those who think that great objects are indeed great. When [āḻvārs](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/azhwars/) talk about followers of [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/), they will refer to them as great persons only. They will refer to samsāris as lowly persons only. īsn’t this the real reason for distinction between great and lowly persons!

**viyavēn tirumālai alladu** – I will not wonder at the greatness of anyone other than the consort of *tirumagaḷ* (śrī mahālakśmi)

**tirumālai alladu deyvam enṟu ēttēn** – I will not think of anyone other than the consort of *tirumagaḷ* as being appropriate for holding on to and vorśipping. How can sins come to such a person as I am, described above; sins which have to be got rid of, only by experiencing their results? brahmavaivartam, prakruti kāṇḍam 26-70 says “*avaśyam anubhōktavyam krutam karma śubhāśubham, nābhuktam kśīyatē kalpakōti śatairapi* ” (one has to experience the results of good and bad deeds. These results will not get negated if not experienced, even after hundreds of kalpa kālams (one kalpa kālam is the lifetime of brahmā which runs to millions of years)). The bad deed carried out by a [jīvātmā](https://granthams.koyil.org/2013/03/06/thathva-thrayam-chith-who-am-i/) creates anger in [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/)’s divine mind and that results in sin for that ātmā; there is no separate ability for the deeds carried out by the jīvātmā. If for any reason emperumān becomes happy with this jīvātmā, there will be no place for these deeds \[they will disappear\]. These deeds cannot attain him again for other reasons too.

**varumāṟu en enmēḷ vinai** – just as a person who has antidote for poison has the courage to thrust his hand into a snake’s mouth, vouldn’t those who have knowledge about [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) not have the courage not to be scared of sins?

**varumāṟu en enmēḷ vinai** – [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/poigai-azhwar/) asks if an ignorant act would befall him when he has attained an omnipotent entity such as emperumān. Only if others have rights over him would he himself have rights over him! In the same way, just as being a servitor to others (than emperumān) is removed, being totally independent to the extent of saying that one is one’s own \[and not dependent on emperumān\] is also removed. Since [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) forgets the sins committed by us in the past \[after we have surrendered to him\], our previous sins get destroyed. Since he is unaware \[he chooses to be\] of our sins in future, such sins will also not stick to us. Hence both the past sins and the future ones will not affect us. These words were mercifully explained by [nanjīyar](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/nanjiyar/) to tiruvāykkulattu āḻvān.

We shall take up the 65<sup>th</sup> pāsuram next.

## 65. 65 -Vinaiyal adarppadar

*vinaiyāḷ adarppadār vennaragil sērār tinaiyēnum tīkkadikkaṇ sellār ninaitaṟ kariyānaic cēyānai āyiram pēr sengat kariyānaik kai toḻudakkāl*

**Пословный перевод:**

ninaidaṟku ariyānai – one who would not know (of his own effort); sēyānai – one who is far away; āyiram pēr sem kaṇ kariyānai – emperumān who has a thousand divine names, has reddish, divine eyes and who has a dark divine form; kai toḻudakkāl – if vorśipped with hands; vinaiyāl adarppadār – will not be troubled by bad deeds; vem naragil sērār – will not go to the horrible hell; tinaiyēnum – even to a small extent \[almost zero probability\]; tīkkadikkaṇ sellār – will not go astray

**Перевод:**

**vinaiyāl adarppadār – they \[persons who are devoted to emperumān\] will not be troubled by deeds such as pāpa (sin) or puṇya (virtue)**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*āḻvār says in this pāsuram that such a position \[of not going after worldly pursuits\] is applicable not only to him but to everyone who is his follower. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**vennaragail sērār** – for the sins committed, they will not be born in this materialistic realm. Alternatively, one can construe that *vinaiyāl adarppadār* would mean that the persons will not suffer in this world and *vennaragil sērār* would mean that the persons will not suffer in hell.

**tinaiyēnum tīkkadikkan sellār** – they will not go astray. darmaputra \[eldest among the five pāṇḍavas\] had to step into hell for a few minutes because he did not believe kriṣṇa who had asked him to tell a lie, in order to kill drōṇa, that aśvattāma is dead and instead said that an elephant by name aśvattāma died. Even that result will not come to these people, says [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/poigai-azhwar/).

When asked as to by doing what all these would happen, āḻvār says

**ninaidaṟku ariyānai** – one who is difficult to even think of, for those who want to know him through their own efforts.

**sēyānai** – when asked why he is difficult to think of, [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/poigai-azhwar/) says that it is so because he is at a very far distance from such people.

If he cannot be approached should one lose him? āḻvār says

**āyiram pēr sengaṇ kariyānaik kai toḻudak kāl** – for those who are able to enjoy [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) with the knowledge that he had granted them. emperumān is like a river which has ghats at every place where one steps in to take a bath \[this means that the river is not too deep in those places and one can enjoy a bath on such places\]. Those who can enjoy emperumān who has thousands of divine names for his auspicious qualities and deeds, who has reddish eyes due to deep affection for his followers and who has the form which, like an ocean, can remove the sorrows of those who are able to see him, will not be troubled by bad deeds; will not attain hell; will not go astray. Just as a disease is rid of by consuming milk, all the sins will disappear by carrying the sweet action of vorśipping emperumān.

We shall take up the 66<sup>th</sup> pāsuram next.

## 66. Kalai elundu

*kālai eḻundu ulagam kaṟpanavum kaṟṟuṇarnda mēlait talaimaṟaiyōr vētpanavum vēlaikkkaṇ ōrāḻiyān adiyē ōduvadum ōrppanavum pērāḻi koṇḍān peyar*

**Пословный перевод:**

kālai eḻundu – waking up in the morning (which nurtures the satva guṇam (purely good quality)); ulagam – the exalted mumukśus (those who are desirous of attaining mōkśam, śrīvaikuṇṭam); kaṟpanavum – learning; kaṟṟu uṇarnda mēlait talai maṟaiyōr – the well versed, great vaidikas (learned in vēdas, sacred texts); vētpanavum – desiring to see; vēlaikkaṇ – in the tirupāṟkadal (milky ocean); ōr āḻiyān adiyē – the divine feet of emperumān who is reclining, holding the incomparable sudarśana cakkaram (divine disc); ōduvadum – being listened to (by these great people); ōrppanavum – thinking of; pēr āḻi koṇḍān peyar – the divine names of the entity who holds the large disc

**Перевод:**

**kālai eḻundu ulagam kaṟpanavum – early morning \[4 am\] is the time when it is most appropriate for great people to wake up and learn, since satva guṇam (purely good quality) is at its peak at this time. nammāḻvār says in tiruvāimoḻi 2-10-1 “kiḷaroḷi iḷamai keduvadan munnam” (before the radiant youth gets wasted) and in tiruviruttam 93 “kālai nanjñānattuṟai padindādi” (in the early hours after having a bath, approach ācāryan and learn about emperumān). emperumān in his varāha caramaślōkam says “stitē manasi susvastē śarīrē sati yō nara: dātu sāmyē stitē smartā viśvarūpanca māmajam” (when the mind is calm and composed, the body is in good health, with the three constituent elements of the body in proper balance, when someone thinks of me who am having the entire universe as my body and who am having no births…) Thus in the morning hours, the body and mind are in composed state for doing sātvik deeds\].**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*āḻvār asks in this pāsuram: if people have knowledge, is it possible to remain without vorśipping him? Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**kaṟṟuṇarnda mēlait talai maṟaiyōr vētpanavum** – what the people, who have good knowledge about emperumān, wish to see joyously

**vēlaikkaṇ ōr āḻiyān adiyē** – is the divine \[pair of\] feet of emperumān who is reclining on the milky ocean which has shore and is very expansive. \[in this interpretation, the term *vēlai* denotes shore\]. If *vēlai*  were to mean ocean itself, āḻiyān will mean disc and the verse would refer to emperumān who is having the disc which is used for protecting the worlds.

**ōduvadum ōrppanavum pērāḻi koṇḍān peyar** – the means to attain emperumān’s divine feet and to think of him are his divine names. emperumān has the complexion of ocean which removes all sorrows.

We shall take up the 67<sup>th</sup> pāsuram next.

## 67. Peyarum karum kadale

*peyarum karum kadalē nōkkum āṟu oṇpū uyarum kadiravanē nōkkum uyirum darumanaiyē nōkkum oṇ tāmaraiyāḷ kēḷvan oruvanaiyē nōkkum uṇarvu*

**Пословный перевод:**

āṟu – river; peyarum karum kadalē nōkkum – will flow only towards the dark complexioned ocean which keeps throwing out waves; oṇ pū – the radiant lotus flower; uyarum kadiravanē nōkkum – will blossom only after looking at sun, who is at a high place; uyirum – ātmā (of avaiṣṇavas (those who are not followers of viṣṇu, emperumān)); darumanaiyē nōkkum – (at the end of its life on earth) will attain only yama, the deity of righteousness; uṇarvu – knowledge; oṇ tāmaraiyāḷ oruvanaiyē – only nārāyaṇa, consort of śrī mahālakśmi who dwells on the radiant lotus flower; nōkkum – will know

**Перевод:**

**peyarum karum kadalē nōkkum āṟu – if it is a river, it will be flowing fully and will flow only towards ocean which keeps throwing out waves. Rivers do not flow towards oceans to fill them, but because they cannot remain without joining oceans.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*In the earlier pāsuram it was mentioned that those who have desire for mōkśam (śrīvaikuṇṭam) and who have knowledge \[about emperumān\] will praise his divine feet and recite his divine names. Why is there such a compulsion? It is because the people mentioned here are those with knowledge. āḻvār says in this pāsuram that since there is no target for knowledge other than emperumān, the consort of tirumagaḷ (śrī mahālakśmi), only he can be praised. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**oṇ pū uyarum kadiravanē nōkkum** – beautiful lotus flower will not blossom without looking at sun, however far sun may be. It will not blossom by looking at any other entity. Even if hot embers are placed next to it, the lotus will not blossom.

**uyirum darumanaiyē nōkkum** – most of the ātmās will be under the control of only yama, the lord of righteousness. Other than śrīvaiṣṇavas,  whom even yama will avoid going nearby saying “they are not under my control”, all the others are under his control.

**oṇ tāmaraiyāḷ kēḷvan oruvanaiyē nōkkum uṇarvu** – in the same way, knowledge will be devoted only to the consort of *tirumagaḷ* (śrī mahalakśmi). śrīviṣṇu purāṇam 6-5-87 says “*samjñāyatē yēna tadasdōśam śuddam param nirmalamēkarūpam, sandruśyatē vā’pyati gamyatē vā tat jñānamajñāna matō’nya duktam*”(that knowledge with which the faultless, pure, incomparable supreme entity is known, seen and attained, that alone is *jñānam* (knowledge). All the rest are *ajñānam* (ignorance)). For the lotus called [ātmā](https://granthams.koyil.org/2013/03/06/thathva-thrayam-chith-who-am-i/), [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) and [pirātti](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sri-mahalakshmi/) are the sun and its rays which blossom the knowledge. Usage of the emphatic syllable “*E*” (*oruvanaiyē*) implies that ātmā does not have sustenance without emperumān. ātmā is inseparable from [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/)’s divine feet like the lines and shadow of the feet.

We shall move on to the 68<sup>th</sup> pāsuram next.

## 68. Unarvar ar

*uṇarvār ār un perumai ūḻidōṟūḻi uṇarvār ār un uruvam tannai uṇarvār ār viṇṇagattāi maṇṇagattāi vēngadattāi nālvēdap paṇṇagattāi nī kidanda pāl*

**Пословный перевод:**

viṇṇagattāy – Oh one who is dwelling in śrīvaikuṇṭam!; maṇṇagattāy – Oh one who incarnated in this samsāram (materialistic realm); vēngadattāy – Oh one who is standing in tiruvēngadam!; paṇ – having musical intonation as the most important part; nāl vēda agattāy – Oh one who is flourishing in the sacred texts!; un perumai – your greatness; uṇarvār Ar – who will know?; ūḻi tōṟu ūḻi – in every kalpam \[brahmā’s life time running to millions of years\]; un uruvam tannai – your svarūpam (basic nature) and rūpam (divine form); uṇarvār Ar – who will know?; nī kidanda pāl – the milky ocean where you are reclining; uṇarvār Ar – who will know (by measuring)?

**Перевод:**

**viṇṇagattāy – Oh one who is dwelling with all your greatness in paramapadam (śrīvaikuṇṭam)!**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*Other than emperumān, there is no refuge for knowledge. Despite this, there is none who knows him, says āḻvār in this pāsuram. Let us enjoy the pāsuram and its meanings.*

**viṇṇagattāy uṇarvār Ar un perumai** – “*yō asyādyakśa: paramē vyōman sō anga vēda yadi vā na vēda*” (will the one who is the lord of all the worlds and who resides in paramapadam, know his greatness himself?) When vēdas (sacred texts) doubt whether you know your greatness, will anyone else know?

**maṇṇagattāy uṇarvār Ar un perumai** – will anyone know your greatness after you descended from paramapadam and took many incarnations in this samsāram to exhibit your simplicity? Even if one gets close to the ocean, can he measure its depth? As said in puruśa sūktam “*tasya dīrā: parijānanti yōnim*” (only wise people know his birth), while vēdam said that some wise people know about him, even these people who have been blessed by great knowledge and great devotion (such as [āḻvārs](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/azhwars/)) by [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) himself, will start getting bewildered, looking at his simplicity. maṇṇagattāy implies all the materialistic realm \[and not our earth alone\].

**vēngadattāy uṇarvār Ar un perumai** – will anyone know your greatness as you stand in tiruvēngadam, in order to redeem all the persons in materialistic realm, being under the control of *arcakas* \[in arcāvatāram (as idol/deity form in many places) he is under the control of the arcakas who are the persons appointed to conduct daily worship in the temples, arranging various rituals through the year\], with all your svarūpa (basic characteristics), rūpa (divine form), guṇa (auspicious qualities), vibhūti (wealth) etc, being common to both nityasūris and samsāris, exhibiting both your greatness and your simplicity?

**nāl vēdap paṇṇagattāy uṇarvār Ar un perumai** – vēda puruśan (vēdam, sacred scripture) says “*vēdāhamētam puruśam mahāntam*” (I knew this supreme being). When vēdam was asked how it knew [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/), it said that he is *mahāntam* (he is so huge that it is not possible to measure him)

**nālvēdap paṇṇagattāy** – vēdas which have musical intonations called *udāttam* and *anudāttam* said that he cannot be measured

**uṇarvārār un perumai** – who can know you, who are having auspicious qualities such as greatness, simplicity etc?

**uṇarvārār un uruvam tannai** – who can know the greatness of your svarūpam (basic nature) which is the repository of your auspicious qualities? Alternatively, who can know the greatness of your *divya mangaḷa vigraham* (the divine, auspicious form) which exhibits such auspicious qualities?

**ūḻidōṟūḻi aṟivār Ar** – It is not for a short time that someone is trying to measure your qualities. Even if they were given time of brahmā’s lifespan, can they measure you and know you? [nammāḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/nammazhwar/) in his [tiruvāimoiḻi 4-3-10](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2017/01/thiruvaimozhi-4-3-10-yanum-eththi/) says “*yānum ētti ēḻulagum muṟṟum ēttip pinnaiyum tānum ēttilum tannai ētta ētta engeydum*” (it is not possible to find the end,  if [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/poigai-azhwar/) praises him, all the people in all the worlds praise him and [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) himself praises himself, viz. no one can know him).

**nī kidanda pāl uṇarvār Ar** – while measuring your basic nature and auspicious qualities would be very difficult, has anyone measured a drop of your qualities when you were reclining in your milky ocean, waiting for your followers to call you for overcoming their troubles?

Thus [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/poigai-azhwar/) says that [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/)’s basic nature, his auspicious qualities, his wealth etc cannot be measured by anyone.

We shall take up the 69<sup>th</sup> pāsuram next.

## 69. Balan tanadu uruvay

*bālan tanadu uruvāy ēḻulaguṇḍu ālilaiyin mēl anṟu nī vaḷarndadu mey enbar ālanṟu vēlai nīr uḷḷadō viṇṇadō maṇṇadō sōlai sūḻ kunṟu eduttāy sollu*

**Пословный перевод:**

bālan tanadu uruvāy – having the form of a child; ēḻ ulagu uṇḍu – swallowing the seven worlds; Al ilayin mēl – on top of a banyan leaf; anṟu – during deluge; nī vaḷarnda – your sleeping; mey enbar – (vaidikas, experts in vēdas) would say (that it) is true; anṟu – during that time; Al – that banyan leaf; vēlai nīr – inside the oceanic water (where you had slept); uḷḷadō – was it?; viṇṇadō – was it in the sky (which does not have a support)?; maṇṇadō – was it in the (dissolved) earth?; sōlai sūḻ kunṟu eduttāy sollu – one who lifted the (gōvardana) hill surrounded by gardens; sollu – only (you) have to tell

**Перевод:**

**bālan tanadu uruvāy – he was so small that if milk falls on one cheek, it will fill up the other cheek!**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*In the earlier pāsuram, āḻvār said that others cannot know about emperumān. In this pāsuram he is asking emperumān whether he \[emperumān\] knows about himself. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**ēḻ ulagu uṇḍu** – keeping the seven worlds inside the stomach \[here seven implies all the worlds other than his *nityavibhūti*, śrīvaikuṇṭam\]

**Al ilayin mēl** – on top of a banyan leaf which formed just then

**anṟu nī vaḷarnda** – that you had rested on that day

**mey enbar** – great people will say that this was true and not any act of magic

**bālan tandu uruvāy** – even though he was in a form (as an infant) needing someone else’s protection, he protected the worlds. He contracted his form that is transcendental \[emperumṇa’s divine form, unlike ours, is not composed of the five elements but is composed of *upaniśats*) and became an infant.

**ēḻulagu uṇḍu** – without any discrimination (that he likes some and dislikes the others) he kept all the entities, other than himself, in his stomach, and on top of the banyan leaf which was smaller than his form . . . .

**anṟu nī vaḷarnda mey enbar** – the riśis (sages) said that it is true that you \[[emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/)\] slept on top of a banyan leaf on the day when there was total annihilation

**mey enbar** –  For men of truth, this will appear as truth and for the others, it will appear as false. While the entire universe was under deluge, and there was flood everywhere, when there was not a piece of straw as far as the eye could see, where was that banyan leaf – was it on top of the ocean or was it on the unsupported sky \[ether\] or was it in the dissolved earth? If one were to ask where it was, it appears as if [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) wanted to hear it from [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/poigai-azhwar/) himself.

**sōlai sūḻ kunṟu eduttāy sollu** – one has to mention the wondrous activity of your \[[emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/)\] lifting the hill, supporting all the objects which were on the hill. The opinion here is that his ability to do the impossible acts can only be seen and enjoyed, and cannot be examined \[by way of how he did it\]. [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/poigai-azhwar/) says that nowhere else can one see the objects being supported and the person supporting them in the same entity. Thus, āḻvār says that he himself cannot explain his qualities. āḻvār says that it was because of him that the earth, which was the support for the banyan leaf on which he was lying, did not dissolve in the deluge.

We shall take up the 70<sup>th</sup> pāsuram next.

## 70. Sollum tanaiyum

*sollum tanaiyum toḻumin viḻum udambu sellum tanaiyum tirumālai nal idaḻt tāmattāl vēḷviyāl tandirattāl mandirattāl nāmattāl ēttudirēl nanṟu*

**Пословный перевод:**

tirumālai – consort of śrī mahālakśmi; sollum tanaiyum – until you are capable of reciting; toḻumin – worship him; viḻum udambu – the \[physical\] body which will fall naturally; sellum tanaiyum – until it lasts; nal idaḻt tāmattāl – with a garland which has beautiful petals of flowers; vēḷviyāl – with yāgam (religious ritual); tandirattāl – with activities \[not involving hymns\]; mandirattāl – with hymns \[not involving actions\]; nāmattāl – with his divine names; ēttudirēl – if you \[attain and\] praise him; nanṟu – will yield benefits

**Перевод:**

**āḻvār says that this physical body has to get destroyed one day. Until it falls, worship tirumāl (the consort of śrī mahālakśmi\] with garland containing flowers having beautiful petals or with rituals or with deeds. Until you have the capability, recite hymn or his divine names.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*āḻvār says that since emperumān carries out impossible deeds, all the people in the world, without engaging with any other activity, should attain him. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**sollum tanaiyum** – until the faculty of speech is in order

**viḻum udambu sellum tanaiyum** – until this physical body which is unstable, falls down

**nallidaḻ tāmattāl** – with garlands containing well blossomed flowers

**vēḷviyāl** – when good flowers are not available \[being seasonal in nature\], carrying out deeds such as *yāgam* etc which are ordained

**tandirattāl** – if it is not possible to carry out both mandiram (mantram) and tandiram (tantram), carrying out only deeds \[without singing hymns\]

**mandirattāl** – if it is not possible to carry out deeds, recite only hymns

**nāmattāl** – since vēdas have ordained specific place, time, the qualified person etc for reciting divine names, one should recite [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/)’s divine names . Who should be praised this way?

**tirumālai** – the consort of [śrī mahālakśmi](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sri-mahalakshmi/) who is our appropriate lord. Are there any customs and practices to recite one’s parent’s names?

[āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/poigai-azhwar/) says that when [pirātti](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sri-mahalakshmi/), who will magnify even small activities of [jīvātmā](https://granthams.koyil.org/2013/03/06/thathva-thrayam-chith-who-am-i/) when he comes to offer his praises to [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/), is there with him, what is the hurdle for carrying this out?

We shall take up the 71<sup>st</sup> pāsuram next.

## 71. Nanru pini muppu

*nanṟu piṇi mūppuk kaiyagaṟṟi nāngūḻi ninṟu nilam muḻudum āṇḍālum enṟum vidal āḻi nenjamē vēṇḍinēn kaṇḍāy adal āḻi koṇḍān māttu anbu*

**Пословный перевод:**

āḻi nenjamē – Oh heart, deep like the ocean!; piṇi mūppu nanṟu kai agaṟṟi – getting rid of disease and old age, well (and attaining kaivalyam); nāngu ūḻi ninṟu – being stable for four yugas (several eons); nilam muḻudum āṇḍālum – even if one were to get all the wealth to rule all the worlds; adal āḻi koṇḍān māttu – the one who has the belligerent divine disc on his hands; anbu – friendliness; vidal – do not let go of; vēṇḍinēn kaṇḍāy – behold, I am beseeching

**Перевод:**

**nanṟu piṇi mūppuk kaiyagaṟṟi – getting rid of diseases, old age etc very well. This implies kaivalyam (a stage in which ātmā enjoys itself).**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*āḻvār tells his heart in this pāsuram, “let the worldly people do whatever they want. Oh heart! you do not let go of emperumān ever”. Just as nammāḻvār says in tiruvāimoḻi 8-10-2 “viyan mūvulagum pōyt tānē tānē ānālum” (even if one were to get all the wealth in all the worlds and also kaivaylam (ātmā enjoying itself instead of carrying out service to emperumān)), āḻvār tells his heart not to have desire for the lowly benefits and worship emperumān. Let us enjoy the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**nāngu ūḻi ninṟu nilam muḻudum āṇḍālum** – even if one were to rule the universe until the entity time exists (several eons). This implies the wealth of entities such as *brahmā* (the deity for creation). Thus if one were to get both *aiśvaryam* (wealth) and *kaivalyam* (ātmā enjoying itself)….

**enṟum vidal** – never let go of the affection towards [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/). *aiśvaryam* (wealth) is transient. Compared to service to emperumān, *kaivlayam* is very lowly. The very fact that these two cannot equal emperumān by any yardstick should be sufficient to discard them. In the same vein, it is enough to worship emperumān alone.

**āḻi nenjamē vēṇḍinēn kaṇḍāy** – just as one has to beg another person for drinking milk, am I not praying to you to carry out a sweet task!

**āḻi nenjē** – while you have already taken steps to worship [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/), am I not beseeching you?

**adal āḻi koṇḍān māttu anbu vidal** – do not let go of the affection that you have on emperumān, who is holding the belligerent divine disc which is raring to spit fire on enemies. emperumān is such an entity that he should be attained, even if one has to effect a payment \[like a sambhāvaṇai\].

**āḻi nenjē** – I am telling you, who are capable of advising me, says the āḻvār.

Let us consider the 72<sup>nd</sup> pāsuram next.

## 72. Anbaliyanai

*anbāḻiyānai aṇugennum nā avantan paṇbāḻit tōḷ paravi ēttennum munbūḻi kāṇānaik kān ennum kaṇ sevi kēḷ ennum pūṇāram pūṇḍān pugaḻ*

**Пословный перевод:**

anbu – the mind which is full of love; āḻiyānai aṇugu ennum – will instruct to approach cakrapāṇi (emperumān, who has the divine disc in his hand); nā – tongue; avan than paṇbu āḻi tōḷ paravi ēttennum – will instruct to worship his divine shoulders which are like beautiful ocean; munbu ūḻi kāṇānai kāṇ ennum kaṇ – eyes will instruct to worship emperumān who does not see the time of eons; sevi – the ears; pūṇ āram pūṇḍān pugaḻ kēḷ ennum – will instruct to hear the praise of emperumān who has donned the decorative pearl necklace

**Перевод:**

**anbu āḻiyānai aṇugu ennum – mind is such that it is an embodiment of affection which is as deep as the ocean. Thus, instead of calling it as mind, āḻvār is referring to it as anbu which means affection. Such a mind is instructing āḻvār to approach and attain emperumān. In the earlier pāsuram, āḻvār instructed his heart \[the terms heart and mind are interchangeably used in all these pāsurams\] “adalāḻi koṇḍān māttu anbu vidal” (Oh dear heart! Do not let go of the affection towards emperumān). Looking at āḻvār, his heart tells him “do not worry about me. Please do not let go of emperumān”.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*āḻvār’s sensory perceptions went ahead of him and engaged with emperumān. They started instructing āḻvār “āḻvīr! Do not let go of emperumān!”. In the earlier pāsuram, āḻvār stood in the position of guru (teacher) and instructed his sensory perceptions. In this pāsuram, his sensory perceptions instructed him on the same matter, in the position of guru. Thus the guru-śiśya (teacher-student) order has got reversed in this pāsuram. Let us enjoy the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**āḻiyān** – just as one is engrossed with the bracelets worn by princes on their shoulders and forearms, the heart is engrossed with the beauty of the [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) who is having the divine disc on his hand.

**nā avantan paṇbu āḻit tōḷ paravi ēttu ennum** – my tongue will instruct me to worship, even if in an disorderly way, his simplicity and strong shoulders.

**āḻittōḷ** – For the word *āḻi i*nstead of the meaning “strong”, if one were to consider the meaning “ocean”, we can say that his divine shoulders are like the beautiful ocean.

**munbu ūḻi kāṇānaik kāṇ ennum kaṇ** – eyes will instruct me to worship [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) who has not seen the times of eons. My eyes do not know whether that is possible or not.

**munbu ūḻik kāṇān** – Are we not those who have committed faults? We do not have to fear as how we will attain emperumān. [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/poigai-azhwar/) says that emperumān will forget the times when we carried out such offences. If he thinks of those times, vouldn’t he be reminded of our offences too? Hence, along with our faults, he forgets the times too, when we committed such faults. ḍidn’t [nammāḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/nammazhwar/) mercifully say in his tiruvāimoḻi 2-7-3 “*mādavan enṟadē koṇḍu ennai ini ippāl pattadu yādavangaḷum sērkodēn enṟu*” (Since I merely said *mādava*, [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) vowed that he will not allow any difficulties to come to me for all times hereafter)! We would know whether he is looking at us with anger or with affection in his eyes when he sees us. Hence eyes will say “look at him”.

**sevi kēḷ ennum pūṇ āram pūṇḍān pugaḻ** – my eyes will tell me “even if he sees our fault, why cannot you hear the praise of emperumān who is beautiful, with such ornaments?”

**pūṇ āram** – decorative ornaments for the divine physical form which, by itself, is like an ornament.  ḍidn’t hanuman say in śrī rāmāyaṇam kiśkintā kāṇḍam “*sarvabhūśaṇa bhūśārhā:*” (divine shoulders which will decorate all the ornaments)!

**pūṇḍān** – beauty which is to be decorated.

**pugaḻ** – isn’t praising emperumān an ornament for [ātmā](https://granthams.koyil.org/2013/03/06/thathva-thrayam-chith-who-am-i/)!

We shall consider the 73<sup>rd</sup> pāsuram next.
