## 73. Pugalvay palippay

*pugaḻvāy paḻippāy nī pūnduḻāyānai igaḻvāy karuduvāy nenjē tigaḻnīrk kadalum malaiyum iruvisumbum kāṟṟum udalum uyirum ēṟṟān*

**Пословный перевод:**

nenjē – Oh my heart!; nī – you; pūm tuḻāyānai – one decorated with beautiful tuḷasi (basil) garland; pugaḻvāy – may praise; paḻippāy – may abuse; igaḻvāy – may disparage; karuduvāy – may think \[good\]; tigaḻ nīr kadalum – ocean with flourishing water; malaiyum – resultant factors such as mountains; iru visumbum kāṟṟum – causative factors such as the expansive sky (ether), wind etc; udalum uyirum – having both śarīram (physical body) and ātmā (soul); ēṟṟān – has always sustained

**Перевод:**

**pugaḻvāy paḻippāy – whether you praise or abuse emperumān who is the object for the faculty of speech.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*āḻvār says in this pāsuram as to how engrossed he became with emperumān, such that even the sensory perceptions became obedient. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**pūnduḻāyānai** – he is so beautiful adorning tuḷasi garland that it is impossible not to praise him.

**igaḻvāy karuduvāy nenjē** – Oh my heart! Haven’t you seen the status of [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/)! Whether you think well or disparagingly of such a lowly entity (here the reference to emperumān as lowly entity is mentioned in the opposite sense. In other words, he is a great entity), oh heart! don’t you know which of these is correct!

**tigaḻ nīrkkadalum malaiyum iruvisumbum kāṟṟum udalum uyirum ēṟṟān** – [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/poigai-azhwar/) tells his mind that irrespective of whatever it does, there is none other than emperumān to attain.

**tigaḻ nīrkkadalum malaiyum** – by mentioning ocean and mountains āḻvār is implying all the resultant entities.

**iruvisumbum kāṟṟum** – by mentioning sky (ether) and wind, [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/poigai-azhwar/) is referring to all the five elements which are the causative factors.

**udalum uyirum** – he is referring to the acit and cit (insentient and sentient entities) which are again, resultant entities.

**udalum uyirum ēṟṟān** – [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) sustains all these. Thus, it is implied that all the entities mentioned above are emperumān’s resultant possessions and he is the owner for all these. bruhadāraṇyaka upaniśat 5-7-22 says “*yasyātmā śarīram yasya prutivī śarīram*” (for whom the jīvātmā also is body, for whom the world is also body). Attain either emperumān who is sustaining all the entities or sustain the entities which are being sustained by emperumān. Even if one looks at the quality that he is the protector, isn’t he the entity to be attained!

**udalum uyirum ēṟṟān** – isn’t he the entity who is implied by all these words!

Let us take up the 74<sup>th</sup> pāsuram next.

## 74. Erran pullurndan

*ēṟṟān puḷḷūrndān eyil erittān mārvidandān nīṟṟān niḻalmaṇi vaṇṇattān kūṟṟorupāl mangaiyān pūmagaḷān vārsadaiyān nīṇmudiyān gangaiyān nīḷkaḻalān kāppu*

**Пословный перевод:**

ēṟṟān – having (the not so famous) bull as vehicle; eyil erittān – burnt the three towns (of his followers); nīṟṟān – smearing ashes (to get rid of a curse); kūṟṟorupāl mangaiyān – sustaining his wife pārvati on one part (of his body); vār sadaiyān – one who has matted hair, signifying that he is carrying out penance; gangaiyān – Sivan who is having gangā on his head (to rid of sins); puḷ ūrndān – having garuda (who has vēdas as his body) as his vehicle; mārvu idandān – tore the chest of iraṇyan (hiraṇyakaśyap) (for the sake of his devotee prahlāda); niḻal maṇivaṇṇattān – having a cool, comfortable form like a blue gemstone; pū magaḷān – having śrī mahālakśmi on his chest; nīḷ mudiyān – having the long crown (indicative of his greatness); nīḷ kaḻalān – one having long divine feet (the origin of gangā); kāppu – is the protector

**Перевод:**

**ēṟṟān – Sivan is famous for having bull as his vehicle.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*āḻvār was asked as to why he was saying that there is no one to be attained other than emperumān. īsn’t rudra (Siva) appropriate to be attained? āḻvār says that since rudra is also the body for emperumān, he is also protected by emperumān only, like all the others. rudra has nothing common with emperumān, other than being body (rudra) and soul (emperumān). All the entities are sustained by emperumān only. If one looks at the qualities of rudra, it would be clear that he is being protected and emperumān is the protector. Let us enjoy the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**puḷḷūrndān** – emperumān has garuda, who is the embodiment of vēdas, as his vehicle

**eyil erittān** – Siva had burnt down the towns of his devotees, in order to get the title of having burnt three towns.

**mārvidandān** – emperumān transformed himself as narasimha (lion head with human body) in order to kill the enemy, iraṇiyan (hiraṇya kaśyap), of his devotee prahlāda.

**nīṟṟān** – Sivan would smear ashes all over his body, like a person carrying out atonement for sins committed

**niḻal maṇi vaṇṇattān** – emperumān has a cool, comfortable form which will eliminate the anguish of his followers

**kūṟṟorupāl mangaiyān** – Sivan has his consort pārvati on one half of his body and feels vain because of that.

**pū magaḷān** – as said in yajur vēdam 4-4-12 about śrī mahālakśmi “*asyēśāna jagatō viṣṇupatnī*” (she is the leader of the world and consort of viṣṇu), emperumān has pirātti (śrī mahālakśmi) as his consort

**vār sadaiyān** – appropriate to the penance that he is carrying out, Sivan has hanging, matted hair.

**nīḷ mudiyān** – emperumān has long crown, which signifies that he is the lord of all the worlds.

**gangaiyān** –  Sivan bears gangā on his head, in order to purify himself.

**nīḷ kaḻalān** – emperumān has divine feet, which are the source for that gangā.

The meaning conveyed here is that emperumān, with all the characteristics mentioned above, is the protector for Sivan, with his own set of characteristics as mentioned above.

We shall move on to the 75<sup>th</sup> pāsuram next.

## 75. Kappu unnai

*kāppunnai unnak kaḻiyum aruvinaigaḷ āppunnai unna aviḻndu oḻiyum mūppu unnaic cindippārkkillai tirumālē nin adiyai vandippār kāṇbar vaḻi*

**Пословный перевод:**

unnai unna – when you are thought of; kāppu – the deities who serve as guards; kaḻiyum – will get disengaged; aru vinaigaḷ – terrible deeds; unnai unna – once you are thought of; āppu avḻindu oḻiyum – will get annihilated, after getting liberated; unnaic cindippārkku – for those who meditate on you; mūppu illai – the six changes, including aging, are not present; tirumālē – Oh, my benefactor and the consort of śrī mahālakśmi; nin adiyai – your divine feet; vandippār – those who worship; vaḻi kāṇbar – will see the archirādi mārga (the path leading to śrīvaikuṇṭam)

**Перевод:**

**kāppu unnai unnak kaḻiyum – there are 14 dēvatās (deities) who have been appointed by emperumān to keep a watch over the activities of jīvātmās (sentient entities) “āditya candrāvanilōnala ca dyour bhūmir āpō hrudayam yama ca rātri ubhē ca sandyē dharma ca jānāti narasya vruttam” (1. sun; 2. moon; 3. wind; 4. fire; 5. sky (ether); 6. earth; 7. water; 8. mind; 9. yama (deity for righteousness); 10. day; 11. night; 12. prātas sandyā (dawn); 13. sāyam sandyā (dusk); 14. righteousness – the 14 deities for these entities always know the activities of jīvātmās). When these deities hear those who recite the names of emperumān, they will move away from such persons saying that emperumān is duty bound to protect his followers and not themselves (the 14 deities). In śrīviṣṇu purāṇam 3-7-4 hasn’t yama told his messenger “parihara madusūdana prapannān prabhuraham anya nruṇam na vaiṣṇavānām” (do not approach those who have surrendered to madusūdana (emperumān); I am the lord of all others and not of śrīvaiṣṇavas)! In śrī bhāgavatam 11-5-4 has it not been said “dēvarśibhūtāpta nruṇām pitrūṇām na kinkarō nāyam ruṇī ca rājan! sarvātmā ya: śaraṇam śaraṇyam nārāyaṇam lōka gurum prapanna:” (Oh King! one who has surrendered to śaraṇyan (nārāyaṇa) who is apt to be surrendered and is the lōkaguru (teacher for the world) is not a servitor or indebted to dēvas (celestial entities), riśis (sages), bhūtams (creatures), relatives and forefathers)! Other deities have to protect only those who are under the control of their karmas (past deeds, both good and bad). If those karmas are negated \[as done by emperumān for those who surrender to him\], these other deities cannot go anywhere near such persons. āḻvār explains now how the karmas get negated.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*āḻvār says in this pāsuram that those who attain emperumān, who protects Sivan also, will have their enemies removed and proceed in the correct path. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**aruvinaigaḷ āppu unnai unna aviḻndu oḻiyum** – It is said that “*avidyā karma samjñā*” (ignorance is referred to by the word karma). All the bondage which are the result due to ignorance will disappear for those who think of you.

**mūppu unnaic cindippārkku illai** – *mūppu* here refers to kaivalyam (ātmā enjoying itself) for those who are outside the protection of other deities and who do not have sins.  Even such kaivalyam will disappear if they think of you. Alternative explanation – the six dimensions of changes in a person’s life, viz.,1. *asti* (existing), 2. *jāyatē* (is born), 3. *pariṇamatē* (transforms), 4. *vivartatē* (grows), 5. *apakśīyatē* (shrinks due to old age) and 6. *vinaśyati*  (dies), will not affect those who think of you.

**tirumālē** – it is because emperumān is with *tiru* (śrī mahālakśmi) that these do not come near his followers. Even if emperumān wants to look into the sinful deeds due to his undeterred independence, *pirātti*  (śrī mahālakśmi) would convince him to change his divine mind by saying “vouldn’t sins, which cannot be rid of due to atonement, get removed with our forbearance!” Hadn’t pirātti shown mercy on the rākśasis (demonic women employed by rāvaṇa) who troubled her, by saying as in śrī rāmāyaṇam yudda kāṇḍam 113-41 “*dāsīnām rāvaṇasyāham marśayāmīha durbala*” (I do not have the wherewithal to see the sufferings of rāvāṇa’s servant-maids) and in śrī rāmāyaṇam yudda kāṇḍam 113-39 “*rājasamśraya vaśyānām kurvantīnām prājñā*” (aren’t they (demonic women) under the control of the king and appointed by him?)! Thus, if one thinks of emperumān who is with pirātti, the sins and *mūppu* will disappear.

**nin adiyai vandippār kāṇbar vaḻi** – those who worship your divine feet will find the correct path. The word *vaḻi* can be construed as *arcirādi mārgam* which is the path leading to śrīvaikuṇṭam. The path to śrīvaikuṇṭam will be as sweet as śrīvaikuṇṭam itself. In the same way, the path to naragam (hell) will be as horrible as naragam itself. For those who do not think of emperumān, the path will be full of thorns and narrow lanes while for those who think of emperumān, the path will be sweet.

**mūppu unnaic cindippārkku illai tirumālē** – mūppu could also be construed as old age. As said in rig vēdam, viṣṇu sūktam “*yuvākumāra yuvati: ca kumāriṇī*” (emperumān is youthful  to a large extent and boyish to a smaller extent while pirātti is girlish to a large extent and youthful to a smaller extent) if one attains emperumān and pirātti, he will be like them in his youthful and boyish states and not attain old age.

We shall take up the 76<sup>th</sup> pāsuram next.

## 76. Vali ninru

*vaḻi ninṟu ninnait toḻuvār vaḻuvā moḻi ninṟa mūrttiyē āvar paḻudonṟum vārāda vaṇṇamē viṇ kodukkum maṇṇaḷanda sīrān tiruvēngadam*

**Пословный перевод:**

vaḻi ninṟu – being steadfast in the path of bhakti (devotion); ninnai – you; toḻuvār – those who attain you; vaḻuvā moḻi ninṟa – as mentioned in vēdas, which speak only the truth; mūrttiyarē āvar – will surely have as their basic nature; maṇ aḷanda sīrān – (without considering his greatness) the one who has the simplicity of measuring all the worlds; tiruvēngadam – the hills of tirumalai; paḻudu onṟum – any shortcoming; viṇ kodukkum – will it not grant paramapadam (śrīvaikuṇṭam)!

**Перевод:**

**vaḻi ninṟu ninnait tozuvār – those who worship you  in the way it is mentioned in vēdas, dharma śāstras (texts based on righteousness), itihāsas (epics such as śrī rāmāyaṇa and mahābhārata), purāṇas (ancient texts written by sages) etc. bruhadāraṇyaka upaniśat 6-5-6 says “ātmā vā arē draśtavya: śrōtravya: mantavyō nididyāsitavya:” (the ātmā which is appropriate to be heard and thought of, is apt to be meditated upon and seen). bruhadāraṇyaka upaniśat 6-4-2 says “tamēva dīrō vijñāya prajñām kurvīta brāhmaṇai:” (let that brāhmaṇa, who is the best among the jñānis (those with knowledge) know about paramātmā and meditate on him). “upāsanam druvāsmruti: darśanasamānākaratā pratyākśapatti”(upāsanam, steady mode of thinking, having the ability to look at all with equanimity and conspicuousness  – all these terms have the same meaning). śrī bhagavat gītā 18-55 says “tatō mām tatvatō jñātvā viśatē tadanantaram” (he knows about me as I am and then attains me). Thus as mentioned in all the sacred texts, being in the path of upāsanam (devotion) up to the stage of darśan (audience)of emperumān.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*āḻvār says in this pāsuram “When it is said that tirumalai (tiruppati hills) itself, which is closely connected to you, gives benefits to those who attain it, is it necessary to say that those who attain you would get salvation?“. embār (bhagavad śrī rāmānujar’s successor  in the line of ācāryas in our sampradāyam) would always meditate on this pāsuram since this pāsuram says that tirumalai serves as a means for attaining emperumān. Let us enjoy the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**vaḻuvā moḻi ninṟa mūrttiyarē āvar** – those who are having their nature as mentioned by vēdas which are without any falsehood and have full integrity. cāndōjña upaniśat 8-12-2 says “*ēśa samprasādōsmāccarīrāt samuttāya param jyōtirupasampatya svēnarūpēṇa abhiniśpadyatē*” (this jīvan (soul) exits from this (physical) body, attains supreme being nārāyaṇa and realises  his basic nature) – as mentioned in such sacred texts, they manifest their natural behaviour.

**paḻudu onṟum vārāda vaṇṇamē viṇ kodummum** – it will give paramapadam (śrīvaikuṇṭam) without any shortcoming either in the result that is obtained \[by the [ātmā](https://granthams.koyil.org/2013/03/06/thathva-thrayam-chith-who-am-i/)\] or in the path taken \[to reach [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/)\]. Since the result includes being servitor to emperumān’s followers also, there will be no shortcoming in the result. Since emperumān is the means for attaining him, there will be no shortcoming in the means too.

**maṇ aḷanda sīrān tirvēngadam** – the tiruvēngadam hill has this quality \[of granting śrīvaikuṇṭam\]. Actually it is calling out “*maṇṇaḷanda sīrānē*” (the 8<sup>th</sup> case, also called as vocative case or *viḷiccol*) (this could be construed as telling emperumān “those who worship you in the ordained way would realise their basic nature; your tirumalai hill itself will grant śrivaikuṇṭam ”). Alternative explanation: the tirumalai hill owned by emperumān who measured the world, will grant mōkśam or śrīvaikuṇṭam. Hasn’t [nammāḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/nammazhwar/) in his [tiruvāimoḻi 3-3-7](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2016/08/thiruvaimozhi-3-3-7-sumandhu-mamalar/) said “*samankoḷ vīdu tarum tadam kunṟamē*” (the tirumalai hill will grant śrīvaikuṇṭam)! People will grant only what they have! \[what applies to people would apply to [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/)’s possessions too\].

We shall consider the 77<sup>th</sup> pāsuram next.

## 77. Vengadamum vinnagarum

*vēngadamum viṇṇagarum vehkāvum ahkāda pūngidangin nīḷ kōval ponnagarum nāngidattum ninṟan irundān kidandān nadandānē enṟāl kedumām idar*

**Пословный перевод:**

vēngadamum – tirumalai; viṇ nagarum – śrīvaikuṇṭam; vehkāvum – tiruvehkā divyadēsam; ahkāda pūm kidangin – having moats with unchanging flowers \[always fresh\]; nīḷ kōval ponnagarum – sweet and beautiful tirukkōvalūr; nāngu idattum – in these four divyadēsams; ninṟān irundān kidandān nadandānē enṟāl – if we say that (emperumān) stands, stays, reclines and walks; idar – the results of our deeds that we carryout standing, sitting, lying and walking; kedumām – will be destroyed

**Перевод:**

**vēngadam ninṟān enṟāl kedumām idar – our sins will disappear if we say that emperumān stood at tiruvēngadam which removes sins**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*āḻvār says in this pāsuram that not only tiruvēngadam, but all the divyadēsams (divine abodes) where emperumān is dwelling have this special quality \[of granting śrīvaikuṇṭam\]. If we meditate on the deeds that emperumān has carried out for our sake, the sins due to our deeds will disappear. āḻvār says that all the sins that we had accrued due to our deeds carried out in our standing, sitting, lying and moving postures will disappear if we think of the benefits that he granted to us in his standing, sitting, lying and moving postures. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**viṇṇagar irundān …** – if we meditate on the fact that he is sitting in paramapadam, our sins will be destroyed

**vehkā kidandān …** – if we meditate that he is lying down in tiruvehkā divyadēsam, our sins will be destroyed

**nīḷ kōval ponnagar nadandān…** – if we meditate that he is walking in the divyadēsam tiruukōvalūr which is famous and delightful, our sins will disappear.

**ahkāda pūngidangin nīḷ kōval ponnagarum** – since this was the divyadēsam where emperumān jostled with [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/poigai-azhwar/)(s), he is thinking back sweetly, of this divyadēśam.

**ahkāda kidangu** – the moats are very close to each other in a protective way in this divyadēśam where emperumān and his followers \[the first three āḻvārs\] jostled for space.

**pūm kidangu** –  beautiful moat. If one were engaged with the moats, the mind will not go towards even emperumān.

**ahkāda pūm kidangu** – moats wherein the flowers are in full bloom, in clusters.

**pūm kidangin nīḷ kōval ponnagar** – if one were to shift the vision beyond the moats, the sweetness and beauty of this place will attract one’s eyes and heart.

**ponnagar** – [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/poigai-azhwar/) terms this divyadēśam as ponnagar (golden place) since emperumān came on his own, jostling with the [āḻvārs](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/azhwars/) for space and graced them.

**vēngadamum viṇṇagarum vehkāvum … kōval ponnagarum** – even though these divyadēśams are far apart from one another, [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/poigai-azhwar/) combines them since they are related to emperumān and are enjoyable for āḻvār.

**nāngidattum ninṟān irundān kidandān nadandānē enṟāl** – if one were to think that emperumān toiled hard in these four places, standing,  sitting, reclining and walking, in order to attain his followers, his sins will disappear.

**enrāl kedum** – one need not reside in these divyadēśams. Even if one were to meditate on emperumān’s dwelling in these places, that person’s sins will get destroyed.

**enṟāl kedum** – the meditation need not be carried out with the knowledge that it would be a path to attain emperumān. Even if it is said just for the sake of saying, the sins will disappear. periyavāccān piḷḷai, one of the commentators for this prabandam, will say “just as one’s distress disappears on calling out to mother”.

**nāngidattum ninṟan irundān kidandān nadandānē enṟāl kedum** – if one were to meditate on emperumān’s deeds while he was standing, sitting, reclining and walking, all the results of sins carried out by the person in these four postures will disappear.

**idar kedumām** – the sorrows accumulated by the [jīvātmā](https://granthams.koyil.org/2013/03/06/thathva-thrayam-chith-who-am-i/) in the samsāram on his own, will disappear on their own without a trace as said by [periyāḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/periyazhwar/) in periyāḻvār tiumoḻi 5-4-3 “*summenādē kai vittu ōdit tūṟugaḷ pāyndanavē*” (without being able to even breathe, they hid themselves in bushes) and as said by [nammāḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/nammazhwar/) in periya tiruvandādi 54 “*vāno maṟikadalō*” ((we don’t know where the sins disappeared – did they disappear in the skies or in the oceans?)

**idar kedumām** – mentioning about *aniśta nivrutti* (removal of undesirables) implies *iśta prāpti* (attaining the desirables) too.

We shall consider the 78<sup>th</sup> pāsuram next.

## 78. Idar ar paduvar

*idar ār paduvār eḻu nenjē vēḻam todarvān kodu mudalai sūḻnda padamudaiya painnāgap paḷḷiyān pādamē kai toḻudum koinnāgap pūm pōdu koṇḍu*

**Пословный перевод:**

vēḻam – elephant \[by name gajēndra āḻvān\]; todar – following (to swallow it); vān – having strength; kodu mudalai – cruel crocodile; sūḻnda – one who destroyed; padam udaiya – having spread hood; pai nāgam paḷḷiyān – emperumān who has as his mattress, the expansive tiruvanantāḻvān (ādiśēśan); pādamē – divine feet; nāgam koy pūm pōdu koṇḍu – with beautiful flowers plucked from punnai tree \[a kind of flower bearing tree\]; kai toḻudum – we shall worship; nenjē – Oh heart!; eḻu – arise (to worship like this); Ar idar paduvār – who will experience sorrows?

**Перевод:**

**idar Ar paduvār – who is capable of bearing the sorrow of separation from emperumān? Just as looking for people who are hungry when food is available, āḻvār is asking who will suffer in separation from emperumān when he is available.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*āḻvār says in this pāsuram that we cannot but carry out all kainkaryams (services) to emperumān as said by lakśmaṇa in śrī rāmāyaṇam yudda kāṇḍam 31-25 “aham sarvam kariśyāmi” (I will carry out all kainkaryams), if we think about all the deeds carried out by emperumān for our sake. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**eḻu nenjē** – Oh my intelligent heart! Rise immediately and set forth. If sorrows come our way, they \[the sorrows\] will suffer the same consequence that the crocodile suffered.

**vēḻam todar vān kodu mudalai sūḻnda** – the crocodile followed the elephant; it thought that it was very powerful; emperumān killed that crocodile which did  not have any mercy in its heart and hence was cruel.

**padam udaiya pai nāgap paḷḷiyān pādamē kai toḻudum** – let us worship the divine feet of emperumān who has the characteristic of having given his divine form to tiruvanantāḻvān (ādiśēśan), who in turn is extremely happy since he is with emperumān and due to which he has spread his hoods fully. Since both killing of crocodile and offering himself to tiruvantāḻvān have been mentioned, this verse clarifies the way emperumān conducts himself in matters relating to his enemies and his followers. It says that emperumān will remove our enemies and make us servitors similar to tiruvantāḻvān.

With what will the service be carried out?

**koynnāgap pūm pōdu koṇḍu** – worship with the sweet flowers plucked from (pun)nāga tree. One need not look for an exalted flower like nāgappū. āḻvār says that any flower from the forest is sufficient to worship him. nanjīyar (disciple of parāsara bhattar and ācārya of nampiḷḷai (one of the commentators for this prabandam)) would say mercifully “since this āḻvār was spending lot of time in the forest, he would have known about the flowers in the forest too”. In an earlier time, he showered mercy on gajēndra āḻvān (the elephant gajēndra) who wanted to offer him flowers, by removing his hurdle and making him his servitor. He will remove our hurdles too if we offer him flowers and worship him.

We shall move on to the 79<sup>th</sup> pāsuram next.

## 79. Kondarai allal

*koṇḍārai allāl koduttārai yār paḻippār maṇ tā ena irandu māvaliyai oṇ tārai nīr am kai tōya nimirndilaiyē nīḷ visumbil ār am kai tōya aduttu*

**Пословный перевод:**

“maṇ tā” ena – give (three footsteps of) earth; māvaliyai irandu – begging mahābali; oṇ tārai nīr – radiant stream of water (poured by him); am kai tōya – once it fell on (your) beautiful hands; nīḷ visumbilār – the dēvas (celestial entities) in the expansive sky; am kai – beautiful hands; tōya – to wash (your) divine feet; aduttu nimirndilaiyē – did you not grow immediately?; koṇḍānai – one who made (his property) as his own; (paḻippār) allāl – blaming only him; koduttārai – one who gave (what was not his, but presumed to be his); yār paḻippār – there is no one to blame!

**Перевод:**

**āḻvār is asking emperumān whether he carried out an activity which the people of this world praised (the people are blaming emperumān). This world is full of people who consider virtue as vice and vice as virtue! While this world already belonged to emperumān, mahābali thought that it belonged to him. While he thought that he was giving it to emperumān as a gift, emperumān also agreed with his perception and accepted it as a gift. But, the people are blaming emperumān for pushing mahābali into pātāḷam (nether world).**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*It has been mentioned in the previous pāsurams that emperumān transforms himself and is capable of carrying out the tasks for the sake of his followers. To the query where he did like this, āḻvār says in this pāsuram that he made himself as one asking for alms for the sake of indra (head of celestials). Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**koṇḍānai allāl koduttārai yār paḻippār** – the demon mahābali seized the wealth of emperumān as if it were his. He had the quality of being a magnanimous person, as a result of which he could not be killed. If the world became his, [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/)‘s followers will suffer. If he became happy because he was gifting it, emperumān did not mind taking it from him by begging for it and hence begged mahābali. Instead of thinking on these lines, the people of the world would blame emperumān and say that he cheated mahābali and got the three worlds from him. In reality, mahābali should have been punished (like a weed is plucked along with paddy grain)! Just as rāvaṇa, who abducted sītāppirātti, was killed, mahābali, who seized the three worlds should have been killed. Could a person who does not own a property, gift that property to another! Did not [āḷavandār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/alavandhar/) say in [stōtra ratnam 53](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2017/02/sthothra-rathnam-53/) “*atavā kinnu samarppayāmi tē*” (what could I offer to you!) upon submitting his ātmā to emperumān and then realising that the [ātmā](https://granthams.koyil.org/2013/03/06/thathva-thrayam-chith-who-am-i/) is emperumān’s property only and felt remorseful! But mahābali gifted the worlds belonging to emperumān to emperumān himself and even after that thought that he had gifted his own properties to emperumān. There is a variation in this verse, with the word *koṇḍārai* used instead of *koṇḍānai*. In this case, the meaning could be construed as referring to the benefit.

**maṇ tā ena irandu māvaliyai** – did emperumān not go to mahābali and begged him for gifting the world, which is a part of his properties!

**oṇṭhārai nīr angai tōya nimirndilaiyē** – emperumān rose rapidly into the skies the moment that stream of water poured by mahābali touched his hand, wondering what if mahābali changes his mind. It appeared as if emperumān got something which he had never before obtained. In śrī rāmāyaṇam sundara kāṇḍam 29-6, it is said “*varśēṇa bījam pratisanjaharśa*” (the seed feels happy due to rains). In the same way, emperumān grew as soon as the stream of water touched  his hands.

**nīḷ visumbil ārangai tōya aduttu** – this could be construed as either (1) reaching up to the sky so that his \[[emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/)‘s\] necklace and beautiful hands touched each other or (2) reaching so that dēvas (celestial entities) could touch him with their hands. Did not [bhūdattāḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/bhuthaththazhwar/) mention in his iraṇḍām tiruvandādi 78 “*daraṇi nivandaḷappa nīttiya poṟpādam sivanda than kai anaittum ārak kaḻuvinān*” (when emperumān stretched his divine feet at the time of measuring the worlds, brahmā washed his divine feet with his hands). Another explanation – did you not rise up so that the sky could touch  your divine hands!

**nīr angai tōya nimirndilaiyē** – the water which mahābali poured on emperumān’s hands and the water that brahmā poured to wash emperumān’s divine feet when he stretched them in the sky, fell onto the earth together (he grew up that fast). We may not have our svarūpa jñānam (the knowledge of our true selves \[which is that we are servitors to emperumān\]) but we would benefit from our sensory perceptions if we could see or hear about such divine activities of [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/)‘s.

We shall next consider the 80<sup>th</sup> pāsuram.

## 80. Adutta kadum

*adutta kadum pagaijñaṟku āṟṟēn enṟōdi padutta perum pāḻi sūḻnda vidattaravai vallāḷan kai kodutta māmēni māyavanukku allādum āvārō āḷ*

**Пословный перевод:**

adutta kadum pagaijñaṟku – against garuda, who is a natural enemy; āṟṟēn enṟu – I cannot survive (in a direct contest); ōdi – moving rapidly; padutta perum pāḻi – the large cot on which (emperumān) reclines; sūḻnda – coiling (around its leg); vidattu aravai – a poisonous snake (by name sumukan); vallāḷan – garuda who is powerful; kai kodutta – mercifully handing over to him for protection; mā mēni māyavanukku allādum – other than emperumān who has great divine form and is a wondrous entity; āvārō āḷ – will they become servitors?

**Перевод:**

**adutta kadum pagai – enmity which is very closely related. Starting from their birth, there has been enmity between garuda and snakes. The fight started between garuda’s mother vinatai and snakes’ mother katru.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*āḻvār says that it is not that emperumān redeemed the worlds from mahābali. If a person surrenders to him and if there is enmity between this person and his follower, not withstanding his own words “na kśamāmi” (I will not forgive the enemies of my followers) emperumān will unite the person who has surrendered with his followers. There was once a snake by name sumukan. While garuda, a nityasūri (permanent dweller of śrīvaikuṇṭam) and emperumān’s vehicle, wanted sumukan as his food. sumukan, upon hearing this, got frightened. He went to indra lōkam (the world of indra, who is the head of celestial entities) and surrendered to emperumān who was sitting on a throne there at that time, by coiling himself on the leg of the throne. This is all that is required for one who wants to surrender. Just as the riśis told śrī rāmā in śrī rāmāyaṇam āraṇya kāṇḍam 1-20 “tē vayam bhavatā rakśyā: bhavadviśayavāsina:” (we, who are living in your country, are to be protected by you), all that is required is to be within the boundary of the place where emperumān blesses his followers. garuda, who went in search of sumukan, saw him near the throne of emperumān and asked him to give sumukan as his food. emperumān responded saying that he cannot give up one who has surrendered to him. garuda, becoming angry, let out a few harsh words typical of confidential servants (such as “is this the gift you are offering to someone who has been bearing you all along?”). emperumān calmed him down and gave garuda the responsibility of protecting sumukan. garuda wore sumukan around his neck like an ornament and protected him. Let us enjoy the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**kadum pagai** – the enmity is such severe that if the name of one person is mentioned to a person on the other side, the other person will end his life.

**āṟṟēn enṟu ōdi** – sumukan, fearful of such garuda, ran away saying that he cannot stand against garuda in a fight.

**padutta perum pāḻi sūḻnda** – sumukan coiled around the leg of emperumān’s cot which was appropriate for emperumān. Words such as *pāyal*, *pāḻi, pāsi* refer to cot.

**vidattu aravai** – emperumān saved that sumukan who is poisonous. Did sumukan have any qualities such as *samam* (controlling mind) or *damam* (controlling the external senses)? Even among enemies one may across one with good qualities (such as vibhīśaṇa). But sumukan is not like that. śrīviṣṇu purāṇam 5-7-72 says “*sarpajātiriyam krūrā:*” (the species of snakes are cruel). This implies that sumukan is not one on whom emperumān would shower his grace.

**vallāḷan kai kodutta** – there is none as skillful as emperumān when it comes to protecting those who have surrendered. emperumān told the person who sumukan was scared of and came running to him, to protect him after giving sumukan to him. Just as people remove the poison through the snake which injected the poison in the first place, emperumān made the enemy of sumukan (garuda) to protect him. Did  not emperumān \[as śrī rāma\] make sugrīva mahārāja who said in śrī rāmāyaṇam yudda kāṇḍam 17-29 “*vadyatām*” (let vibhīśaṇan be killed) to say “*asmābhi: tulyō bhavatu*” (let vibhīśaṇa be equal to us)! By handing over sumukan to garuda and protecting him through garuda, emperumān ensured that his words in varāha purāṇam “*na kśamāmi*” (I will not tolerate the enemies of followers) and in śrī rāmāyaṇam yudda kāṇḍam 18-3 “*na tyajēyam*” (I will not give up on those who surrendered) rang true.

**mā mēni** – this refers to the greatness in the divine physical form of emperumān after protecting sumukan. In śrī rāmāyaṇam yudda kāṇḍam 1-83 it says “*krutakrutya: tadā rāma:*” (only after coronating vibhīśaṇa  śrī rāma felt that he had carried what he had intended to)!

**māyavanukku** – emperumān is most wondrous.  he cannot detect himself, the boundary for the bias that he has towards his followers.

**allādum āvārō āḷ** – can one become a servitor to anyone other than emperumān? Those who are servitors to other deities have to look after their protection themselves. īsn’t it only emperumān, who takes it upon himself to take charge of the protection of a person who has surrendered to him, who should be attained! Hasn’t [nammāḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/nammazhwar/) mercifully said in tiruviruttam 27 “*sēmam sengōn aruḷē*” (our protection is only through the mercy of emperumān)!

We shall take up the 81<sup>st</sup> pāsuram next.

## 81. Alamar venri

*āḷamar venṟi adu kaḷattuḷ ajñānṟu vāḷamar vēṇḍi varai nattu nīḷaravaic cuṟṟik kadaindān peyar enṟē tol nagaraip paṟṟīk kadattum padai*

**Пословный перевод:**

āḷ amar venṟi adu kaḷattuḷ – in the battlefield which contains warriors and victory; ajñānṟu – during that time (when the demons troubled dēvas, the celestial entities); vāḷ amar vēṇḍi – desiring the war which carries respect; varai nattu – keeping the mantaragiri (a divine mountain) as (as agitator, to churn the ocean); nīḷ aravaic cuṟṟi – coiling the long snake (vāsuki) as rope (for churning); kadaindān – one who churned (the milky ocean); peyar – divine names; paṟṟi – scooping (the cētanas, the sentient entities); tol naragai – the ancient hellish regions; kadattum – will enable to cross; padai anṟē – is it not an upāyam (means)!

**Перевод:**

**āḷ mar venṟi adu kaḷattuḷ – being full of warriors, close to each other, having victory**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*āḻvār asks whether samsāris (those living in materialistic realm) can get rid of their samsāram by doing beneficial activities themselves. He is asking whether there is any way other than reciting emperumān‘s divine names to survive. He says that there is no way to protect ourselves other than reciting the divine names of emperumān, who churned the oceans, unmindful of his strain, while giving the elixir to those who were after other benefits \[dēvas, the celestial entities\]. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**adu kaḷattu** – battlefield in which lives are lost

**ajñānṟu** – that day when the demons troubled dēvas and ate them up

**vāḷamar vēṇḍi** – *vāḷ* – radiance. Desiring a war which will have radiance. When demons and celestial persons were engaged in the war, when the demons became very powerful and celestial persons started losing their strength, in order to create a war which will bring esteem for celestials who are friendly towards him and who are his followers, [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) waited.

**varai nattu nīḷ aravaic cuṟṟik kadaindān peyar anrē** – but that needs a means. In deep waters \[of the ocean\] keeping a mountain which can sink, coiling the soft snake vāsuki, emperumān churned the huge ocean.

**tol naragaip paṟṟik kadattum padai** –  is it not the divine name of [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) who is capable of measuring the ocean and churning it, which is capable of making us to cross the ancient hell of samsāram (materialistic realm) and to keep us in mōkśam (śrīvaikuṇṭam)!

**paṟṟik kadattum padai** – is it not the divine name of emperumān which will sweep us from samsāram and keep us in paramapadam (śrīvaikuṇṭam)! Just as emperumān gave elixir to those who desired other benefits \[dēvas\], just as svayamprabhai (a lady character in śrī rāmāyaṇam) through the power of her penance, placed the army of monkeys who went in search of sītāppirātti on the shores of the ocean, the divine name of [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) will, by itself, take us to paramapadam and uplift us by giving us *dāsyāmrutam* (the nectar of servitorśip). jitantē ślōkam 4 says “*tvāmēva śaraṇam prāpya nistaranti manīśiṇa:*” (those people who surrender to you cross samsāram).

We shall take up the 82<sup>nd</sup> pāsuram next.

## 82. Padaiyarum val kannar

*padaiyārum vāḷ kaṇṇār pārasināḷ paimpūm todaiyalōdu ēndiya dūpam idaiyidaiyil mīn māya māsūṇum vēngadamē mēl orunāḷ mān māya eydān varai*

**Пословный перевод:**

padai ārum – like a sword; vāḷ kaṇṇar – women with radiant eyes; pārasi nāḷ – on the day of dvādaśi (12th day of new moon or full moon); pai – beautiful; pūm todaiyalōdu – with garlands made of flowers; ēndiya – carrying; dūpam – smoke; idaiyidaiyil mīn – stars which appear in between; māya – to be hidden; māsūṇum – to become stained; vēngadamē – Oh tirumalai!; mēl oru nāḷ – once upon a time; mān – mārīca (a demon related to rāvaṇa) who came in the form of deer; māya – to die; eydān – one who shot the arrow; varai – (his) hill

**Перевод:**

**padaiyārum vāḷ kaṇṇār – women with eyes like a \[sharp\] spear and having a bright radiance. The word padai refers to sword; hence it can also be construed as women with eyes sharp like a sword. The opinion here is that these women, whose beauty is such that others would desirously come and attain them, are attaining tirumalai (the divine hills) themselves.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*When we have to recite the name of the one who churned the ocean, should we not see him in person and then recite? For those who say that only dēvas (celestial people) can see and attain him, āḻvār says that in order to remove their grief and to enable them to attain him emperumān  has taken residence in tirumalai. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**pārasi nāḷ** – on the 12<sup>th</sup> day of moon (either new or full)

**paim pūm todaiyalōdu ēndiya dūpam** – since these are young girls, their focus could shift to worldly pursuits. tiruvēngadam (tirumalai) has such a quality that instead of their straying into worldly pursuits, it is making them come to tirumalai and worship [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/). The beautiful garlands that these girls have strung are to be offered to emperumān. They are also carrying fragrant smoke with them for vorśipping emperumān.

**idai idaiyil māsūṇum vēngadamē** – The smoke carried by these girls causes stain on the sky \[over the tirumalai\]. The garlands that they are carrying hide the starts that keep coming now and then in the sky.

**mēl oru nāḷ mān māya eydān varai** – in an earlier time, [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) engaged in an activity for sītāppirātti  in śrī rāmāyaṇam. She desired to have a deer that came near their hermitage. śrī rāma, to fulfil her desire, went after the deer, and had to kill it. It is said that emperumān will help these girls who have come to tirumalai to worship him. tirumalai is the residence of emperumān who engages in activities for the sake of his followers, being at their disposal \[as evident from the above mentioned incident\]. It is mentioned in this pāsuram that this hill is the residence of emperumān, who has no shortcoming in the qualities of being simple, being at the disposal of his followers, being sweet and capable of disposing of the enemies of his followers.

We shall move on to the 83<sup>rd</sup> pāsuram next.

## 83. Varai kudai tol

*varai kudai tōḷ kāmbāga ānirai kāttu āyar nirai viadai ēḻ seṟṟavāru ennē uravudaiya nīrāḻi uḷ kidandu nērā nisāsarar mēl pērāḻi koṇḍa pirān*

**Пословный перевод:**

uravu udaiya – having strength; nīr āḻiyuḷ kidandu – in the tiruppāṟkadal (milky ocean) containing water, where you were reclining; nēr Am – those who came as enemies; nisāsarar mēl – on the demons (madu and kaitabha); pēr āḻi koṇḍa – striking the huge cakrāyudam (divine disc); pirān – Oh benefactor!; varai kudai (āga) – holding the mountain (gōvardana) as umbrella; tōḷ kāmbu āga – making shoulders as the handle of umbrella; ānirai kāttu – protecting the herds of cows; āyar – belonging to the covherds; nirai vidai ēḻ – the seven fat bulls; seṟṟa āṟu – the way you killed them; ennē – how was it?

**Перевод:**

**varai kudai tōḷ kāmbāga ānirai kāttu – you  held the mountain (gōvardana) which could not be shaken by anything, as an umbrella, keeping your shoulder as the handle for that mountain, in order to protect cows.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*emperumān asks āḻvār “how do you know that I am at the disposal of my followers and would engage in activities desired by them?”. āḻvār responds, saying “you are holding the disc that killed the demons madu and kaitabha. You also held the mountain gōvardana as an umbrella and you also controlled the seven bulls. All these activities were  carried out for the sake of your followers and not without any reason ” Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**āyar nirai vidai ēḻ seṟṟavāṟu ennē** – why did you kill the seven huge bulls which came as a hurdle to your marrying nappinnai pirātti \[incarnation of nīḷā dēvi during kriṣṇāvatāram\]? Was it to demonstrate your omnipotent nature? It was to offer yourself to your followers, who have no other refuge,  by removing their enemies. Instead of hurling your divine disc at indra \[who ordained hailstorm to trouble the covherds\] you protected the cows and covherds by lifting the gōvardana hill, hurting your shoulders!

**uravudaiya nīr āḻiyuḷ kidandu** – *uragam* means snake and *uravudaiya nīr* would refer to ocean having snake. Here reference to snake implies all the wild species in the ocean. Alternatively *uram* also means *midukku* which is strength. Here it would refer to ocean which is deep. You were reclining on such tiruppāṟkadal (milky ocean).

**nērā nisāsarar mēl** – even during the time that you were resting, reclining on tiruppāṟkadal, you powdered to pieces the demons madu and kataibha with your divine disc, when they came as enemies. What sort of activities have you indulged in – killing demons even when you were resting, holding the mountain which would have pained your shoulders, killing the well grown bulls risking your divine form! Thus, when his followers face trouble from their enemies, instead of killing them through his *sankalp* (will), [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) comes in person and removes their hurdles, suffering pain to his divine form.

We shall move on to the 84<sup>th</sup> pāsuram next.

## 84. Piran un perumai

*pirān un perumai piṟar ār aṟivār urāy ulagu aḷanda  nānṟu varāgattu eyiṟṟaḷavu pōdāvāṟu enkolō endai adikkaḷavu pōnda padi*

**Пословный перевод:**

pirān – Oh emperumān!; urāy – without any tiredness; ulagu aḷanda nānṟu – when (you) measured the worlds; endai – you, my svāmy; adikku – for the divine feet; aḷavu pōnda – appropriate for measurement; padi – the earth; varāgattu – in the form of varāha (incarnation as wild boar); eyiṟṟu aḷavu – one part of the tusk; pōdā ārū – being insufficient; en kol – how?; un perumai – your greatness; piṟar Ar aṟivār – who can know (other than you)?

**Перевод:**

**pirān un perumai – āḻvār is wondering at the greatness of emperumān. He says “One can only doubt whether you know or do not know your own greatness. No one can doubt that others are incapable of knowing your greatness” (is it not true that no one knows!).  taittirīya upaniśat 2-8-9 says “sō angavēda yadi vā navēda” (does he know or not, his own greatness).**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*āḻvār asks emperumān whether his protective nature was such that some people could measure it! He says that emperumān’s desire to protect was much larger than the objects which were to be protected. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**urāy ulagu aḷanda nānṟu** – If we consider *urāy* is the same as *ulāy* (since the sounds ra and la are interchangeable as per grammar), it will mean wandering. In other words, [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) was wandering all over the place and measuring \[during his incarnation as trivikrama\]. Alternatively, since celestial entities do not set their foot on earth, instead of doing like that on his own possession of earth, emperumān kept his divine foot firmly on top of the heads of all the people. [parāśara bhattar](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/parasara-bhattar/) would mercifully explain this as “when  he effortlessly measured, wandering”

**endai adikkaḷavu pōnda padi varāgattu eyiṟṟaḷavu pōdāvāṟu enkolo** – the earth, which was sufficiently big for you to measure with your divine foot, became insignificant when you took your incarnation as varāha (wild boar) such that it appeared like a dot on the tusk of the boar. How did this happen? On one day, the earth was big enough, but on another day it was insignificant. How is this! Is it not because your desire to protect the earth is much bigger than that of the protected entity, earth! And, I am fully engrossed in such qualities of yours, says the [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/poigai-azhwar/).

We shall take up the 85<sup>th</sup> pāsuram next.

## 85. Padi kandaridiye

*vadivil \[vadivu il\] – vitout any form poṟi aindum – te five sensory perceptions uḷ adakki – controlling tem inside te body pōdodu nīr ēndi – taking flovers and vater neṟi ninṟa – standing firmly in te good pat (of attaining emperumān) nenjamē – ōh heart! nī – you pāmbu aṇaiyinān – emperumān vho has tiruvanantāḻvān (ādiśēśan) as his mattress padi – divine form kaṇḍu aṟidiyē – have you seen and enjoyed? puḷ kodi – (his) garuda flag mast kaṇḍu aṟidiyē – have you vorśipped? kūṟāy – (you) tell*

**Пословный перевод:**

**Перевод:**

**padi kaṇḍu aṟidiyē – this can be construed as āḻWar asking his heart whether it has seen and known emperumān’s qualities or it has seen and  known emperumān’s divine form. padi can mean either quality or physical form. emperumān’s divine physical form is a part of his qualities.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*āḻvār tells his heart to keep enjoying further the boundless greatness of emperumān. His heart asks him “I have been enjoying his greatness. What else should I do?“. āḻvār says “you have only learnt to attain him after controlling your sensory perceptions. What have you seen among his qualities?”. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings: padi kaṇḍu aṟidiyē pāmbaṇaiyinān puḷ kodi kaṇḍu aṟidiyē kūṟāy vadivil poṟi aindum uḷḷadakki pōdodu nīr ēndi neṟi ninṟa nenjamē nī*

**pāmbu aṇaiyinān puḷ kodi kaṇḍu aṟidiyē** – have you seen the way emperumān has given his divine form to tiruvanantāḻvān (ādiśēśan) and known about it? Have you seen his divine form in front of his garuda flag mast and known about it?

**kūṟāy** – you, who said that you have seen everything, please say what you have seen.

**vadivil poṟi aindum uḷḷadakki** – controlling inside the body, without letting them wander outside, the five sensory perceptions starting with ear etc. Alternatively, controlling the formless sensory perceptions. It is implied here that sensory perceptions have diversified desires, instead of converging interests. Has not [tirumangai āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thirumangai-azhwar/) said in periya tirumoḻi 1-6-3 “*kaṇḍavā tirinda*” (wandering as pleased)!

**pōdodu nīr ēndi neṟi ninṟa nenjamē nī** – Oh my heart! you have controlled your senses and you are vorśipping emperumān with flowers and water. Please say what you have seen as the result of your devotion (did you see his divine form when he came in front of his flag?). Can you feel smug with the knowledge about him, before seeing him? While one should feel deeply desirous about enjoying emperumān more and more, can you feel satisfied with mere knowledge? If one were satisfied with a little bit of enjoyment, it would not amount to really attaining emperumān. If one were satisfied with mere knowledge, what had been attained would not be considered as knowledge at all! It would be considered as ignorance. Even [nammāḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/nammazhwar/), who was granted knowledge with the dimension of devotion, through “*madinalam aruḷinan*” (emperumān granted knowledge and devotion), did not feel satiated with that and with the anguish of seeing that emperumān immediately, in the [tiruvāimoḻi 5.3](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2017/06/thiruvaimozhi-5-3-masaru-sodhi/) “*māsaṟu sōdi*”, threatened to carry out *madal* on emperumān (in ancient literature, *madal* is a method by which the beloved girl will inform the whole town about the way the beloved boy had given hopes to her and then showed disinterest in her, with the aim of appealing to the townspeople to get them together)!

We shall consider the 86<sup>th</sup> pāsuram next.

## 86. Niyum tirumagalum

*nīyum tirumagaḷum ninṟāyāl kunṟu eduttup pāyum pani maṟutta paṇbāḷā vāsal kadai kaḻiyā uḷ pugāk kāmar pūm kōval idai kaziyē paṟṟi ini*

**Пословный перевод:**

kunṟu eduttu – lifting gōvardanagiri (like an umbrella); pāyum pani maṟutta – blocking the torrential rain; paṇbāḷā – Oh one who is simple!; vāsal kadai – outside the entrance \[to the āśram where the three āḻvārs were standing\]; kaḻiyā – without going out; uḷ pugā – not entering; kāmar pūm kōval – at tirukkōvalūr which has both natural and artificial beauty; idai kaḻiyē – only the corridor (space between the entrance and the inner portion); paṟṟi – as dwelling place; nīyum tirumagaḷum – periya pirātti (śrī mahālakśmi) and you; ini – now; ninṟāyāl – did you not shower your mercy by standing!

**Перевод:**

**nīyum tirumagaḷum ninṟāyāl – even if pirātti were to complain about your followers to you \[emperumān\], you would reply, as mentioned by periyāḻvār in periyāḻvār tirumoḻi 4-9-2,  “en adiyār adu seyyār” (my follower will not do that). Even for those who knowingly indulge in wrongful deeds, pirātti would plead on their behalf, as mentioned in śrī rāmāyaṇam yudda kāṇḍam 116-45 “nakaścinnāparādyati” (there is none who has not committed a wrongful deed). Such pirātti and you. Did you not set out to the forest only to protect your followers, making it as a solemn vow, as mentioned in śrī rāmāyaṇam ayōdyā kāṇḍam 31-2 “rāgavanca mahāvratam” (it was the great vow by rāghava \[another name for śrī rāma\])! At that time, did sītāppirātti not say as in śrī rāmāyaṇam yudda kāṇḍam 27-6 “agratas tē gamiśyāmi” (I am going ahead of you) in order to protect the followers!**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*emperumān, analysing what this āḻvār wanted, took the responsibility on himself to give what he wanted, came along with pirātti to where he was present and started jostling him. emperumān, unable to hear what this āḻvār was telling his divine mind “have you known emperumān as he is?”, came to where the āḻvār was. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**ninṟāyāl** – this could be construed in any of three ways: 1. [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) came on his own \[to where āḻvār was present\]; 2. emperumān’s mercy is causeless; 3. After emperumān joined with the (three) āḻvārs, he felt at peace.

**kunṟu eduttup pāyum pani maṟutta paṇbāḷā** – Oh the epitome of simplicity, who took a hill to block the rain which was battering the covherds! Just as he lifted the mountain even before it started raining, to protect the cows and covherds from their suffering, emperumān mingled with the āḻvārs and removed his own suffering. Has it not been said in śrī rāmāyaṇam bālakāṇḍam 1-28 “*guhēna sahitō rāma: lakśmanēna ca sītayā*” (only after joining with guhan, śrī rāma felt that he had joined with lakśmaṇa and sītā)!

**vāsal kadai kaḻiyā uḷ pugā** – neither leaving the corridor for the outside nor entering the corridor to go inside. Just as those who are in love wish to stay within the vision of their loved ones, [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) also stayed in the corridor where the three āḻvārs were standing. It appeared to him that there was wildfire on both sides of the corridor! \[on the outer side of the corridor were the people who were interested only in worldly pursuits and on the inner side was the riśi (sage) who tried to reach emperumān through other means \[devotion\] instead of directly approaching emperumān). śrī rāma said in śrī rāmāyaṇam ayōdyā kāṇḍam 97-8 “*yadvinā bharatam tvām śatrugnancāpi mānada, bhavēnmama sukam kincit bhasma tat kurutām śikī*”(let fire burn to ashes the comfort of leaving bharata, you \[lakśmaṇa\] and śatrugna). While the pāsuram initially talked of both emperumān and pirātti (*nīyum tirumagaḷum*) here the reference is only to emperumān. This is because pirātti is like the shadow of emperumān; hence there is no need to refer separately to the shadow!

**kāmar pūm kōval idai kaḻiyē paṟṟi ini** – this could be construed as either [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) is dwelling now at the corridor, at the desirous , beautiful tirukkōvalūr or he will henceforth dwell like this. The word *ini* refers to getting something which had been lost since the beginning. nammāḻvār has also mercifully said in [tiruvāimoḻi 2-7-3](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2016/04/thiruvaimozhi-2-7-3-madhavan/) “*ini ippāṟpattadu yādavangaḷum sērkodēn*” (henceforth I will not allow any faults to come)! emperumān did not come to tirukkōvalūr to rid the effect of his karma! It was to prevent samsāris such as ourselves (dwellers of this materialistic realm) from going to hell to experience the effect of our karma! īsn’t tirukkōvalūr the divyadēśam (divine abode of emperumān) which sowed the seed for [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) showering *mayarvaṟa madinalam* (granting of knowledge and devotion) on all [āḻvārs](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/azhwars/)!

We shall consider pāsuram 87 next.

## 87. Iniyar puguvar

*iniyār puguvār eḻu naraga vāsal muniyādu mūrittāḷ kōmin kanisāyak kanṟu eṟinda tōḷān kanai kaḻalē kāṇbadaṟku nangaṟinda nāvalam sūḻ nādu*

**Пословный перевод:**

kani – the demon who was in the form of wood-apple; sāya – to fall down and perish; kanṟu – demon who came in the form of a calf; eṟinda – thrown; tōḷān – emperumān who has divine shoulders; kanai kaḻalē – divine feet with ornaments which make a sound; kāṇbadaṟku – to see (the path); am nāval sūḻ nādu – the living creatures in the expansive country which is beautiful and goes by the name of jambhūdvīpam; nangu aṟindana – knew very well; ini – henceforth; eḻu naraga vāsal – at the gates of the seven types of hell; yār puguvār – who will enter; muniyādu – without getting angry (at me); mūri tāḷ kōmin – close (the gates of hell) with strong latch

**Перевод:**

**eḻu naraga vāsal ini yār puguvār – Who will enter the gates to the seven hell henceforth? After emperumān said in śrī bhagavat gītā 18-66 “sarvapāpēbhyō mōkśayiśyāmi” (I shall liberate from all sins) what is the purpose for various types of hell where one has to experience the effects of those sins? eḻu naraga vāsal could also be construed as the gate to hell attained by those who leave from their physical forms and rise. After the incarnation of āḻvārs, there is no old samsāram (materialistic realm) \[that is, the samsāram that used to be dreadful is now changed\].**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*āḻvār says that after emperumān came here \[tirukkōvalūr\], there will be no one to go to naragam (hell). Those in charge of naragam will have to close the gates and leave the place. āḻvār is mercifully saying what nammāḻvār mercifully said in tiruvāimoḻi 5-2-1 “naliyum naragamum nainda namanukku ingu yādonṟumillai” (hell which causes suffering, was destroyed. There is no work for yama (Lord of righteousness and the one in charge of hell) in this place. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**muniyādu** – there is no significance for the anger of *yamakinkarars* (servitors of yama). [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/poigai-azhwar/) tells them “Hence please listen to what I am saying without getting angry that I am talking of the destruction of your place”. If you get angry, you will suffer the same fate that befell the demons who came in the form of wood-apple and calf.

**mūrittāḷ kōmin** – Close the hell with a huge latch. Only if the closure is a temporary one, would one need to use a small latch. Since there is no work for hell, close it with a huge latch.

**kani sāyak kanṟu eṟinda tōḷān kanai kaḻalē kāṇbadaṟku** – to see the divine feet, which make a sound due to ornaments \[anklets\], of the one who has beautiful shoulders, who threw a calf which was possessed by a demon, to kill a wood-apple which was possessed by another demon. The sound of the ornaments donned on the divine feet of [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/), which he changed while he targeted the wood-apple with a calf, is referred to here as *kanai kaḻal*.

**nāvalam sūḻ nādu nangu aṟinda** – the people in this world called *jambūdvīpam* (nāvalam tīvu) knew beautifully well the divine feet of the one who has the capability to destroy enemies. They will not leave this earth which has tirukkōvalūr in it, where emperumān has taken residence. This becomes the reason for emperumān to protect us. Haven’t the sages of daṇḍakāraṇya told śrī rāma  in śrī rāmāyaṇam āraṇya kāṇḍam 1-19 “*tē vayam bhavatā rakśyā bhavat viśayavāsina:*” (we, who live in your country, are to be protected by you)! Mentioning of tirukkōvalūr here implies that this will be true of all divyadēsams (divine abodes) where [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) has taken residence \[as arccāvatāram, idol form\]. The [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/poigai-azhwar/) believes that the divyadēśams  where the aruḷicceyal (collection of all [divyaprabandam](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/) of āḻvārs) is learnt, form the body for the country.

We shall consider the 88<sup>th</sup> pāsuram next.

adiyēn kriṣṇa ramanuja dāsan

## 88. Nadilum ninnadiye

*nādilum ninnadiyē nāduvan nādōṟum pādilum nin pugaḻē pāduvan sūdilum pon āḻi ēndinān ponnadiyē sūduvēṟku ennāgil ennē enakku*

**Пословный перевод:**

nādilum – when thinking (with the mind); nin adiyē – only your divine feet; nāduvan – I shall think of; pādilum – when singing (with the mouth); nin pugaḻē pāduvan – I shall sing only your praises; sūdilum – when thinking to wear (something) on the head; pon āḻi ēndinān – holding the divine disc, your; pon adiyē – beautiful divine feet; sūduvēṟku – one, wearing (on the head); enakku – for me; en āgil en – what if something happens (to someone)?

**Перевод:**

**nādilum nin adiyē nāduvan – when I am searching with the help of my heart, I shall search only for your divine feet.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*In the previous (87th) pāsuram, āḻvār said that the world has understood the greatness of emperumān. The reason for this is that tirukkōvalūr is a part of this earth. But, despite understanding the greatness of emperumān, the earth did not get to attain him. It appeared that yama (the deity of righteousness) had time \[here\], looking at the activities of samsāris (dwellers of this materialistic realm). Despite sītāppirātti telling rāvaṇa in śrī rāmāyaṇam sundara kāṇḍam 21-20 “tēna maitrī bhavatu tē” (let friendship happen for you with śrī rāma) and śri rāma saying, as in śrī rāmāyaṇam yudda kāṇḍam 18-19 “yati vā rāvaṇasvayam” (even if it is rāvaṇa himself, bring him), rāvaṇa did not bow down to śri Rama and lost his heads, did not he! Thus, āḻvār looked at himself and said “Let the word do whatever it wants. Just as it is mentioned in tiruvāimoḻi 1-2-8 “uḷḷam urai seyal uḷḷa immūnṟaiyum” (the three faculties of mind, speech and action), I am indeed fortunate to engage with emperumān with the three faculties of mind, speech and action!”. When everyone lost his belonging to a waylaying robber, one person alone escaped from that; in the same way while the rest of the world is engaged in worldly pursuits, I am able to involve with emperumān and my mind, speech and actions are totally engaged with him, says the āḻvār. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**nādōṟum pādilum nin pugaḻē pāduvan** – when praising every day, I shall praise only your glory. The usage of the long syllable \[E\] in *adiyē* and *pugaḻē* implies that carrying out these is not with some other benefit in mind, but keeping the activity itself \[searching or praising\] as the benefit.

**sūdilum pon āḻi ēndinān pon adiyē sūduvēṟku** – for me who would wear only the beautiful divine feet of yours, who are holding the beautiful divine disc, when I want to wear anything on my head

**ennāgil ennē enakku** – how does it matter to me as to what happens to the others who are in this world? Another interpretation – even if I go to śrīvaikuṇṭam, I will be engrossed with [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) with my mind, speech and action; since I have attained that here itself, what does it matter as to where I am? Yet another interpretation – what is the point in feeling sorry for the samsāris? They will attain emperumān if they know about him and will lose, if they do not know about him. What does it matter to me? While the means \[to reach emperumān\] is through his mercy, isn’t the loss due to our activities? [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/poigai-azhwar/) says that he obtained the benefit because emperumān is in tirukkōvalūr. Is it possible for me to correct the samsāris? He completes the pāsuram feeling happy that at least he was able to benefit.

Let us proceed to the 89<sup>th</sup> pāsuram next.

## 89. Enakkavar ar oruvare

*enakkāvār ār oruvarē em perumān tankkāvān tanē maṟṟallāl punakkāyām pūmēni kāṇapp podiyaviḻum pūvaippū māmēni kāttum varam*

**Пословный перевод:**

punam – on the land appropriate to itself; kāyām pū mēni – the colour of kāyām pū (a blue coloured flower); kāṇa – as one keeps looking; podi aviḻum – blossoming; pūvaippū (mēni) – the colour of pūvaippū (blue coloured flower); mā mēni – (emperumān’s) great divine form; varam kāttum – will display properly; enakku – to me (who is so fortunate); Ar oruvarē āvār – is there anyone who is comparable to me?; emperumān – sarvēśvaran (supreme being) also; tāne tanakku āvān – he can be compared only to himself; maṟṟu – can he become my equal?

**Перевод:**

**enakku āvār Ar oruvarē – āḻvār asks “Is there anyone who is comparable to me?”**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*āḻvār recounts the happiness that he had mentioned in the previous pāsuram. Just as it is rare for samsāris to think of emperumān, it is rare for āḻvār to forget him. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

But why \[do you ask this\], is [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) not comparable to you?

**emperumān tankkāvān tāne maṟṟu allāl** – emperumān is comparable only to himself and cannot be compared to me. emperumān did not become a servitor to someone and get this benefit! Did he not come on his own, without having to be a servitor to someone! Does he have any glory as I do, about having received someone’s grace? When asked “why are you talking as if you do not have any shortcoming? Have you seen emperumān well? You still have a grievance that you have not seen him yet!”. [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/poigai-azhwar/) responds that in order to get rid of his grievance of not having seen emperumān, he has seen those entities which are like him. I am on the same land where there are objects which look like him; there is a difference of just a foot between him and me, says the āḻvār.

**punam kāyam pū mēni** – the colour of kāyām pū (a blue coloured flower) which is fresh as it is on the land which is appropriate for it.

**kāṇap podi aviḻum pūvaip pū** – the colour of *pūvaip pū* (another blue coloured flower) which blossoms as it is being looked at.

These

**māmēni kāttum varam** – if the word *varam* is taken to mean “*vṟiyata iti varam*” – ‘that which is wished for’, the meaning for this verse will be: show the divine form of [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) who is wished for. If the word is taken to mean *variśtam* – beautiful, the meaning for the verse will be: show the divine form of emperumān beautifully. Hasn’t [nammāḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/nammazhwar/) mercifully conveyed the same meaning in his periya tiruvandādi pāsuram 73 “*pūvaiyum kāyāvum neelamum pūkkinṟa kāvimalar enṟum kāṇḍōṟum pāviyēn mellāvi mey migavē pūrikkum avvavaiyellām emperumān uruvē enṟu*” (flowers such as pūvai, kāyā etc have the same colour as emperumān’s divine form; thus whenever I look at them I feel like looking at emperumān himself)!

We shall consider the 90<sup>th</sup> pāsuram next.

## 90. Varattal vali ninaindu

*varattāl vali ninaindu mādava nin pādam sirattāl vaṇangānām enṟē urattināl īrariyāy nēr valiyōnāna iraṇiyanai ōr ariyāl nī idandadu ūn*

**Пословный перевод:**

īr ari āy – being the enemy appropriate to be cut; nēr valiyōn Ana – having the strength to fight in direct combat; iraṇiyanai – hiraṇya kaśyap’s; Un – body; ōr ari āy – unique form of narasimha (face of lion and body of human); urattināl – through (your) strength; nī idandadu – you tore; varattāl – through boons (granted by brahmā and other celestial entities); vali ninaindu – thinking of (his) huge strength; sirattāl – through his head; nin pādam – your divine feet; vaṇangānām enṟē – was it because he did not worship? (no; it was because emperumān could not tolerate the faults \[committed by hiraṇya kaśyap\] towards his follower prahlāda)

**Перевод:**

**varattāl vali ninaindu – iraṇiyan (hiraṇya kaśyap) thought a great deal about the boons that he had obtained from various deities, who were given that power \[to grant boons\] by emperumān. He had thought a mountain out of a mole hill. With the boons obtained, he thought that he was more powerful than emperumān himself. Alternatively, he did not know that the deities who granted him the boons were under the control of emperumān.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*In the previous pāsuram, āḻvār pointed out to entities which are like emperumān and how he sustained himself. In this pāsuram, he mentions about how emperumān removed the enemy of his devotee prahlāda through his physical form and how he gave himself to prahlāda. āḻvār mentions about emperumān’s qualities of removing the enemies of his followers and showering affection on his followers. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**mādava** – isn’t the consort of [tirumagaḷ (śrī mahālakśmi)](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sri-mahalakshmi/), the entity to be attained by everyone!

**nin pādam sirattāl vaṇangānām enṟē** – Did you kill him because he did not bow down to your divine feet? You did not kill him because he considered you as an enemy. You killed him because he considered the child prahlāda as his enemy and tortured him. Had you ever thought that he had committed fault against you? You only thought that he had not attained the benefit that he should have and that he did not regret his actions. It could also be considered that [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) did not want his property (the soul of iraṇiyan) to go in the wrong path and end up suffering and hence killed him.

**urattināl** – you destroyed him through your strength. Though he had obtained so many boons, even iraṇiyan had to retreat after seeing your strength .

**īr ariyāy nēr valiyōnāna iraṇiyanai** – he was the enemy apt to be torn asunder, strong enough to engage with you in direct combat. Earlier [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/poigai-azhwar/) had spoken of the power of his boons (*varattāl vali ninaindu*). Here he is referring to the strength of his shoulders (*nēr valiyōn*).

**urattināl īr ariyāy** – an enemy fit to be broken through strength; enemy fit to be killed. Alternatively, he was the enemy whose body you had torn with your strength.

**ōr ariyāy** – as a unique lion. [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/poigai-azhwar/) is saying that he is the incomparable *narasingam* (combination of lion and human being), anywhere in the world. Another explanation – he is the *narasingam* that his followers will always aim to attain, thinking of the activity that he had undertaken for the sake of his follower.  In other words, this indicates the way his followers, coming after the time of prahlāda, will rid themselves of their fear, after meditating on [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/)’s activity in protecting prahlāda from his enemy. *ōr ariyāy* – unlike the other lions which were created by brahmā, this was the incarnation that emperumān took out, of his own volition.

**nī idandadu Un** – you tore his body. You carried out this activity to benefit iraṇiyan so that iraṇiyan’s soul will not suffer further as he had not vorśipped you, an act that was apt for iraṇiyan. īsn’t vorśipping [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) the apt activity for the soul! Several pramāṇams (authentic sources) have been quoted in the vyākhyānam to substantiate this point: (a) [nammāḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/nammazhwar/)’s tiruvāimoḻi 6-5-1 “*toḻum ivaḷ*” (she who worships) (b)” *prahvānjaliputastita:*” (he stood  in humility with folded palms) (c) mahābhāratam śānti parvam 344-45 “*nama: ityēdi vādina:*” (those who say  *nama:* (I am totally dependent on you)). (Physical) Body is formed in order to make the person suffer, just as it happened in the case of kaikēyi! Did you tear apart iraṇiyan’s body because he did not worship you! No. (it was only because he caused hardship to prahlāda).

We shall take up the 91<sup>st</sup> pāsuram next.

## 91. Unak kurumbaiyin

*ūnak kurumbaiyin uḷ pukku iruḷ nīkki jñānac cudar koḷīi nāḷ dōṟum ēnattu uruvāy ulagu idanda ūḻiyān pādam maruvādārkku uṇḍāmō vān*

**Пословный перевод:**

ūnak kurumbaiyin – in the hut called body made of flesh; uḷ pukku – entering (analysing); iruḷ – ignorance (which considers the body as sweet); nīkki – removing; jñānam sudar – lamp of true knowledge; koḷīi – lighting; ēnattu uru āy – incarnating as varāha form (wild boar); ulagu idanda – one who separated the world from the shell of the universe; ūḻiyān – one who was present during deluge, that emperumān’s; pādam – divine feet; nāḷ dōṟum – every day; maruvādārkku – those who do not worship; vān – paramapadam (śrīvaikuṇṭam); uṇḍāmō – will it be available?

**Перевод:**

**ūnak kurumbaiyin uḷ pukku – reaching up to the point where faults are located inside the body which is having abundance of faults. īsn’t the body made of flesh! ḍoesn’t body, looking at it superficially, appear to be good to look at, hiding its qualities of being lowly, mean, unstable, worthless, disgusting, covered with a layer of skin to hide the inner features! Body is referred to as a hut since the body is like a hut for ātmā (soul).**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*āḻvār says that even though emperumān is such that he would grant whatever we ask of him, other than those who get rid of their desire for worldly pursuits and surrender to his divine feet, it is very difficult to attain paramapadam (śrīvaikuṇṭam). In order to attain the goal, śouldn’t one get an inkling about lowly matters that they are lowly and attainable matters that they are to be attained? In the previous pāsuram, āḻvār mentioned about the help that emperumān rendered to one (prahlāda) who had desire towards him. In this pāsuram, he says that desire is the only one which is required. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**iruḷ nīkki** – removing the ignorance which considers body as pleasurable

**jñānac cudal koḷīi** – lighting the lamp of knowledge. Knowing the characteristics of ātmā (soul) as they are, enabling knowledge about [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/), forgetting about self which is an attribute and focussing on emperumān who is the substrate \[holding the attribute\]. The characteristic of ātmā is not something which is formed at one point of time and removed at another. It is there always. If it is hidden, thoughts seeking other benefits \[instead of service to emperumān\] and about other means \[instead of emperumān’s divine feet\] will come on their own.

**nāḷ dōṟum maruvādārkku uṇḍāmō vān** – will paramapadam (śrīvaikuṇṭam) fructify for those who do not worship at the divine feet of [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) who transformed himself in order to protect his possessions (when he protected earth by transforming himself as varāha, the boar) unlike those who try to save themselves when their possessions are being destroyed?

**nāḷ dōṟum** – would śrīvaikuṇṭam be attainable for those who do not think everyday ,with total focus, of the divine feet of emperumān who dug out the earth during his incarnation as varāha?

**ūḻiyān** – he protects us even during deluge, when we are not there and he is present. An incident is quoted here about periya kōyil dāsar, who was a disciple of [nanjīyar](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/nanjiyar/) (ācāryan of [nampiḷḷai](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/nampillai/), who is the commentator for this prabandam). periya kōyil dāsar, who had surrendered to the divine feet of nanjīyar, did not come to nanjīyar for many days. One day he came to nanjīyar and heard a few good words. One of the persons present there at that time, told dāsar “it has been a long time since you came here” to which dāsar responded saying “isn’t my goal a certainty when I attained the divine feet of nanjīyar? My loss is a few days when I did not see him in-between” The person (who spoke to him first) realised that dāsar has given up the habit (and the faith due to that) of coming to his ācāryan everyday and listening to his good words. He asked dāsar “has not [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/nammazhwar/) mercifully sung the pāsuram ‘*nāḷ dōṟum maruvādārkku uṇḍāmō vān*?’. *Have you not heard of this pāsuram*?’ *You do not speak like someone who has seen the difference of having vorśipped at the divine feet of nanjīyar for a few days”*. While atonement has been mentioned \[in sacred texts\] for those who, out of ignorance, miss a step when carrying out their karma (deeds, both good and bad), has it been stated that [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) can be attained even by those who do not have a desire in attaining him? Would a person, who has once surrendered to emperumān but who goes about his usual ways in pursuing worldly matters in samsāram \[after this act of surrendering\], get his desired benefit? He had missed out on the benefit \[of carrying out service to emperumān\] not because he had no qualification for it but because he had not desired it. In order to enjoy emperumān, it is not that only [cētana](https://granthams.koyil.org/2013/03/06/thathva-thrayam-chith-who-am-i/) (sentient entity) is required; the desire \[by cētana\] is also required. When obtaining such a huge benefit, śouldn’t at least desire be there?

Let us consider the 92<sup>nd</sup> pāsuram next.
