## 92. Vanagit tiyay

*vānāgit tīyāy maṟi kadalāy mārudamāy tēnāgip pālām tirumālē ānāycci veṇṇey viḻunga niṟaiyumē munnorunāḷ maṇṇai umiḻnda vayiṟu*

**Пословный перевод:**

vān āgi – taking the form of ether (sky); tī āy – taking the form of agni (fire); maṟi kadal āy – taking the form of ocean; mārudam āy – taking the form of wind; tēn āgi – (for nityars and muktars, permanent dwellers of śrīvaikuṇṭam and those who have got liberated from samsāram and have reached śrīVaikuṇṭam respectively) being sweet, like honey; pāl Am – he will be enjoyable, similar to milk; tirumālē – Oh the consort of mahālakśmi!; An āycci – when born in the clan of covherds who tend to cows; veṇṇey viḻunga – when swallowing butter; mun oru nāḷ – once upon a time; maṇṇai – all the worlds; umiḻnda – spat out; vayiṟu – the divine stomach; niṟaiyumē – will it get filled-up?

**Перевод:**

**vān āgit tīyāy maṟi kadalāy mārudamāy – being the controller of all those who are living in the worlds, made of the five elements (sky, fire, water, wind and earth)**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*The earlier pāsuram mentioned about emperumān protecting his followers. In this pāsuram, it is mentioned that  even though emperumān has attained everything, he still considers those materials which have been touched by his followers as the ones sustaining him. In each of his incarnations, he considers whatever materials sustain those who are born in that species as those which sustain him. As examples, when he incarnated as varāha (boar), the tuber kōraikkiḻangu was his sustaining foodstuff and when he incarnated as kriṣṇa (in the clan of covherds), butter sustained him. Let us look at the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**tēnāgip pālām tirumālē** – cāndōjña upaniśat 3-14-2 says “*sarvarasa:*” (emperumān is sweet so that those who are in paramapadam (śrīvaikuṇṭam) can enjoy him). [nampiḷḷai](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/nammazhwar/), with lot of involvement, will mercifully say that it is impossible to match the beauty of the way in which [nanjīyar](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/nanjiyar/) (his ācārya) would explain the line *tēnāgip pālām tirumālē*. Thus, by the words *vānāgi* and *tēnāgi* both nitya vibhūti (spiritual realm) and leelā vibhūti (materialistic realm) have been referred to. His status as the consort of śrī mahālakśmi is superior to these two, in terms of his wealth.

**An āycci vṇṇey viḻunga niṟaiyumē** – this line implies that even though he has no unfulfilled desires, there are still certain materials that he desires. Butter is more desirable to emperumān than pirātti’s (śrī mahālakśmi’s) divine bosom. pirātti’s bosom is for his enjoyment whereas butter is for his sustenance. Hasn’t [nammāḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/nammazhwar/) mercifully said in [tiruvāimoḻi 1-3-1](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2015/06/thiruvaimozhi-1-3-1-paththudai-adiyavar/) “*malar magaḷ virumbum nam arumpeṟaladigaḷ – uralinōdu iṇaindirundu ēngiya eḷivu – ettiṟam*”(how simple and amazing is the quality of our svāmy (lord) who is very much desired by pirātti and is very rare to attain, who was tied down to a mortar and had a forlorn look!)

**An āycci veṇṇey viḻunga niṟaiyumē mun oru nāḷ maṇṇai umiḻnda vayiṟu** – once upon a time, when there was deluge, you had saved the earth by keeping it in your stomach and later spat it out so that it could see other spatial bodies. Did you eat butter to fill up such a stomach (which held the entire earth within itself)?

**An āycci veṇṇey** –  covherd women’s butter is just a material. It appears that kaṇṇan (śrī kriṣṇa) would not eat the butter if the strength of smell of the covherd clan is reduced even a little bit in the butter!

**niṟaiyumē** – will it fill? Is it not required to stuff the entire expansive space inside the stomach with butter? Just as the samsāris will suffer because of deluge, kaṇṇan will also suffer if he does not get butter. It is said that [piḷḷai uṟangā villi dāsar](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/pillai-urangavilli-dhasar/) (a disciple of [śrī rāmānuja](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/emperumanar/)) would say that the space inside kaṇṇan’s stomach, which will not get filled up however much butter he eats, could be let out to vaśermen \[the meaning implied here is that kaṇṇan’s stomach is similar to the vaśerman’s pot which will never get filled up however much clothes are filled in it for applying starch\].

Let us take up the 93<sup>rd</sup> pāsuram next.

adiyēn kriṣṇa ramanuja dāsan

## 93. Vayiralala valuruvi

*vayiṟu aḻala vāḷuruvi vandānai anja eyiṟu ilaga vāy maduttadu en nī poṟi ugirāl pū vadivai īdu aḻitta pon āḻik kaiyā nin sēvadimēl  īdaḻiyac ceṟṟu*

**Пословный перевод:**

pū vadivai – the beauty of flowers; īdu aḻitta – lowering their esteem very much; pon āḻi kaiyā – having a divine hand which holds the divine cakkaram (divine disc); vayiṟu aḻala – (followers) to grieve with sorrow; vāḷ uruvi vandānai – iraṇiyan who came wielding his sword; anja – to become fearful (of your divine form); nin sēvadimēl – (keeping) on your reddish divine feet; poṟi ugirāl – with multi coloured finger nails; īdu aḻiyac ceṟṟu – even after destroying to smithereens; eyiṟu ilaga – showing brightly your teeth; nī vāy maduttadu en – why did you fold your tongue?

**Перевод:**

**vayiṟu aḻala vāḷ uruvi vandānai – after seeing the way iraṇiyan came wielding his sword, emeprumān’s followers were grieving as to what harm will befall him. Just as tirumangai āḻvār says in periya tirumoḻi 5-7-5 “enganē uyvar dānavar ninaittāl” (how will the demons sustain themselves when they think \[of what happened to hiraṇya kaśyap\]?), emperumān’s followers also were afraid after seeing the divine form of narasimha (combination of lion face and human torso).**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*The previous pāsuram mentioned about the partiality that emperumān shows towards his followers. emperumān asks āḻvār “is there such a partiality in me? I have not known of such partiality within me”. āḻvār responds through this pāsuram “if it veren’t there, did the rage that you showed against iraṇiyan (hiraṇya kaśyap) an act?”. Did you show your anger because he was an apt enemy for you? In the previous pāsuram, emperumān’s quality of vātsalyam (motherly love) was mentioned. This pāsuram mentions the rage born out of that vātsalyam. Let us enjoy the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**vayiṟu aḻala** – to grieve with sorrow. Who is grieving with sorrow? It is āḻvār. āḻvār is thinking that iraṇiyan is wielding his sword now, against narasimha, instead of thinking that he had done like that several *yugas* (eons) back. This is similar to [periyāḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/periyazhwar/) saying with the emotional stance of yaśōdā in periyāḻvār tirumoḻi 3-3-7 “*enṟum en piḷḷaikkut tīmai seyvārgaḷ anganam āvārgaḷē*” (those who harm my son (kriṣṇa) will suffer the same way). This is also similar to sugrīva telling śrī rāma in śrī rāmāyaṇam yudda kāṇḍam 17-5 “*asmān hantum na samśaya:*” (without doubt he (vibhīśaṇa) has come only to kill us” \[such is their fear for the safety of emperumān\].

**anja eyiṟu ilaga vāy maduttadu en nī** – why did you fold you tongue to display your teeth, in such a way that iraṇiyan was scared? īsn’t this ostentatious laughter appearing sweet to pirāttis (consorts of emperumān) also? This act has not been done because there was no other way! This laughter, with your teeth visible, is more liked by pirāttimārs than the gentle smile that you show when you are together with them.

**poṟi ugirāl** – with the finger nails, made of many colours

**pū vadivai īdu aḻitta pon āḻik kaiyā** – Oh emperumān who are holding in your hand, the divine disc which has won over even flowers by its softness and beauty! The word *ponnāḻi* could also be construed to refer to the special ring on his hand.

**nin sēvadi mēl īdaḻiyac ceṟṟu** – keeping iraṇiyan on his divine feet which is used by his pirāttimārs (consorts) to recline; keeping iraṇiyan on his divine feet which his followers, like ourselves, desire; blowing iraṇiyan to smithereens such that he cannot be stuck together again, and then…

**eyiṟu ilaga vāy madittadu en nī** – why did you fold your tongue and remain angry without controlling it, even after killing iraṇiyan with your divine nails, instead of killing him either through your vow or through your divine disc? īsn’t the reason for this due to your love for your devotee prahlāda! Even after killing iraṇiyan, didn’t you take your own time to reduce your rage! emperumān himself remains in a way similar to that mentioned by śrī rāma in śrī rāmāyaṇam yudda kāṇḍam 114-10 “*maraṇāntāni vairāṇi*” (enmity ceases with the occurrence of death). When a danger befell his follower sugrīva in śrī rāmāyaṇam yudda kāndam 41-4 did he not say “*tvayi kincit samāpannē kim kāryam sītayā mama*” (what is the benefit for me with sītā if any danger comes your way)? The term *vātsalyam* does not refer to the cow suckling its calf. It is pushing out its earlier born calf with its horn, in order to protect the calf that it gave birth to on that day! Just as [tirumangai āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thirumangai-azhwar/) mentioned in periya tirumoḻi 5-8-3 “*koṇḍa sīṟṟam  uṇḍuḷadu*” (there is an anger that you took upon yourself), it is this rage that he takes against his enemies which provides the refuge to his followers when his followers are threatened. Is there any limit to the rage that śrī rāma had when his devotee hanuman was injured by rāvaṇa when rāvaṇa shot his arrows? śrī rāmāyaṇam yudda kāṇḍam describes this in its ślōka 59-136 “*tatō rāmō mahā tējā:*” (śrī rāma was radiant then). rāvaṇa who was looking at this could not look śrī rāma in his face. Is it possible to control an ocean if it starts burning? “*rāvaṇēna krutam vraṇam*” (the injury created by rāvaṇa) – rāvaṇa created an insignificant problem for hanuman as much as a mosquito will create for a lion. Is this injury (created by rāvaṇa) anywhere near that which hanuman experienced when he tried to pluck sun thinking that it was a fruit, when he was wandering in the sky? śrī rāma became angry because he could not tolerate even this small injury to his follower hanuman. “*drustvā kōpasya vaśamēyivān*” (on seeing, he came under the control of anger). After seeing the injury to hanuman, śrī rāma could not be seen. He forgot himself. He forgot hanuman. hanuman had earlier said “*na rāvaṇa sahasram mē*” (even if a thousand rāvaṇas come, they cannot equal me). But rāma thought of hanuman as a monkey \[and not as a mighty warrior when rāvaṇa showered his arrows on hanuman\]. “*kōpaysa vaśamēyivān*” (he came under the control of anger). Just as hanuman appeared to lose to someone who is not his equal, śrī rāma lost to his anger which was not up to his standard. It was that śrī rāma who became enraged who was described as in śrī rāmāyaṇam ayōdyā kāṇḍam 20-4 “*nakrudyatyapiśabdōpi krōdanīyāni varjayan*” (since he avoids giving space for anger, even if he is abused he does not become angry) and as said in śrī rāmāyaṇam ayōdyā kāṇḍam  1-10 “*sa ca nityam prāśāntātmā mrudupūrvam ca bhāśatē ucyamānōpi paruśam nōttaram pratipadyatē* ” (always having a soft heart, he talks softly. Even if others use harsh words, he does not respond to that)!

We shall take up the 94<sup>th</sup> pāsuram next.

adiyēn kriṣṇa ramanuja dāsan

## 94. Serrelundu

*seṟṟeḻundu tīviḻittuc cenṟa inda ēḻulagum maṟṟivaiyā enṟu vāy angāndu muṟṟum maṟaiyavarkkuk kāttiya māmēniyanai allāl iṟaiyēnum ēttādu en nā*

**Пословный перевод:**

seṟṟu eḻundu – getting up angrily; tī viḻittu – looking with fiery eyes (at duryōdana and others); senṟa inda ēḻu ulagaum maṟṟu ivai – these are the seven worlds which keep going about; A enṟu vāy angāndu – opening his divine mouth wide open; muṟṟūm – all the worlds; maṟaiyavarkku – to bhīśma, drōṇa and other vaidikas (those who believe in vēdams); kāttiya – showed (in his mouth); māyavanai allāl – other than kriṣṇa who showed amazing power (no one else); en Na – my tongue; iṟaiyēnum – even a little bit; ēttādu – will not praise; eḻundu – getting up angrily (to destroy the worlds); tī viḻittu – looking with fiery eyes; seṟṟu – destroying (all the worlds); senṟa inda ēḻ ulagum maṟṟu ivai – (saying) these are the seven worlds that went (inside my stomach); āvenṟu vāy angāndu – opening his divine mouth wide open; muṟṟum – all the worlds; maṟaiyavarkku – to mārkaṇḍēya riśi (sage), a vaidika (one who believes in vēdams); kāttiya – one who showed mercifully; māyavanai allāl – other than emperumān who has agatitagatanā sāmartyam (the ability to bind together those which cannot be bound); en nā – my tongue; iṟaiyēnum – even a little bit; ēttādu – will not praise

**Перевод:**

**When the reference is to duryōdana and others**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*āḻvār, who was thinking of the danger from iraṇiyan (hiraṇya kaśyap) \[in the previous pāsuram\] now thinks of the danger from duryōdana’s group. In this pāsuram, āḻvār is mercifully saying about the viśvarūpam that emperumān manifested before mahābhārata war to bhīśma and others or as an alternative explanation, how he showed to riśi (sage) mārkaṇḍēya, the worlds that he had swallowed during deluge. It would be appropriate to consider the first two words in this pāsuram “seṟṟeḻundu tīviḻittu”  together with the incarnation as narasimha in the previous pāsuram or with emperumān’s act as in this pāsuram. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

\[Just before the mahābhārata war, kriṣṇa had gone to the court of dritarāśtra as a messenger from yudiśtra and other pāṇḍavas. duryōdana, desiring to kill kriṣṇa, had put a false seated throne for him when he visited the court. As soon as kriṣṇa sat on that throne, the throne got pulled down and kriṣṇa went down where duryōdana had arranged for wrestlers to kill kriṣṇa. But kriṣṇa took his viśvarūpam (a form which encompasses the whole universe with all its galaxies) and showed it to bhīśma, drōṇa and such other vaidikas, who follow vēdams (sacred texts)\]

**seṟṟeḻundu tīviḻittu** – getting angry on duryōdana et al who said “your coming here as a messenger is a mistake; pāṇḍavas sending you as a messenger is mistake”, who laid out a false throne for him, who abused vidura and who carried out contemptuous deeds to [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) and his followers, and looking at them with fiery eyes.

**senṟa inda ēḻulagum maṟṟivai A enṟu vāy angāndu** – opening his divine mouth widely and telling them to see the worlds which are in the universe, inside his divine mouth

**muṟṟum maṟaiyvaṟku kāttiya māyavanai** – the amazing entity \[emperumān\] who showed all the worlds to vaidikas such as bhīśma, drōṇa et al.

**māyavanai** – this indicates the *agatitagatanā śakti* (the ability to bind entities which cannot be bound) of [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/), when he showed inside his mouth, the worlds which are floating in the universe outside.

**māyavanai allāl iṟaiyēnum ettādu en nā** – my tongue will not praise anyone other than that amazing benefactor *vatataḷasāyi* (one who lies on the banyan leaf). Here the reference \[to not praising others is not to other dēvatas, but\] is to the other forms of [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) such as param (supreme being in śrīvaikuṇṭam), vyūha (his form in milky ocean) and other vibhava forms (incarnations).

**en nā** – my tongue which is unable to eat because it is not praising emperumān.

**en Na ēttādu** – since [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/poigai-azhwar/) has said in the [64](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2017/08/mudhal-thiruvandhadhi-64/)[<sup>th</sup>](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2017/08/mudhal-thiruvandhadhi-64/) [pāsuram](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2017/08/mudhal-thiruvandhadhi-64/) of this prabandam ‘*tirumālai alladu deyvam enrēttēn*” (I will not worship anyone other than the consort of śrī mahālakśmi) he has already confirmed that he will not praise other deities. Hence there is no need for him to convey the same message here. Instead, he is saying that he will not praise other forms of [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) \[as mentioned above\].

**When the reference is to mārkaṇḍēya maharishi**

**eḻundu** – just as a mother throws out a stick that her child is playing with when the child tries to poke itself with that stick, [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/), desiring to put an end to the world when he sees the samsāris (dwellers of materialistic realm) utilising the sensory perceptions that he had given them in the wrong ways as this would disable them from harming themselves more and more, gets up angrily.

**tī viḻittu** – looking at the samsāris with fiery eyes such that they will get annihilated by his mere look, manifesting in his eyes, the anger in his heart, at their improper deeds.

**senṟa inda ēḻulagum seṟṟu** – destroying the seven worlds such that they (the inhabitants of these worlds) will be without a form and name, and keeping them within himself

**ēḻ ulagum** – mentioning seven worlds implies all the worlds

**maṟṟu** – more than that

**ivaiyāvenṟu** – aren’t the destroyed entities these

**vāyangāndu** – opening his divine mouth widely

**muṟṟum** – as mentioned in mahābhāratam āraṇya kāṇḍam 191-123 “*yacca kincin mayā lōkē druśtam stāvarajangamam, tata: paśyamaham sarvam tasya kukśau mahātmana:*” (whatever entities in the world which were seen seen by me, whether mobile or immobile, I have seen them in that supreme being’s divine stomach), all the destroyed worlds

**maṟaiyavaṟkuk kāttiya māyavanani** – the amazing [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) who showed to *mārkaṇḍēya bhagavān* (great sage mārkaṇḍēya) all the worlds as they were before \[annihilation\].

**māyavanai** – how amazing is this! It is amazing that even after destroying all the entities leaving them as they were for mārkaṇḍēya to see! It is amazing that even after removing their names and forms, leaving them as before, for mārkaṇḍēya to see! The opinion here is that no one else can demonstrate such an *agatitagatanā śakti* (power to bind those entities which cannot be bound together)

**māyavanai allāl iṟaiyēnum ēttādu en nā** – my tongue will not praise anyone other than that amazing benefactor *vatataḷasāyi* (one who lies on the banyan leaf). Here the reference is to the other forms of [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) such as param (supreme being in śrīvaikuṇṭam), vyūha (his form in milky ocean) and other vibhava forms(incarnations).

**en nā** – my tongue which is unable to eat because it is not praising emperumān.

**en Na ēttādu** – since [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/poigai-azhwar/) has said in the [64](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2017/08/mudhal-thiruvandhadhi-64/)[<sup>th</sup>](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2017/08/mudhal-thiruvandhadhi-64/) [pāsuram](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2017/08/mudhal-thiruvandhadhi-64/) of this prabandam ‘*tirumālai alladu deyvam enrēttēn*” (I will not worship anyone other than the consort of śrī mahālakśmi) he has already confirmed that he will not praise other deities. Hence there is no need for him to convey the same message here. Instead, he is saying that he will not praise other forms of [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) \[as mentioned above\].

We shall take up the 95<sup>th</sup> pāsuram next.

## 95. Navayil unde

*nā vāyil uṇḍē namō nāraṇā enṟu ovādu uraikkum urai uṇḍē mūvāda mākkadikkaṇ sellum vagai uṇḍē en oruvar tīkkadikkaṇ sellum tiṟam*

**Пословный перевод:**

nā – tongue (which is the implement for praising emperumān); vāyil uṇḍē – is inside the mouth (without the need for searching for it outside); ōvādu – many times (without getting tired); uraikkum – apt to say; namō nāraṇā enṟu urai – tirumantram; uṇḍē – is available; mūvāda – not coming back; mā gadikkaṇ – in paramapadam (śrīvaikuṇṭam) which is the goal to be attained; sellum vagai uṇḍē – arcirādi mārga, the path to go \[to śrīvaikuṇṭam\] is ready; tī gadikkaṇ – in the path to naragam (hell); oruvar – some people; sellum tiṟan – why are they going?

**Перевод:**

**nā vāyil uṇḍē – Is there a need to search outside for the tongue which is to be used for reciting the divine names of emperumān! īśvaran (emperumān) has already created tongue! Just as sumitrā tells lakśmaṇa in śrī rāmāyaṇam ayōdyā kāṇḍam 40-5”sruśtatva vanavāsāya” (you have been created only for dwelling in forests), hasn’t the tongue been created only for praising emperumān?**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*In the previous pāsuram, āḻvār mentioned that his tongue will not praise anyone other than emperumān. In this pāsuram, he shows his amazement at samsāris (dwellers of this materialistic realm) who are using such parts of their body (as their tongues etc) as implements for nurturing samsāram (materialistic life). Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**namō nāraṇā enṟu ōvādu uraikkum urai uṇḍē** – Hasn’t [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) given us the mantram (incantation) which is so simple that it can be recited ten times in a breath, instead of some mantrams such as *sahasrākśarīmālāmantram* (a mantram which is made up of thousands of letters) which cannot be recited even within a day! Just as the tongue has been given by emperumān, hasn’t this mantram been given by the same emperumān! As said in “*kim tatra bahubhirmantrai: kim tatra bahubhirvratai: namō nārāyānēti mantra: sarvārta sādaka:* ” (what is the purpose of other mantrams and other vratams (vows)? The incantation namō nārayaṇāya will give all benefits), hasn’t it been stated that even other incantations of emperumān are purposeless!

**mūvāda mākkadikkaṇ sellum vagai uṇḍē** – hasn’t [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) created mōkśam (śrīvaikuṇṭam) by reaching which one does not have to return again, unlike other worlds such as svargam (heaven) from where one will be toppled after enjoying for a few days!

**ennoruvar tīkkadikkaṇ sellum tiṟam** – when such entities \[tongue, tirumantram, mōkśam etc\] are available without any shortcoming, how strange are the ways in which the worldly people repeatedly attain samsāram (materialistic realm)! When there is an easy, straightforward path available for getting uplifted, how are people going to difficult paths for getting destroyed and attaining meaninglessness! Hasn’t it been stated in śrī viṣṇu purāṇam 6-7-11 “*anātmanyātmabuddir yā ca asvē svamiti yā mati: samsāra taru sambhūti bhījamētat dvitā stitam*”(the thought that something (body) is ātmā (soul) when it is not, and the thought that something is one’s own when it is not, are the seed with two parts which nurture the tree called samsāram)!

We shall move on to the 96<sup>th</sup> pāsuram next.

adiyēn kriṣṇa ramanuja dāsan

## 96. Tirambaden nenjame

*tiṟambāden nenjamē sengaṇmāl kaṇḍāy aṟam pāvam enṟu iraṇḍum āvān puṟandān im maṇḍhān maṟi kadaldān mārudandān vāndānē kaṇḍāy kadaikkat pidi*

**Пословный перевод:**

en nenjamē – Oh my heart!; aṟam pāvam enṟu iraṇḍum āvān – he \[emperumān\] is the controller for both the types of deeds, virtuous and sinful; sengaṇmāl kaṇḍāy – look at the emperumān who has reddish lotus like eyes; im maṇḍhān – this earth; maṟi kadaldān – the ocean which keeps lapping waves; mārudam tān – the breeze; vān – the sky also; puṟandān – (different from all these) jīvan (all living species), mahān (one of the essential entities in creation, known as “great”), ahankāram (ego) etc; tāne kaṇḍāy – look! they are all emperumān himself!; tiṟambādu – without mistake; kadaikkaṇ pidi – know till the end

**Перевод:**

**tiṟambādu kadaikkatpidi could be added in the end \[of the verse\] to get the meaning. ōr, it can be recited together as in tiṟambādu aṟam pāvam enṟu iraṇḍum āvān to get the meaning. Alternatively, tiṟambādu en nenjamē could be considered as it is and meaning can be obtained. Oh heart! You, who are carrying out whatever I tell you, without any mistake! Oh heart, who says unless I see emperumān I will not go back!**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*āḻvār tells his heart “let the cētanars (sentient entities) carry on with whatever they are doing. Oh my heart! Consider first what I am telling you” Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**en nenjamē** – Oh heart, who keeps asking me to tell everything!

**sengaṇmāl kaṇḍāy** – look at emperumān who has lotus like eyes. cāndōjña upaniśat 1-6-7 says “*tasya yatā kapyāsam puṇḍarīkam ēvam akśiṇī*” (emperumān’s eyes are like lotus just blossomed by sun); [nammāḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/nammazhwar/) in his tiruvāimoḻi 6-7-10 says “*anaittulagum udaiya aravindalōcanan* ” (the lotus eyed ([emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/)) has all the worlds). Alternatively, *sengaṇmāl*  could be taken to mean having reddish eyes out of affection for us, his followers, and hence we can take it that [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/poigai-azhwar/) is asking his heart to look at emperumān who has reddish eyes due to overflowing affection for us.

**aṟam pāvam enṟu iraṇḍum āvān** – he \[[emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/)\] himself is both *puṇyam* (virtuous deed) and *pāvam* (bad or sinful deed). His anugraham (kindness or liking) is *puṇyam* and his nigraham (disliking) is *pāvam*. There is nothing else apart from these two which would give the benefits \[of our actions\]. All are under his disposal only. kauśītaki upaniśat 3-9 says “*ēśa Eva sādu karma kārayati*” (emperumān himself makes us perform good deeds). [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/poigai-azhwar/) will not hold on to deeds which do not make sense as means as he avoids both action and activity as emanating from him (īsn’t he one who holds on to emperumān as the means!). Looking superficially, it would appear that *puṇyam* and *pāvam* are different from him. But if one were to look at things deeply, it would be clear that he is the one who controls everything.

How is this?

**puṟandān immaṇḍhān maṟikadaldān mārudandān vāndānē kaṇḍāy** – all are under the disposal of [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/). *maṇ* (earth), *maṟikadal* (agitating ocean, with lapping waves), *mārudam* (breeze), and the entities which are outside these elements such as *mahān* (great entity) etc are all his manifestations, says the [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/poigai-azhwar/). āḻvār tells his heart to hold on to the fact that emperumān is the *cētanan* (sentient entity) with the body. ōr, *kadaikkaṇ* – hold on to this till the end, without any error. [nammāḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/nammazhwar/) has also mercifully said this in his tiruvāimoḻi 6-9-7 “*ulagil tiriyum karuma gadiyāy ulagamāy ulagukkē ōruyirumānāy*” (you are the deeds, the entities which carry out the deeds and the life of these entities)! When emperumān’s independence is absolute without any shortcoming, how can the cētana (sentient entity)’s thinking that he is independent be congruent? The fact is that cētana is never independent; it is just that *he* thinks that he is independent. In mahābhāratam uttara kāṇḍam 42-35 it says “*ya: anyatā santamānam ātmānam anyatā pratipadyatē*” (whoever thinks that emeprumān’s possession of [ātmā](https://granthams.koyil.org/2013/03/06/thathva-thrayam-chith-who-am-i/) is his…). Even when cētana thinks that he is independent, due to the grace of emperumān, the cētana’s nature is not destroyed.

We shall consider the 97<sup>th</sup> pāsuram next.

## 97. Pidi ser kaliru

*pidi sēr kaliṟaḷitta pērāḷā un tan adi sērndu aruḷ peṟṟāḷ anṟē podi sēr anaṟku angai ēṟṟān avirsadaimēl pāynda punaṟ gangai ennum pērp pon*

**Пословный перевод:**

pidi sēr – roaming with female elephant; kaḷiṟu – gajēndra āḻvān \[a male elephant\]; aḷitta – protected; pērāḷā – Oh magnanimous entity!; podi sēr – applying ashes; anaṟku – for fire; am kai ēṟṟān – one who held his hand, that rudra’s; avir sadai mēl – on his shining matted hair; pāynda – fell; punal – having \[copious\] water; gangai ennum pēr – having the name gangā; pon – great gold; un than adi sērndu – attaining your divine feet; aruḷ peṟṟāḷ anṟē – did she not get your grace!

**Перевод:**

**pidi sēr kaḷiṟu aḷitta – āḻvār is referring to gajēndra (male elephant) who was roaming with a female elephant. The implication here is that he does not know anything other than roaming with his female elephant. bruhadārṇyaka upaniśat 4-4-23 says “śāntō dānta uparatastitikśu:” (śāstras, the sacred texts, say that only that person who is able to control his external and internal sensory perceptions and who dissociates himself from worldly pursuits, is fit to reach śrīvaikuṇṭam). Engaging with worldly pursuits instead of with emperumān is considered as sinful deed (however gajēndran was habituated to roaming with his female elephant). If emperumān had protected such an entity, sin (or virtue) should be considered as what is decided by emperumān. While gajēndra was thinking of his female elephant, emperumān was thinking of gajēndra! Did gajēndra not think of emperumān before? Thinking of his female elephant is his status, until now, says āḻvār. Thus, birth \[whether born in a noble heritage or a lowly heritage\], deed \[good or bad\] etc are not the apt features to be considered. emperumān’s thought (mercy) alone gives the benefit, says āḻvār.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*āḻvār had said in the previous pāsuram that emperumān is both the good and bad deeds (puṇyam and pāpam) and that he is very pure. When asked, aren’t gangā etc pure, he says, in this pāsuram, that they attained their pure state because of their connection with him. This pāsuram is like an explanation to the previous pāsuram. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**aḷitta pērāḷā** – Oh the great one, who protected that gajēndra! It is implied here that if [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) protects one who is not apt, anything becomes acceptable when carried out by emperumān.

**pēṟāḷā** – is your grace only this much! Hasn’t [nammāḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/nammazhwar/) too mercifully stated in his [tiruvāimoḻi 3-3-5](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2016/08/thiruvaimozhi-3-3-5-sodhiyagi/) “*sōdiyāgi eḷḷa ulagum toḻum ādimūrtti enṟāl aḷavāgumō*” (Is it anything great if I say that you, having a radiant divine form and being vorśipped by all the people in the world, are the supreme being, and the causative factor for all the worlds?)

**un than adi sērndu aruḷ peṟṟāḷ anṟē** – did not gangā get all the greatness only after attaining your divine feet! This implies that darmam (righteousness) is  under the control of [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/).  īsvara samhitai says “*gruhītvā darmapānīyam*” (taking gangā who is the epitome of righteousness). gangā is referred to as righteousness because of her connection with emperumān. He graces others to become pure.

**podi sēr** – Siva, who has applied ashes. Alternatively, Siva who is reclining on ahses.

**anaṟku angai ēṟṟān** – Siva, who is holding fire in his beautiful hands. Other than carrying out such atonements, it is not possible to remove the sins of Siva. Even his thought that he is the controller of all is an exotic one. emperumān protected such Siva only by keeping his original nature in mind.

**avir sadai mēl pāyndu** – gangā who has such water which flowed through the shining matted hair of Siva.

**undan adi sērndu aruḷ peṟṟāḷ anṟē** – despite carrying out suitable penances such as holding fire and applying ashes all over his body, Siva did not become pure. He became purified only after holding in his head gangā, who has the connection with [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) through his divine feet! Has it not been mercifully stated in periyāḻvār tirumoḻi 4-7-2 “*nalam tigaḻ sadiyān mudik konṟai malarum nāraṇan pādat tuḻāyum*” (the head of Siva has become purified since he is carrying the water from emperumān’s divine feet. The reddish konṟai flower from the head of such Siva and the greenish tuḷasi from the divine head of nāraṇan who is the lord of that Siva…). Hasn’t [āḷavandār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/alavandhar/) too mercifully stated in [stōtra ratnam 13](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2016/12/sthothra-rathnam-13/) “*pādōdagēna sa: Siva:*” (due to the water from \[emperumān’s\] divine feet, he became Siva (purified))! Oh emperumān! Whatever is not connected to your divine feet is not pure. There is no sin which can prevent your mercy. [kūrattāḻvān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/kuraththazhwan/) too has mercifully divined in atimānuśya stavam 53 “*yat tvat priyam tadiha puṇyam apuṇyam anyat*” (whatever is dear to you is puṇyam, the rest is pāpam).

We shall take up the 98<sup>th</sup> pāsuram next.

## 98. Pon tigalum meni

*pon tigaḻum mēnip purisadai am puṇṇiyanum ninṟu ulgam tāya nedumālum enṟum iruvar angattāl tirivarēlum oruvan oruvan angattenṟum uḷan*

**Пословный перевод:**

pon tigaḻum mēni – having a form which shines like gold; puri sadai – platted, matted hair; am puṇṇiyanum – rudra \[Siva\] who has beautiful puṇyam (virtuous deeds); ninṟu ulagam tāya nedumāḷum – sarvēśvaran (lord of all) who stood and measured all the worlds; enṟum – always; iruvar angattāl tirivarēlum – even if they roam around with two \[different\] forms; oruvan – one among them, Siva; oruvan angattu – holding on to the divine body of nārāyaṇa; enṟum – at all times; uḷan – will sustain

**Перевод:**

**pon tigaḻum mēnip puri sadai – having a physical form shining like gold and having platted, matted hair.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*Why point out a fault in Siva that he took gangā which has connection with the divine feet of emperumān on his shining matted hair? He is being called īsvaran (one who controls) and is responsible for the function of destruction. āḻvār says that he got the quality of being īsvaran because he is the body (śarīram) of emperumān and not of his own nature. Let us look at the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**am puṇṇiyanum** – Siva did penance beautifully and observed means by himself. [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) has a comfortable form resembling dark clouds \[being magnanimous, ready to pour benefits on everyone\]. The golden form of Siva which is like fire, indicates that his form is the exact opposite of emperumān’s. That Siva is different from emperumān is brought out by his matted hair, which is the exact opposite of the long crown that emperumān has. From these two verses, it is clearly mentioned that emperumān is superior to Siva in every way.

**am puṇṇiyanum** – mahābhāratam śānti parvam 20-12 says “*mahādēvasarvayajñē mahātmā hūtvātmānam dēvadēvō babhūva*” (rudra, the mahātmā (great soul) in the sarvayajña (a kind of ritual) offered himself as the havis (a material which is given as an offering during the ritual) and became a dēvadēvan (dēva among the dēvas)). Due to the grace of [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/), rudra (Siva) who had carried out sinful deeds, reached an exalted state and hence [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/poigai-azhwar/) calls him as puṇṇiyan (one who has done virtuous deed).

**ninṟu ulagam tāya nedumālum** – emperumān had carried out many exalted deeds, as per his desires. He is also the sarvēśvaran (Lord of all) who kept his divine feet on the heads of Siva and others, without any distinction.

**enṟum iruval angattāḷ tirivarēlum** – even if they exist with two different forms always

**oruvan oruvan angattu enṟum uḷan** – one (Siva) is the body of another (nārāyaṇa) and exists. ōr, one (Siva) would reside in a part of the divine form of the other (nārāyaṇa) and have his existence. Hasn’t [nammāḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/nammazhwar/) mercifully said in [tiruvāimoḻi 1-3-9](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2015/07/thiruvaimozhi-1-3-9-valaththanan-thiripuram/) “*valattanan tirupuram erittavan*” (the one on the right side of [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/)’s divine form is Siva who burnt the three towns)! Thus, hasn’t nammāḻvār mercifully said in [tiruvāimoḻi 4-10-3](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2017/04/thiruvaimozhi-4-10-3-parandha-dheyvamum/) “*paran tiṟam anṟip pallulagīr deyvam maṟṟillai*” (there is no other deity without being under the control of emperumān)! \[the inference is that this 98<sup>th</sup> pāsuram of [poygai āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/poigai-azhwar/) is the root for [nammāḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/nammazhwar/)’s pāsuram in tiruvāimoḻi 4-10-3\].

We shall consider the 99<sup>th</sup> pāsuram next.

## 99. Ulan kanday

*uḷan kaṇḍāy nannenjē uttaman enṟum uḷan kaṇḍāy uḷḷuvār uḷḷattu uḷan kaṇḍāy veḷḷattin uḷḷānum vēngadattu mēyānum uḷḷattin uḷḷān enṟu ōr*

**Пословный перевод:**

nal nenjē – Oh my heart who is well disposed! \[towards emperumān\]; uttaman – puruśōttaman (the best among all entities); enṟum – at all times; uḷan kaṇḍāy – exists (only to protect us); uḷḷuvār uḷḷattu – those who think of him; uḷan kaṇḍāy – resides permanently; veḷḷattin uḷḷānum – one who is reclining in tiruppāṟkadal (milky ocean); vēngadattu mēyānum – one who is standing in tiruvēngadam (tirumalai); uḷḷattin uḷḷān enṟu – is residing inside (my) heart; ōr – know

**Перевод:**

**uḷan kaṇḍāy – Oh heart! You have seen that he is our refuge on the day that we surrendered to him. In mahābhāratam uttara kāṇḍam 83, draupadi says “sāham kēśagraham prāptā tvayi jīvatyapi prabhō” (Oh emperumān! Even when you were existing, I was dragged by my hair by enemies).  My svāmy, when you are there, my enemies have the courage to touch my hair! Can one connect your existence and the insult that happened to me? Were you like me a woman or were you not a puruśōttama (best among all entities) for me to suffer like this! āḻvār says that he is not saying a non-existing person as uḷan (exists). He is there to protect us when we destroy ourselves. His existence is to save us and our existence is to destroy ourselves. Our carrying out prapatti (surrender) is not due to our fear of others but due to fear of ourselves. It is similar to someone telling an intelligent person “I would myself tie a rope, unknown to me, around my neck in order to kill myself. At that time, you should cut the rope and save me”. Our carrying out surrendering is like this only.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*āḻvār says that emperumān, who protects all, stays in places such as tiruppāṟkadal (milky ocean) only in order to take shelter inside the hearts of those who do not avoid him. āḻvār tells his heart to make a note of this. āḻvār’s divine mind was scared that great people in the world are bewildered into believing that cētanas (sentient entities) who are like themselves are īsvaras (emperumān). āḻvār says that even if one were to tie a rope around one’s neck in order to kill himself, there is an entity who is there to cut the rope and to protect us. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**nan nenjē** – Oh heart! you are present to tell me appropriately that emperumān is there. You are capable of explaining the meanings of these words to me!

**uttaman** – magnanimous person. In other words, he does favours to others in order to benefit himself. Hence he exists in order to protect us and does that to benefit himself. taittiriya upaniśat ānandavalli 6 says “*asannēva sa bhavati asat brahmēti vēda cēt*” (a person does not exist if he does not have knowledge about *brahmam* (supreme being)) along with “*asti brahmēti cēt vēda santamēnam tatō vidu:*” (if he has knowledge about *brahmam* others know that he exists). emperumān exists without any distinction between these two types of entities (one who does not know him and one who knows him).

**uḷḷuvār uḷḷattu uḷan kaṇḍāy** – if one day cētana (sentient entity) believes that emperumān exists, then emperumān will exist forever for him. He will reside inside the heart of the one that he wants to pervade, not knowing how to leave that person’s heart and be there forever.

**veḷlattil uḷḷānum vēngadattu mēyānum uḷḷattil uḷḷān enṟu ōr** – Think that the one who is reclining in milky ocean and who is standing in tiruvēngadam (tirumalai) is in our heart. He resides in those places (milky ocean and tiruvēngadam) only to enter our heart. Think that the place that he desires is only our heart. Haven’t [tirumaḻisai āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thirumazhisai-azhwar/) in nānmugan tiruvandādi 36 “*aṇaippār* *karuttanāvān*” (he is there only to enter the hearts of his followers) and [periyāḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/periyazhwar/) in periyāḻvār tirumoḻi 4-5-10 “*idavagaigal igaḻndittu enpāl idavagai koṇḍanaiyē*”(leaving aside your dwelling places \[such as śrīvaikuṇṭam, tiruppāṟkadal etc\], you have decided to dwell in my heart! \[what a motherly affection is this!\]) divinely mentioned the same!

We shall consider the 100 pāsuram next.

## 100. Oradiyum sadudaitta

*ōradiym sādudaitta oṇ malarc cēvadiyum īradiyum kāṇalām en nenjē ōradiyil tāyavanaik kēsavanait taṇḍuḻāy mālai sēr māyavanaiyē manattu vai*

**Пословный перевод:**

en nenjē – Oh my heart!; ōradiyil tāyavanai – one who measured all the worlds with one divine foot; kēsavanai – destroying a demon named kēśi; taṇ tuḻāy mālai sēr – decorated with cool, tuḷasi garland; māyavanaiyē – only that emperumān who has amazing activities; manattu vai – keep in your heart; ōr adiyum – the divine foot which measured the worlds; sādu udaitta – kicked such that the wheel broke into smithereens; oṇ malar sēvadiyum – divine foot which is like a beautiful reddish flower; īradiyum – both the divine feet; kāṇalām – could see

**Перевод:**

**ōradiyum – divine foot which engaged everyone towards it and measured**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*To the question as to what should we do to attain such an entity \[emperumān\], āḻvār says “be firm that one can attain him only through his grace” Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**sādu udaitta oṇ malar sēvadiyum** – the sweet foot which destroyed the demon who had entered a wheel. Was it not with the divine foot, which was soft like a flower that had just blossomed, that he destroyed the wheel!

**īradiyum kāṇalām en nenjē** – we can see both the divine feet. We can see those two divine feet which are apt to be attained.

**en nenjē** – Oh heart who is desirous of seeing. When asked as to what is the means for this, [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/poigai-azhwar/) says that his divine feet are the means.

**ōr adiyil tāyavanai** – as mentioned earlier, the divine feet which are so simple that all can attain

**kēsavanai** – the term kēśava refers to destroying of enemies \[demon called kēśi who came in the form of a horse\]. He is the one who offers his divine feet as well as destroys enemies.

**than tuḻāy mālai sēr māyavanaiyē** – Have firm belief in your heart that he is the means, who is decorated with tuḷasi garland and who is having unbounded sweetness.

**taṇ tuḻāy mālai sēr māyavanaiyē** – Even if he is not simple to approach and even if he does not kill enemies, is it possible to ignore when seeing his sweetness?

**māyavanaiyē** – Don’t think that we need to take any effort to attain him. By using the long syllable in the word *māyavanaiyē* it is implied that he is capable of making us to think only of him and of no one else. Once this firm belief is undertaken, we can see the two divine feet which are to be attained. There is no hurdle to seeing those two divine feet. If asked as to what is to be done after attaining them, [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/poigai-azhwar/) says that we can continue to carry out kainkaryam permanently.
